### From 1.08 to 1.09

- **A commit is bounded at push, and a history cuts its fields** (6.1; the "Still not covered" of 1.04 to 1.08, carried, and the operator's popup answers of session 76, "Both: hook + read cut", "1 MiB a commit", "Subject 1K, body 16K; LastChange 1M" and "Refusal, '…'+cut, 1000/ticket"). A commit's message packs small: one of 60 MiB is about 60 KiB in a pack, which the hook passed, and each history read it whole, its bytes, a copy as text and the page's JSON, up to about 0.5 GB for one page; a ticket's Git part kept every one of its up to 500 pushes, which could go past the server's ceiling of 4g. Now the git endpoint's hook refuses a push whose commits or tip are more than 1 MiB (`MaxCommitBytes`, `internal/gitendpoint/treebound.go`; the hook moved to `prereceive.go`), and a size it cannot read refuses it; every history (`Log`, `LogAll`, `LogRange`, `FirstParentLog`, `Show`) streams git's output through one reader of `internal/gitstore/readbound.go`, `stream`, and keeps each field to its cut (`history.go`), a cut one ending in "…" with the commit's `cut`; `LastChange` cuts its message at 1 MiB; and a ticket's Git part keeps at most 1,000 commits, the rest counted in `omitted`. The protected files of the change: `internal/gitendpoint/prereceive.go`, `treebound.go`, `gitendpoint.go` and `commitbound_test.go`, and `internal/gitstore/readbound.go` with its test.
- After the change's review and security review (the operator's popup answers "Protect history.go", "IDs once full + 512 MiB raw", "Newest, count on the page" and "All four"): `internal/gitstore/history.go` is protected (16.8, `internal/release/kind.go`, also protected); a history stops at 512 MiB of git's output, where it had read on to its two minutes; a ticket's Git part keeps its newest commits, reads a push for its ids once full, and its page says how many it left out (`web/src/components/runs.tsx`, `OmittedCommits`); the hook takes a push only on its check's own "ok", so a check that fails refuses, and refuses a tip that is not a commit; its text no longer says that nothing of the hook reads a commit before its size is checked; and the cut of a commit's parents has its test. After their second round ("IDs after a failure + UTF-8 only" and "All lows, no cache"): a push whose history is past the bound of a read is read for its ids, as is every older push after it, where it had been left out unsaid and every push could cost a read to the bound; the hook refuses a commit that is not in UTF-8, by which git printed a message up to three times as long; `CommitIDs` reads at most 500, as a push's history does; and the texts say the view's 2 GB and no longer that nothing reads a commit before its size is checked.

### From 1.07 to 1.08

- **The git endpoint works on a few pushes and fetches at once, and the server has a ceiling of memory** (6.1, 13.10, 14.1; the security review of 1.07, round one, finding 2, carried, and the operator's popup answers of session 75, "2 per repo, 4 per server", "Wait 5 min, then 503", "mem_limit 8g, pushes killed first", "Own counts: 4/repo, 8/server", "4g ceiling, reserve 8 GB" and "Spool every push first"). Each push was bounded in time and in what it reads, but nothing counted them: a run's token could send any number at once, each with its own git, hook and up to 512 MiB of body, and the server, which runs them all, had no ceiling of memory. Now every request of the git endpoint takes a slot of its repository and one of the server before its body is read: a push or its advertisement of refs two and four, a fetch or its advertisement four and eight, counted apart; a request with no free slot waits five minutes, then is refused with HTTP 503 and a fixed text (`internal/gitendpoint/slots.go`, `work.go`). Its body arrives within two minutes of its slots; a push's is read whole into a file first (`body.go`), since git http-backend spins on a core without end on a push's body that ends before its length, which a run could start at will before 1.08. Every git the endpoint starts sets an OOM score of 500 first, which a push's hook and the server's program inherit (`oomscore.go`), and a request whose score cannot be set is not taken; `crew-server` has `mem_limit: 4g` in the Compose file, and `machine_reserve.memory_gb` is 8 by default, the core services' ceilings and 1 GB more, so memory is not overcommitted (dave gives out 54 GB in place of 58, and the tests' machines are 4 GB larger, so their room stays). The ceiling holds once an install has started the server with it.
- After the change's review and security review (the operator's popup answers "Score 500 for fetches too", "10 min per request", "2× slots per repo, then 503", "Push's after-work", "Count ref advertisements", "memswap_limit: 4g" and "One repo per project, pinned"): fetches and advertisements of refs set the score too; an answer is written within ten minutes, so a fetch whose client stops reading holds no slot; at most twice a repository's slots wait beside them, else a request is refused at once; an advertisement takes a slot, so git shows the text; a push's slots go back before its record is written, and its refs are read whatever becomes of the client's connection, a failed read recording it for no ref; `crew-server` has `memswap_limit: 4g`; and a test pins the two writers of `git_repos`, so a project keeps its one repository, by which the slots keep projects apart. After their second round ("Refuse GET with body + deadline on all", "Per-read timeouts + give main back", "UNIQUE(project), schema step 81", "'after' row asserts the log" and "Fetch test write time 3 s"): an advertisement that says it has a body is refused before it takes a slot, which it held while git waited for that body, and every request has the read deadline; each read of a push's refs has a minute of its own, where one minute had covered git and its hook too, so a push slower than a minute was heard of for no ref; when a read fails, a request that may push `main` has it heard of with no value, which the run's record skips and the terminal answers by giving Crew's `main` back (`internal/projterm/projterm.go`, `internal/runs/roles.go`); and schema step 81 holds one repository a project, in place of the test that read the writers' text. Protected among the changed files (`release.Protected`): `internal/gitendpoint/` (`gitendpoint.go`, `slots.go`, `work.go`, `body.go`, `oomscore.go` and their tests), `deploy/` (`compose.yaml`, `memlimit_test.go`), `internal/settings/` (`crew.yaml`, `reserve_test.go`), `internal/projterm/` (`projterm.go`, `unreadmain_test.go`), `internal/runs/roles.go` and `internal/db/` (step 81 and `onerepo_test.go`); not protected: `internal/runs/unreadpush_test.go` and the tests of `internal/capacity/`, `internal/runs/` and `internal/httpapi/` whose machines grew by 4 GB.

A run keeps the step of `crew-run`'s own that the server took from its last turn's `exit.json` in `runs.end_step` (schema step 80, a column, no row rewritten), and an eval reads a workspace end there, never by the detail's beginning. A sign-in end measured nothing only when Crew's own login check after the run's end found the CLI signed out, and the eval run waits until a check tells; a limit and a model end are the agent's, since `agy`'s check reads no limits and its adapter gives no model end. Before a turn after the first starts, the harness removes the `exit.json`, the spool and the standard error an agent wrote into its folder ahead. Named as still not covered: the step, a refusal and the setup's reason are `exit.json`'s word, and an agent can take its case out by its work, through a check that errors on its branch's setup or past its time, a microVM it powers off, or a reviewer with no verdict. dave held five eval runs, none going on, counted read-only: three measured, two failed for the reviewer's limit and the reviewer's refusal. Protected among the changed files: `internal/harness/` (`kata.go`, `turnfolder.go`, `turnfolder_test.go`) and `internal/db/` (the schema step and `steps_design_test.go`, whose bullets may now name a version 1.x). The eval's reading itself, `internal/pipeline/evalend.go`, `evals.go` and `internal/measure/`, is not protected; that is carried to the next session by the operator's popup answer.

### From 0.99 to 1.00

- **crew-run's own ends, the note that the work in progress was not kept, and the trust in `exit.json`** (13.9, 12.4; the operator's popup answers of session 67, "Fixed sentence, error to log" and "Server writes Crew's sentences"). An end of `crew-run`'s own names its step in `exit.json`, and the server writes the step's fixed sentence from the list in `runspec`, whatever the detail there says; `crew-run`'s error goes apart, in `error`, which the server logs with the run and stores nowhere else. The note that the work in progress was not kept is a mark, and the server adds its fixed sentence itself and logs git's error. Each error could name the sandbox's door, and git's could relay the endpoint's refusal, whose text can name files an agent committed; `exit.json` is in the agent's own home. Every other end detail is the CLI's, the sandbox's word, read as data. The server logs `crew-run`'s error only for a step on the list and for the note, as the home's word. Named as not covered, by the operator's popup answers of session 67 after the security review: the eval's reading of an end for Crew's own reason by the home's reason, step or detail; a later turn's `exit.json` written ahead (12.4); and 16.8 says why `internal/runs/turnend.go` and `events.go` are not protected. dave held four runs that ended so with the workspace's error and none with the note, counted read-only; no schema step rewrites them.

Every other writer of an error after "did not start" (the assistant's waiting reason, a hosted deploy's sandbox and its bridge's probe, the prompt writer, the Setup terminal and its microVM, the updater's switch, the login check's sandbox, the project terminal's microVM, a gate's check server, the replacement of the updater, and `crew admin`'s microVM) reaches only the operator's pages, the operator's commands and `crew-ctl status`, and 13.9 names them. From its security review, and the operator's popup answer "Keep leave it, correct 13.9": a run's end detail is read from `exit.json` in the agent's own home, so it is the sandbox's word whatever `crew-run` writes, and a fixed sentence written by `crew-run` would guarantee nothing; the note that the work in progress was not kept adds git's output to every end of the CLI's own turn, this one included, which can name the sandbox's door and relay the git endpoint's refusal, whose text can name files an agent committed, and 13.9 names it beside `crew-run`'s other ends as not decided. In the second round: an error of git in the workspace can name the door too. Each stored copy was followed to its readers, every role's MCP tools included. No code changed, and dave held none of these texts, counted read-only.

### From 0.97 to 0.98

- **The code that asks GitHub for a sandbox's token is protected** (16.8; the security review of the release of 0.3.11, finding 1, and the operator's popup answer of session 65, "Fix before 0.3.11"). `internal/githubapp/` asks GitHub for the token a sandbox gets, and is the only code that limits each token it asks for to reading, and to the repositories it is given (safety rule 7): the broker hands on what it is given. A run's repositories are chosen in `internal/runs/roles.go` and `internal/harness/`, which were protected. Setup's check that the App may only read contents is a second limit, made once, when the App is saved. `internal/githubapp/` was not on the list of protected parts, so with `updates.auto_install` set to `unprotected` a release that changed only it, to ask for a token that can write, would have installed by itself. It is now on the list, under the code that chooses a sandbox's credentials, which the requirements' protected part already names. The running version decides from its own list, so the release that carries this change is classified by the list before it; that release is protected anyway, since it changes `internal/release/`. A test fails when the path is left off the list.
- **More of the code that limits a sandbox's credentials or holds the operator's logins is protected** (16.8, and the requirements' protected part; the security review of the fix above, its findings 1 and 2, and the operator's popup answers of session 65, "Add all before 0.3.11" and the requirements edit approved). The code that gives the project terminal's token its repositories (`internal/projterm/`), and Setup's check of the App, were under the code that chooses a sandbox's credentials only in the requirements' words; they are now on the list. The code that holds the operator's logins or hands them on was named nowhere: the server's own use of GitHub with the SSH key and the `gh` login (`internal/github/`), Setup, which makes and stores them (`internal/setup/`), and the test of a login that comes back from a run, with the adapter of `agy`, which says whose a login is, whole (`internal/logins/`, `internal/cli/`; safety rules 6, 8 and 9). It is now a protected part of its own, in 16.8 and in the requirements. The adapter of `agy` is protected whole, as `internal/orchestrator/` and `internal/config/` are, since its account check is in `google.go` and the list of adapters in `cli.go` (the review of that change). A test fails when any of these paths is left off the list.
- **The mail account, the paths a login goes through, and the secrets of hosted projects are protected** (16.8, and the requirements' protected part; the security review of the change above, outside its round, and the operator's popup answers of session 65: "Add them before 0.3.11", "Whole package", "mail/ and notify/ whole", "Leave it, say why", and the requirements edit approved). The code that holds the operator's logins now names the mail account that sends notifications (`internal/notify/` reads it from the vault, `internal/mail/` sends with it) and the rule for the paths in a sandbox's home that the server writes or reads, a login's among them (`internal/homepath/`, which the vault, `crew-run`, the harness, Setup, the read of a login that comes back and the pipeline's tools use). The secrets of hosted projects, which `internal/hosting/` stores and hands to a hosted project's container, are a protected part of their own, the package whole. A project's remote, which gives the project terminal's token its repositories and which the server pushes to, is written only when the project is made, from the operator's form or a repository Crew makes for it (`internal/board/projects.go`), or for Crew's project from `updates.source_repo` (`internal/board/crewproject.go`), and is never changed after; its writer in `projects.go` is not protected, and 16.8 says why (the review of this change reworded the reason; no test pins that the remote has no other writer). `cmd/crew-run/`, which applies the same path rule inside a sandbox, is not on the list either: it is an input of the runtime image, and a release whose runtime image differs is held for Install when its live steps pass (16.2). A test fails when any of these paths is left off the list.

### From 0.91 to 0.92

- **No error's text in the waiting reason when the board cannot try a ticket** (13.9; `docs/reviews/2026-10-28-security-release-0.3.7.md`, finding 2; the operator's popup answer of session 57). When the board could not try a ticket, its waiting reason held the error's text, up to 500 characters, for any check: git's errors name the server's repository paths, and an agent reads the reason through `get_ticket`. The reason is now fixed, "the board could not try it; the error is in Crew's server log", and the error is logged with the ticket's key. The selftest, which shows a ticket's waiting reason, shows that sentence. A check run that ended in error still puts its detail, Crew's own error text, into the waiting reason and the stuck reason: 13.9 says so, and the operator's decision on it is the next session's (the security review of session 57, finding 1).
- **One ticket's error holds no other in the merge queue** (7.10; `docs/reviews/2026-10-28-security-release-0.3.7.md`, finding 4; the operator's popup answers of session 57). An error of one ticket in the merge queue, such as a branch that shares no history with `main`, ended the project's pass at that ticket, so it held every ticket behind it, on every pass. That ticket now waits with the fixed reason "Crew could not try its merge; the error is in Crew's server log", and the pass goes on; the errors are logged together. Its test found a second way the same error held the others: the pipeline's steps of a ticket (after a build, a join, a revert, a stage of the project's own, and a merge that passed) reconcile the board of the whole project, and returned its error, which was another ticket's, as their own, so the step of the ticket behind it stopped. They now log it and go on; the board has already written that ticket's reason.

### From 0.87 to 0.88

- **No unprotected file of the server changes its environment** (16.8; `docs/reviews/2026-10-27-review-cmd-crew-protected-7888ce6-b15241e.md`, finding 1; the operator's popup answer of session 53). Protecting `cmd/crew/` whole decides which releases wait for Install, but a package the server links that is not protected runs in the same process, and its `init` could set `CREW_DOOR_NAME` or check mode before the server's command reads them. A test in `internal/release` walks the imports of `cmd/crew` within Crew's module, as the server's image builds it, and fails on a call or a value of `os`, `syscall` or `golang.org/x/sys/unix`'s `Setenv`, `Unsetenv` or `Clearenv`, of the last two's `Exec`, which starts the server again with an environment the caller chooses, on a dot import of one of them, and on a `go:linkname` directive, in a file that is not protected; it reads the files a package's build constraints leave out as well, so a file of another architecture is checked. Packages of other modules are pinned by `go.mod` and `go.sum`, which are protected. It tests names, not every way a linked package can reach the process: protection by path stays no boundary inside it. Nothing of the server changed.

### From 0.84 to 0.85

- **The rest of the copied code no binary reached is gone** (15.1, 17.2; `docs/reviews/2026-10-27-review-dead-project-code-6d8a71f-5034f55.md`, finding 1, and `docs/reviews/2026-10-27-security-dead-project-code-6d8a71f-5034f55.md`, finding 3; the operator's kickoff of session 50). The tests of the bridge's name keep their golden vector, on the function the sandboxes use.

Section 5's table gives Crew as where both come from, and 15.1's layout no longer lists the deleted `internal/terminal`.
- **The forwarding override is a key of the install file** (13.6, 16.11; `docs/reviews/2026-10-27-review-0.3.5-fixes-code-docs.md`, finding 1; the operator's popup answer of session 50). `CREW_ALLOW_IP_FORWARD` lived only in the environment of whoever ran Compose, so the next install, edit or update started the server without it. It is a key of `crew.env` now, which `crew-ctl edit` takes as `1` or empty and every write of the file keeps, and which `crew-ctl` and the updater both give Compose; `doctor` warns while it is set.
- **A pipe into a quiet grep** (BUG-021). `crew-ctl`, the firewall's test, the host setup, the proof, and the tests' scripts run with `pipefail`, and piped commands into `grep -q`, which stops reading at its match: a command that wrote on after it got SIGPIPE, and the pipe failed, so a match read as none, and a negated check as passed. Each such grep reads its input to the end, and a test fails on a new one in any shell script under `deploy/` and `tools/`, the libraries they source included; `image/entrypoint.sh`, whose one such pipe reads a line that `head -1` writes at once, and the spike's scripts are not read by it.
- **The ingress's door knows a sandbox by where it comes from** (13.6, 20.3; `docs/reviews/2026-10-27-security-release-0.3.5.md`, finding 1; the operator's popup answer of session 50). With forwarding off a sandbox could still reach the server's own address on another network, by a route through its own bridge, and the door told sandboxes apart by the address a request arrived at alone, so such a request reached the routes behind it that need no ingress secret: `/healthz`, and the outside MCP's token check. The door now also refuses a request from any address on a sandbox's or a hosted project's bridge, since a guest can send from any of them, not only the one Docker gave it, and knows a bridge's door and subnet before the server joins it; the server's wiring of the door and its start are protected (16.8; review and security review of a910d63..5ac8f90 and of its fixes). The windows a restart of the server, a removal and a hosted bridge after a restart leave are said in 13.6, and left (the operator's popup answer of session 50).

### From 0.83 to 0.84

- **The copied project code no binary reached is gone** (17.2, 19.7; `docs/reviews/2026-10-26-security-bd427d1.md`, finding 2; the operator's popup answer of session 47). The browser terminal copied from styrman-crew, `internal/terminal`, and the store of tmux records it imported, `internal/conversation`, were imported by no command of Crew; Setup's terminal and the project terminal are Crew's own. With them went the half of `internal/orchestrator` that kept one long-lived microVM per project, which they alone called: its start and stop, the idle reaper, the setup microVM, the port list and the preview's attach, the broker's lookup by address, and their tests. What starts, keeps apart, runs in and removes a sandbox or a hosted project is as it was, and the test of a removal that waits out an exiting microVM now drives a sandbox's removal. The two commands that counted a container with no sandbox label as this server's, `ExecUnlabelled` and `StartInteractiveUnlabelled`, went with the code that called them, and with them the lax check of whose a container is: every command and every action by a sandbox's name reads it the one way, `sandboxTarget` (19.7; `docs/reviews/2026-10-26-security-bd427d1.md`, finding 2).
- **R122 says the terminal bridge was written again** (R122, the requirements' summary and scope, 1, 3, 5; `docs/reviews/2026-10-27-review-dead-project-code-6d8a71f-5034f55.md`, finding 2; the operator's popup answer of session 48). The lists of the eight parts copied from styrman-crew keep the terminal bridge and say it was written again for Setup and the project terminal, and that the copy, which nothing used, is gone since 0.84. The M1 row and the list of reference repositories stay as the record of what was copied.

### From 0.82 to 0.83

- **The server forwards nothing** (13.6, 16.8; `docs/reviews/2026-10-26-security-release-0.3.4-extension2.md`, findings 1 and 2; the operator's popup answer of session 47; BUG-020). 13.6 said the server does not forward between bridges, and nothing made it so: `crew-server` and `crew-caddy` ran with Docker's default, the machine's `net.ipv4.ip_forward=1`, while each joins many bridges, and the check written for it was called by nothing. Compose starts both with forwarding off, and so do a gate's check server and the tiers' servers; a server in its container starts no sandbox while its namespace forwards or cannot be read, unless `CREW_ALLOW_IP_FORWARD=1`. The whole of `internal/config/` is a protected part, where `config.go` alone was, so the check and the runtimes' list are protected wherever they are in it. An update starts the containers from the Compose file in place, so the release's deploy files go in before its update (16.11), and `doctor` fails while the server or the ingress runs without forwarding off (the operator's popup answer of session 47).
- **The override reaches the server** (13.6; `docs/reviews/2026-10-27-review-ipforward-bug020.md`, the second finding; 16047ac). Compose passes `CREW_ALLOW_IP_FORWARD` to `crew-server`, so the override 13.6 names is set in the server's environment, which Compose passes, where nothing passed it before.

### From 0.81 to 0.82

- **A command in a sandbox leaves what has no sandbox label** (19.7; `docs/reviews/2026-10-26-security-release-0.3.3.md`, the extension over 325ff81, finding 1; the operator's popup answer of session 46). A command in a sandbox, by its name or its ID, counts a container with no sandbox label as not this server's, as every other action on a sandbox has since 0.81: the harness's turns, the Setup terminal, the project terminal, `crew admin sandbox` and install's proof run nothing in one, and the harness's read of whether its run's container runs is refused for one. Only a command in a project's container, a setup VM or the server's own container, which have no sandbox label, keeps the older rule, and says so by its name: the idle reaper's probe of a project's container and the root removal in the server's own container too (review of bd427d1, finding 1; BUG-019).
- **What keeps a sandbox apart is protected wherever it lives** (16.8; the security review of the release of 0.3.4, finding 1, its extension, and the review of its fixes; the operator's popup answers of session 46). `internal/orchestrator/sandbox.go` was the one protected file of the package, and was split by the refactoring protocol; what keeps a sandbox apart had already left it, unprotected: whose a sandbox is, its bridge and mode, the commands in it, a failed start's clean-up, a hosted microVM with its bridge and the proof of its firewall, and the helpers they call. The whole of `internal/orchestrator/` is protected now, with `internal/config/config.go`, which holds the microVM runtimes an install may name, so a release that changes any of it waits for Install. A test finds the file each guard function is in, by its receiver and name, and fails when a release changing that file would not be protected.
- **`chrome-devtools-mcp` by its integrity** (16.12 "System packages and tools in the images"; security review of the release of 0.3.4, finding 2; the operator's popup answer of session 46). The runtime image's build downloads the package's tarball, checks it against `CHROME_DEVTOOLS_MCP_INTEGRITY` of `versions.lock`, which `tools/check-versions.py` holds in step with `image/Dockerfile`, and installs that file, where it installed the version from whatever registry answered. Its dependencies would still resolve by semver: 1.12.0 has none, and npm leaves out its two optional peers (security review of the release of 0.3.4, extension, finding 3).

### From 0.80 to 0.81

- **An unpause by the ID its pause read** (19.7; `docs/reviews/2026-10-26-security-b39f701.md`, finding 2; the operator's popup answer of session 45). The pause before a copy of a hosted project's data gives the ID of the container its check read, and the unpause after the copy acts on that ID with no check of its own, where it read the name again and went on by name when that check errored (since 0.77). A live app is still never left paused, and a container another server makes under the name meanwhile stays as it is. The pause gives the copy only what undoes it, so nothing can unpause by a name (security review of ff41a0f).
- **A sandbox's bridge by the ID its check read** (19.7; `docs/reviews/2026-10-26-security-b39f701.md`, finding 1; the operator's popup answer of session 45). A sandbox's bridge has its own labels read before it is joined, left, removed, or given a microVM or a probe, also when its container is this server's, and each of these acts on the bridge's ID from that read, where it went by name after the container's check; a bridge just made is joined and given its sandbox by the ID Docker gave it. An adoption counts the sandbox's address only when its endpoint is on that bridge. A hosted microVM whose create failed removes nothing, where it removed by name a container that might be another server's. A sandbox's start, a hosted bridge's and a probe that fail remove what their creates made, by the IDs Docker gave, and under the name only what this server's labels name, where they removed by name what had no label too (security review of the release of 0.3.3, finding 1). A removal of a sandbox, every other action that takes only its name, and every use of its bridge count a container or bridge with no sandbox label as not this server's, so the removal a failed deploy or start asks for leaves it too, and no door of the broker is given to one; a command, and the harness's read of whether a container runs, keep the older rule, for a project's container and a setup VM. A spec's labels no longer overwrite a sandbox's own.

### From 0.79 to 0.80

- **An action by a sandbox's name acts on the container the check read** (19.7; the carried low of `docs/reviews/2026-10-26-security-54f60eb-round2.md`, finding 2; the operator's popup answer of session 44). A removal, stop, start, restart, pause, unpause and adoption of a sandbox, and the reads of its log and of whether it runs, take the container's ID from the check of whose it is and act on that ID, as a command in it has since 0.79, so a container another server makes under the name after the check is not touched. An unpause whose check errors still goes on by name (since 0.77). Its bridge is joined and removed by name, after the same check.

### From 0.78 to 0.79

- **No gcloud in a run or a conversation** (3, 11.8, 12.6, 13.8, 14.1, 15.3, 16.2, 16.8, 20.3, 21, 25, the appendix; plan `docs/plans/2026-10-26-gcloud-rule8-plan.md` D1 to D11; the requirements' line 261, R123 and R199, the operator's approved words). AGENTS.md safety rule 8 holds over R123, the operator's choice of session 43: the broker gives no run and no conversation gcloud, whatever its sandbox's row says; `sandbox.credentials` and `conversations.credentials` are retired, taken out of a doc saved, imported or read, and schema step 78 takes them out of the stored settings; neither settings screen offers gcloud, and Setup says a run gets none. Only a terminal's sandbox may be started with gcloud, and `crew admin sandbox start --gcloud` is refused for every other kind (security review of 8543b5a). The terminal stays as built, with no gcloud, which rule 8 would allow it.
- **No command in another server's sandbox** (19.7; BUG-015, the review of 1e00ae4, finding 1). A server runs no command in a sandbox another server on the same Docker started, and none when Docker cannot say whose it is: the harness's turns, the probes, and the terminals alike. A command runs on the container the check read, so one made under the name after the check gets none (security review of 54f60eb). Whether its container runs is ErrAnothers too, read from the same inspect.

### From 0.77 to 0.78

Another release's files leave it, and an install that stopped half way stays unmarked.
- **The CLI's files sealed** (12.10, 15.3, 16.9, 21, B15; plan `docs/plans/2026-10-26-cli-files-sealed-plan.md` D1 to D18). The CLI's own files of every run and conversation are stored sealed with the key file, for their conversation and path, with the sum of the sealed bytes; schema step 77 removes the plain ones, so each continued in a new sandbox after the update is rebuilt once. They are put back all or none; a turn whose files cannot be sealed removes the older ones, so a sandbox is not given the CLI's state from before that turn, and a turn that keeps files removes those of any other path, so a file that does not open costs one rebuilt turn that keeps its file, not one at every continue (reviews of 6751b80 and 0d70b2b). Backups made before 0.78 hold the plain rows until they are no longer kept.
- **From the review of 5ddd59a** (19.7; `docs/reviews/2026-10-26-review-5ddd59a-round2-fixes.md`, `-security-5ddd59a-round2-fixes.md`; BUG-015, BUG-016). A server reads neither the log of another server's hosted sandbox nor whether it runs, and a removal whose check of the owner errors keeps the sandbox's doors, so the broker still answers it. The tiers' stacks give the database's files alone to others to read, and write the test's file anew each run.

### From 0.74 to 0.75

- **The news after three setup checks that could not tell** (13.5; the operator's choice in session 36). It said the runs that need the setup wait, which no code does: Crew holds them only while a check on `main` runs or a fix ticket is open. It now says Crew stops trying and the runs go on without waiting.
- **Conversations, the schema and the settings** (12.6, 14.1, 15.3; M20 phase 1, plan `docs/plans/2026-10-25-m20-plan.md` D8, D13, D17, D20, D22). Schema step 76: the purposes of a conversation, the operator's messages, the work folder, a ticket's conversation and its adoption, a comment's quote, a draft's source conversation, and `crew_delete_conversation()`. The data table names `conversation_messages` and no `trajectories` table: a run's trajectory is its events. `conversations.idle_minutes` is 5 to 240, `max_sandboxes` 1 to 20, `summary_every_turns` 5 to 100, and `credentials` `all` or `[]`. As built, no run's spec asks for gcloud, so no run and no conversation gets it whatever the settings say (12.6); the operator keeps that as a follow-up.
- **Conversations, the sandboxes and the runs** (12.6, 13.3, 7.9; M20 phase 2, plan D4, D16, D23, D32, D34). A conversation's sandbox is of the kind `conversation` and holds what a run of its project holds; one about no project names no repository and gets no GitHub token. Both kinds are large with Docker inside, and a conversation's run is counted for room also while it waits for the next message. Its turns are runs of one subtask of the scope `conversation`; such a subtask ends done or stopped, never waiting, so only the operator's next message continues it. Its run waits `conversations.idle_minutes`, and its CLI's files are kept `conversations.keep_cli_files_days`. A turn takes a lead slot. A chat reads its ticket's branch, its subtasks' branches and main, and once the ticket is Done main at its merge commit, kept in `runs.git_commit` (step 76); a ref that changed between turns is a new run.
- **Conversations, the work folder and the chat's workspace** (11.8, 12.4, 12.10; M20 phase 2, plan D7, D17, D18, D33, D35). A conversation's work folder, `/home/agent/conversation`, is saved after every turn that changed it: folders and regular files only, a link or a special file left out and named on the Sandbox card, at most 200 MB of files and 20000 entries, with the values of the sandbox's credentials taken out of each file, and put back in the next sandbox with the same limits. A chat's workspace is fetched again and reset before every turn, before the CLI's own files are kept out.
- **Conversations, the Crew MCP** (11.5, 11.9; M20 phase 3, plan D3, D5, D19, D36). The assistant reaches the read tools, with `list_eval_results` and `get_metrics` of the Ask group, and in a conversation `create_ticket`, whose ticket has the origin `conversation`, names the conversation and the run, and counts in the run's ten. A chat's four tools have the group Chat and their limits in 11.5's table: `get_trajectory` is a project's tool now, bound for a chat to its ticket's runs, and the improver's as before, a page of 500 events with `after`.
- **Conversations, from an answer to the work** (7.3, 9.3, 10.2, 11.6, 11.8, 11.9; M20 phase 3, plan D3, D9 to D11, D19, D26 to D28). Make a ticket of this adopts the ticket the answer's run proposed: while no Analysis run took it up, the form's text replaces the assistant's and it is approved by adding it; later only its adoption is recorded and its text, status and stage stay; one that ended, or was adopted, is refused, and the form adds a new ticket. With no proposal it adds the operator's own, of origin `conversation` or `chat`, whose `seen` says the assistant drafted it, and a chat's is linked as created from its ticket. Tell the agent and Remember this write as `assistant (from the chat)`: the working agent reads such a comment, and any memory entry of it, as data whose source is the chat's assistant, sent on by the operator, and the index's cap does not refuse such an entry, which the operator confirmed. The operator's own comment on marked text keeps the quote and where it was marked, and the agent reads the quote inside a fence after the operator's words. Start a plan from this makes a draft whose first message gives the operator's words as theirs and the answers fenced as data. A question in a chat is the signal `asked_about` and a line of the ticket's history, neither holding what was asked.
- **Conversations, the summaries and the view** (12.10; M20 phase 3, plan D21, BUG-007). Crew's summary is written every `conversations.summary_every_turns` turns, for the operator's conversations about a project and the chats too. The view of a conversation is read in one snapshot, so it never shows a sandbox idle before the answer its turn wrote.
- **Conversations, the routes** (15.4, 6.2; M20 phase 4, plan D15, D30, D31, D37, D38). The conversation routes serve a conversation and a chat alike by the conversation's id; a chat is read and asked by its ticket, `GET` and `POST /projects/{p}/tasks/{t}/chat`, as the API names a ticket. Every one is plain. A refusal answers 400 `FAILED_PRECONDITION`, as the board's do. A chat is not in a project's export.

### From 0.72 to 0.73

- **Hosting, the build and the start** (6, 13.3, 13.10, 19.2, 19.3; M19 phase 4, plan `docs/plans/2026-10-25-m19-plan.md` D1, D11, D15, D18, D20, D22, D29 to D31, D41, D42). A project's image is built from `main` in a sandbox of the kind `host-build`, with no agent, in the mode of the project's checks, by Crew's own line with the Dockerfile as its argument; its tar is cleaned of every name and loaded by the server, which takes the id Docker gives only when it is the config's digest (the overlay2 store) or the manifest's (containerd's). Before the app starts, a probe on its new bridge must find the server, the ingress and the machine silent and, on `app-`, the internet answering. The app runs as `crew`'s uid with no capability, beside what serves, until its health address answers; then it serves, and the one before goes 20 seconds later. A failure leaves what served serving. The data is copied with what serves paused; a roll back can put back the data as the deploy rolled back to left it, and when it fails, puts back the data and the version of before. A hosted sandbox is counted at its size and gets its room before every agent's; a deploy a restart cut off is failed, "interrupted by a restart of Crew". Schema step 75 lets a sandbox's mode be a hosted bridge's.

- **Hosting, the operator's flows** (13.1, 16.9, 19.2, 19.5, 20.3; M19 phase 5, D14, D16, D17, D18, D21). Host locally on a project whose `main` has no Dockerfile makes the ticket that makes it hostable, once, and hosting goes on by itself when `main` has it. Follow main deploys `main`'s head once, listing the tickets Done since; a head that failed is not tried again until a newer one. A change of a live project's internet, size, port, health address, data folder or values starts its image again at once. A roll back asks for a fresh sign-in; Deploy, Stop and the logs do not. Archive stops hosting, Pause leaves it, Delete removes its data, copies, logs, images and values. What a hosted project prints is copied every minute into a file a day, at most 256 MiB, kept `hosting.log_days`. The server mounts `<data>/hosted`, which the host setup makes as `crew`'s.

- **Hosting, the screen, the news, the texts** (18.1, 19.2, 19.4 to 19.7, 20.3, the route table; R150; M19 phase 6, plan D3, D16, D19). The Hosting screen as host.png. A hosted project that stopped answering is news sent at once, once per episode; a deploy that went live is a line of the daily mail. Follow main is one deploy of `main`'s head at a time, listing the tickets Done since, and R150 says so. An own domain and several containers come later, together. 19.7 says how hosting is built.

- **From the reviews of M19** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-25-review-m19-whole-range.md`, `-security-m19.md`; 13.3, 16.9, 19.4, 19.7). A server lists and reconciles only the sandboxes it labelled with its own container's name (those of a release before have none and are the installed server's), so a second server on the same Docker removes none of another's. Health leaves a project whose deploy runs. The route and health read the port and the health address of what serves, kept at its switch. A restart of Crew during a roll back with data puts the data and what served back. A roll back holds Follow main off the head it left. The backup walks a project's data through a root, and leaves out a file the disk has no room for; the restore writes a project's data through a root at its own `data/`; a copy of the data needs room beside the margin. A change one hosted project's page asks of another is refused. An image may not set Crew's labels; `hosting.data` must be a clean path.

### From 0.71 to 0.72

- **Hosting, the schema** (6, 15.3, 19; M19 phase 1, plan `docs/plans/2026-10-25-m19-plan.md` D7, D29). Schema step 75: each hosted project's state, and every deploy, one at a time running; a hosted project's settings and secrets are a row of `credentials`, not a table of their own.

The server gets `CREW_APPS_DOMAIN`, and hosts nothing while it is empty, Crew's own address, or one of the two is under the other; a project whose name is not a DNS label, or is `crew` or `auth`, is not hosted. `hosting.*` is bounded: the port from 1024, as the app runs as no root; plain paths for the health address, the data folder, which is none of the guest's own, and the Dockerfile; the numbers with a floor and a ceiling. Turning `hosting.internet` on asks for a fresh sign-in. A hosted project's settings and secrets are set as a whole behind a fresh sign-in, at most 50, not `CREW_*` or `PORT`, and only their names are shown.

- **Hosting, the ingress** (16.11, 19.4; M19 phase 2, D5, D6, D25, D26, D33, D38). The ingress asks the server for the hosted routes every 10 seconds on a door of its own, by a name only `crew-ingress` has; hosted projects sign in at the apps domain's own portal, with its own cookie and an HKDF key; who may open one is in its policy by mail address; a hosted app gets no token, no portal cookie, and sets no cookie for the apps domain; with a DNS token, one wildcard site and the apps zone's records. `APPS_DOMAIN` has no default; doctor names the second redirect address.

### From 0.69 to 0.70

- **From the reviews of M17's Close** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-24-review-m17-whole-range.md`, `-security-m17-p3-p5.md`, `-security-m17-p4-p7-sessions-another.md`; 14.3, 16.10, 16.11, 16.12, 20.3). The ingress sets its token only on Crew's own route, as R172 already said: with it the server takes the admin's mail and a sign-in's time, so a hosted app that got it could call Crew's API as a fresh operator. A fresh sign-in is asked also for taking a held main, a project's export, `agents.checks_use_another_family` and `conversations.credentials`, and a project's own checks on the builder's family. `crew-ctl restart-if-quiet` fails its unit without `flock` and when a restart was needed for more than 8 days with no yes; a sandbox whose container Docker could not remove after its work ended does not hold the restart; a reboot that fails writes its news once. The registry's token file is root's or sudo's user's, no link, 0600. A release's image file is held to an index's one linux/amd64 manifest, of no variant, that names the config, as a pull already was. On another installation the Updates card says a release never installs by itself, and Versions says whether the next release installs by itself by its clock, not its kind alone.

An index is refused when it holds any entry besides its one exact linux/amd64 manifest that Docker could take for linux/amd64 once it normalised it (case, `x86_64`, a variant, an empty field, no platform); other platforms, as PostgreSQL's index has, stay. `restart-if-quiet` counts the 8 days from a stamp in `/run` of when it first saw the restart wanted. The token file is checked and read through one open of it, with no link followed.

### From 0.60 to 0.61

- **Go's modules come through the server's module proxy** (13.6, 16.2 step 4, 21; found at L5 on dave, S15-D4). L5's second build from Crew's board failed at `crew-caddy`: proxy.golang.org answers the zips of large modules (klauspost/compress, cloudflare/circl, google.golang.org/api) with a redirect to `storage.googleapis.com/proxy-golang-org-prod/`, off `sandbox.sources` since 0.57; the build passed only with the host on dave's list for that build. The operator chose that Crew's proxy answers `GET /goproxy/<path>` for Sources sandboxes only, fetches proxy.golang.org itself and follows a redirect only into that bucket's path, GET only, size-capped, with no fallback; the server sets `GOPROXY` in Sources sandboxes, `release.sh` passes it as a build argument, and each Dockerfile that runs Go declares `ARG GOPROXY`. `make check` hands it to its Go containers too, so a Sources check takes the same way. A change to `release.sh` reaches a board build only from the release after the one that carries it; until a release built with this is installed, a board build needs `storage.googleapis.com` for its build, with the operator's yes each time.

The module proxy's ten minutes bound what it writes to the sandbox too, so one that stops reading gives back its tunnel; a sandbox not in Sources is logged as refused itself, not as a refused host of the list's measure; 13.6 names the module proxy's 403, 404 and 405 beside its 502; 20.3's door on the sandbox networks names the proxy and the module proxy; the list's comment names Go's bucket, which the server reaches and the list does not hold.

### From 0.59 to 0.60

L5's first build from Crew's board failed at the export of the runtime image, "failed to receive status: rpc error: code = Unavailable desc = error reading from server: EOF", as the probe of the thirteenth session had once: the memory cgroup of a large sandbox, 4 GB over Kata's base, killed the guest's Docker daemon while it exported the layers, which needs about 4.1 to 4.4 GB. A probe of the same sandbox with 2 GB failed every time with the kernel's oom-kill of `dockerd`; with 4 GB it passed once in three; with 6 GB it passed. The operator chose a size of its own, `sandbox_sizes.build`, 6 GB over the base, for the build and the gate's make check, which the capacity check counts at that size and among the sandboxes with Docker inside, and which waits for room at it (`room_waits` takes the size `build`); builders and testers keep the large size, and so do a project's checks, whose `make check` passed in a large check sandbox (from the review of d790d32). On dave, `sandbox_sizes.large.memory_gb` was set to 6 for L5, until the release with this size is installed; then it is set back to 4. After a roll back to a release before 0.60, a stored `sandbox_sizes.build` is taken out before the machine's settings are saved again (from the security review of d790d32).

- **From the reviews of c5eff65..5a72946** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-22-review-code-docs-c5eff65-5a72946.md`, `-security-c5eff65-5a72946.md`). A version the look takes has at most nine digits a part and no leading zero, so a publisher cannot make a key or a title that does not fit; a ticket the board refuses costs its line, and the look is kept; the reading of the publishers is bounded to five minutes; a ticket's title names the publisher, and an images' ticket each image, for the release's notes; the build runs `agy --version` only as `nobody`. Accepted, as the operator's answer 5 and 13.6 say: `storage.googleapis.com` lets a Sources sandbox reach every bucket, as `github.com` lets it reach every repository.
- **`agy` is pinned** (12.8, 13.4, 13.6, 16.12; the operator's answer 5 of the twelfth session). A probe of a Sources sandbox ran the vendor's install script, which failed: it reads a release manifest from a host that `sandbox.sources` does not list. The manifest names the version, a versioned tarball, and its sha512, so `agy` has a numbered download after all. `versions.lock` pins the three; the runtime image downloads the tarball, checks it, and fails on another version; the install script is not run. `sandbox.sources` gets `storage.googleapis.com`, which serves the tarball and takes uploads to any bucket (13.6 says so), and loses `antigravity.google`, which served only the script. A machine that saved its own `sandbox.sources` keeps it whole: there the operator adds the host, or the build fails at the tarball with the proxy's refusal. `check-versions.py` checks the address against the vendor's pattern and the version in it. `image/bump-pins.sh`, the unwired copy of styrman-crew's script, is removed. The gh pin moved to 2.103.0: GitHub's source dropped 2.102.1, which broke every build that day.

### From 0.54 to 0.55

- **The machine's `crew-ctl` compared with the running release** (16.11, 16.12; Q12 of M16, and the reviews of 864fb57..97382bc). Install records the hash of each deploy file it put in place, in `install.json` and in the releases folder; every run of `crew-ctl` compares the files in place with the running release's deploy archive, and Versions the record, and both give the one command for root: `install --deploy-files`, new, which puts the running release's deploy files in place once its archive checks against `release.json` and its gate, by their names alone, and changes nothing else. Every Python of `crew-ctl` is isolated. On dave every gate and build ran the checkout's `crew-ctl`, as the installed one lagged.
- **Taking an improvement out before it is installed** (10.5, 16.2 step 3, 16.3; the operator's answer 3 of report 10, and the cold read of P5's D9, `docs/reviews/2026-10-22-review-m16-s10-p5-plan.md`). Take it out of an improvement whose ticket is Done was refused, since only an open ticket could be closed. Now it makes a revert of the ticket and removes the improvement, and a release that holds the improvement and not its revert is held, not refused: the build goes on, and the release waits for Install with the reason. The hold is read from the history at each notice, on every round of the releases before the clock, and by the clock for the release it installs, so a release made before the press is held too, and a press that fails halfway is held on the next round; it reads the project from the improvement's ticket and holds when it cannot read (security review of 1863a0e..f8d57b2: it held nothing on a server without its builder). A removed improvement no longer takes the one improvement's place in a build.
- **Crew's project is never Done** (6.6, 7.2, 9.8, 16.2, 22; the operator's answer 2 of report 10, S10-D13). On dave its first ticket's Done made the project Done: it ran a retrospective, a final report, and the fit check of an eval case the retrospective proposed, pushed the tag `done` to GitHub, and could no longer be paused. Crew's project has no plan, and its board takes tickets for as long as Crew runs, so it is never Done. Schema step 65 pauses a Done Crew's project, as the operator's own pause, which no limit's resume undoes.
- **A builder whose run ended while it waited is taken up** (11.3, 12.10; the operator's answer 1 of report 10, S10-D11). On dave the operator's pause came between CREW-1's `request_move` and its checks; its run ended at its idle time while the project was paused, and after Resume nothing started its judge, since a ticket whose builder ran was left to the merge queue. A check and a judge that take longer than the idle time ended the run the same way. Now such a builder's turn is taken up as if it had just ended: its checks and its judge start with no turn of the builder, and a round that fails continues the conversation in a new microVM; at the round limit the run is ended for its rounds, and its subtask retried once.
- **A check is capped by its project's runs, not its ticket's** (13.6; the operator's choice during L4, S10-D12). On dave the lead made CREW-1 Model only, and its merge check, capped at the ticket's runs since 0.53, ran in Model only, so `make check` could not reach its sources and failed twice. A ticket narrowed to Model only now narrows its agent runs alone, which hold the login; its checks have the project's `check_network`, never wider than the project's runs. A ticket the operator set wider still widens its checks, as since M10.

### From 0.53 to 0.54

- **What a release holds, and Build a release** (16.2 steps 3 and 6, the routes; P5, the cold read `docs/reviews/2026-10-22-review-m16-s10-p5-plan.md`). A version's detail carries what it holds since the release before, read from Crew's project for the commit of its folder or its build: its tickets by key and title, and the commits from outside, or why that cannot be read; and its kind, protected paths, and number of changed paths against the running release. The candidate's panel shows them and its `release.json`, every title, subject, path, and pin marked as text from outside the app. A server build's notes are written from the same lists, titled with Crew's count of them, and its GitHub page shows each line as text (from the reviews of 95d116d, which also bound the reading: once for a release with a folder, at most 50 merges no ticket made, and a note instead of a failed detail). Versions offers Build a release where the server builds and no build runs, with a choice of patch, minor, or major, and asks once. Taking an improvement out of a release not yet installed waits for a later session.
- **From the reviews of 0.54's first changes** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-22-review-621d116-a9532aa.md`, `-security-621d116-a9532aa.md`; 9.4, 12.3, 16.2). Only a lead's analysis confirms a bug's ticket: a reviewer analyses a fix ticket the lead created, and escapes choose its model. The ticket must have been Done before the bug was reported. A confirmed ticket is settled, so a replacement fix ticket's analysis records no second signal; a proposal, or a notice that waits, confirms nothing. A hold a server before 0.53 stored is shown in today's words. The amounts of an old gate are cut where it is shown (Versions, its detail, a release's page), never where the updater reads `gate.json`, which stays as written; and only the old lines' own shapes are cut. Make check's proxy values are quoted for the shell. `check-clean` lists an exported tree's links to files, as git lists them.
- **Analysis confirms which ticket a bug is in** (9.4, 10.2, 11.5, 12.3, 15.3, 22; the operator's choice of report 9, option B). Since 0.53 the escaped defects alone decide the model of an agent that checks, and the ticket a bug is in was its reporter's word. Now an agent's approval of a fix ticket says in `bugIn` which Done ticket's change the defect is in, as it found it in main's history, or none; it is refused without it, and with a ticket that is not Done. The analysis is never the reporter's run. Only a confirmed ticket counts an escape and gets the signal `escaped_defect`, which `report_bug` no longer records by itself; the operator's word is confirmed as it is given. Schema step 64 adds `bugs.in_confirmed`.
- **`make check` runs in Crew's project's check sandbox** (13.6, 14.1, 16.2; L4, the operator's answer 3 of report 9). Unpaused for one docs-only ticket, the project's first check failed at once: the backend's check of main, in a Sources check sandbox, which gets the commit as `git archive` writes it. What did not work there, each found on a probe made as such a sandbox is, Crew's own proxy in front: `tools/check-clean.py` and two crew-ctl tests needed a `.git` (an exported tree has every file read; the tests make a commit of a copy); make check's containers and its check image's build did not get the proxy, and a build resolves its `FROM` past the daemon's proxy (the Makefile names the proxy to each, and pulls the check image's base images through the daemon first); Debian's packages came over plain HTTP (the check image moves them to HTTPS, as the runtime image does); a user namespace cannot mount `/sys` in a container, and the microVM's kernel has no dummy link (in a sandbox the firewall test runs as the microVM's root in namespaces of its own, on bridges, as Docker's are); and Docker Hub served the layers from `production.cloudfront.docker.com`, which joins `sandbox.sources`. The same make check is the gate's own, from Build a release (16.2 step 5).
- **Versions shows no amount of a gate recorded before 0.53** (16.2 steps 5 and 6; the operator's choice of report 9). The real-ticket lines of GATE-1 to GATE-3 on dave said "for $1.13" and the like, in the release folders' `gate.json` and in the gates' rows. They are shown without the amount wherever a gate is shown, so Versions, a version's detail, the inbox, and a release's page on GitHub carry none; the file and the rows stay as written. Versions marks every text from outside the app as not its own, and the no-dollar check reads Versions: on dave's 0.2.19 it failed there, on the errors of the failed gates 0.2.17 and 0.2.18, which quote the check's own title.

### From 0.52 to 0.53

- **Crew's project's checks** (13.6, 14.1, 16.2; the operator's choice of report 8, option A). Crew's project checks with `make check`, no setup, in a check sandbox with a Docker daemon of its own, in Sources; its runs get no gcloud. A project's check sandboxes may have a network mode of their own, `sandbox.check_network`, Sources among them, which no sandbox that runs an agent gets. Make Crew's project gives these settings; schema step 61 gives them to the project made before. It stays paused.
- **From round 2 of the reviews** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-22-review-dc298f7-d264d4c-round2.md`, `-security-round2-dc298f7-d264d4c.md`; 12.3, 16.11). A run of an agent that checks counts one escape at most. The updater's lock is kept a root's regular file of 0600, and uninstall takes it out. The shared no-dollar check reads the screens that hold a plan's, a ticket's or a run's own words for amounts alone, and knows "5 €" and "EUR 5".
- **From the reviews of the estimates** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-22-review-09aa226-dc298f7.md`, `-security-no-cost-estimate.md`; 12.3, 15.3). An agent that checks is better only when at least `better_points` fewer of its runs let a defect escape. Schema step 63 also measures an improvement of `cost_per_ticket` by `tokens_per_ticket`. The shared no-dollar browser check reads more screens, titles and labels, and more words (an estimate, USD, dollars, a euro amount, cost), with a unit test of its rules. Which ticket a bug is in is the reporter's word, which Analysis does not confirm: it was so before 0.53, and is an open item.
- **From the reviews of 0.53** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-22-review-58e18a0-09aa226.md`, `-security-no-dollars-crew-checks.md`; 7.7, 10.4, 13.6, 16.2, 16.11). A check's network mode is never wider than its runs'. Schema step 61 takes Crew's project by `improvements.crew_project`, not by its key. The backend's own tickets are no work agents added, and 7.7 says the scope guard applies to every project with plan tickets from 0.53 on. The improver's schedule is each 1 or more, now that no weekly budget bounds it. The updater's lock is beside its socket, in root's folder, never a link.
- **Four small fixes from report 8** (16.4 step 4, 16.9, 16.11). Versions says whether a release is copied to GitHub: with `updates.publish` off, the line of a release that passed its gate said "being copied to GitHub", and nothing was copied. One updater runs at a time: it takes a lock in its folder before it reads a request or touches the socket, so a second one stops (on dave, `docker exec crew-updater crew-updater --version` started a second updater that took the socket over and removed it). The updater refuses `CREW_UID` or `CREW_GID` 0. The smoke microVM is bounded as the restore's is.
- **No limit in dollars** (3, 4, 6.2, 6.3, 7.2, 7.3, 7.5 to 7.9, 8.2, 9.6, 9.8, 10.2, 10.4, 10.5, 11.5, 12.4, 14.1, 15.2, 15.3, 16.2, 18.1, 18.3, 20.1, 20.2, 21, 22, 23, 28; the operator's choice, 2026-10-21, report 8). Gone: the run's budget and the stop reason `budget` (a run stopped for it before keeps it), a ticket's budget with its over-budget event, signal, and mark, the project's budget and its pause, the weekly budgets of housekeeping, the retrospective, and the improver, a suite's cap, and the real ticket's $2. Gone from the settings: `budget_usd`, `limits.run_budget_usd`, `limits.default_task_budget_usd`, `knowledge.housekeeping_budget_usd`, `knowledge.evals.budget_usd`, `knowledge.evals.max_cost_increase`, and `improvements.budget_usd_per_week`. What bounds the work instead: a run's turns, minutes, goal rounds, and the watchdog; a ticket's fix rounds, one new approach, one cancel and replace, and its added subtasks; a project's tickets approved with the OK, and the scope guard, which now counts tickets: `max_added_share` is the share of the plan's tickets that agents may add, and a project with no tickets from the plan has none; and the subscriptions' windows for the rate of all work. Housekeeping starts at most once in 24 hours, a retrospective runs once per completed part and once at Done, the improver by its schedule, and a suite is its cases times its repeats. The real ticket of a gate is bounded by its runs' turns and minutes, its fix rounds, and the gate's eight hours. Schema step 62 takes the removed keys out of the stored settings, and an import takes them out of a file.
- **No cost estimate** (4, 6.3, 7.2, 7.11, 8.1, 8.3 to 8.5, 9.1, 9.6, 9.7, 10.2, 10.4, 10.5, 10.7, 11.9, 12.3, 12.7, 12.10, 14.1, 15.3, 15.4, 16.2, 20.1, 20.2, 22, 23, 28; the operator's choice, 2026-10-21, report 8). Crew prices nothing: the machine settings `prices` and `agents.auto.cheaper_share` are gone. Where a cost was shown, tokens and time are: on runs, subtasks, trajectories, summaries, the task view, the Projects screen, the API, and Analytics, whose Cost is now Use. The final report and the gate's report carry no cost, and "not worse" compares no cost. The model choice decides on results alone: "as well, for less" is gone, and an agent that checks changes only to an entry that let fewer defects escape and asked for a fix about as often; the watch of a change made before keeps its own mirror. The signal `cost_and_time` keeps its name and holds tokens and minutes; `over_budget` is no longer recorded. The cost and budget columns stay, unread and unwritten, for a roll back, until a later release drops them (15.3); schema step 63 takes the two settings out of the stored settings and dismisses an open recommendation for the reason `cheaper`. The agent CLI's adapter keeps a field for a cost a CLI reports, which `agy` never fills and Crew no longer reads: the adapter is part of the runtime image, whose change would hold a release's live steps for Install, so the field goes with the next release that changes that image.

### From 0.51 to 0.52

M16's phases, built on 0.51. The operator chose that this version keeps one heading for the sessions that build on it (2026-10-20).

- **What building M16's first phase settled** (9.6). The fit check's judge reads the case's text as an eval's builder is given it, the held-out files as the case's merge had them, and the rest of that merge's diff, which shows what the work introduced; it answers `NOTHING MISSING`, or one `MISSING:` line for each thing. Its instruction is Crew's own and not a file of the baseline, so no improvement and no override changes the check that guards what the evals measure. Its run has the scope `eval`, and its cost is the project's. An answer in another form, or a run that ended without one, is asked for again, three times in all, and then the case waits for the operator with why. Only a waiting case's text is changed: an active case was measured on its text, so a new text is a new case, made again from its ticket once the old one is retired. Accept keeps what the check listed beside the case. The kept output of a failed held-out check is read with its suite's runs (`GET /projects/{p}/evals/runs`), and no agent reads it. An eval run that ends, or a suite that stops, stops every run of its subtasks that has not ended with the driver's stop, which interrupts a turn that runs: the end used before refused a run mid-turn, which is why such a run went on to its time limit. A subtask that waited at a limit and went on in a run the eval run does not name is stopped too. A case the lead proposes is no longer active at once when its held-out files need what its text does not state, which narrows the known limit of 0.49. From the reviews of this phase (`docs/reviews/2026-10-20-review-m16-p1.md`, `docs/reviews/2026-10-20-security-m16-p1-fit-check.md`): a held-out file that cannot be read is an error, never an empty file, and the case ends waiting with why after three tries, so the case behind it is checked meanwhile; a judge that read only part of the files, which are longer than Crew gives one, makes no case active by itself; with no judge of another family than the builder's offered, the check does not run on the builder's family, and the case waits as not checked; the kept output is made storable, with no NUL and no rune cut in two.
- **What building M16's second phase settled** (16.2). Make Crew's project reads `updates.source_repo` over `https` with a token of the GitHub App that can only read, as an existing repository is imported (6.6), keeps its history, and sets the project's remote to the repository's SSH address, `git@github.com:<owner>/<name>.git`, so `main` is fetched and pushed with Setup's key. The project's name is the repository's, and its key is `CREW`. It runs at once, with no breakdown: no lead breaks its plan into tickets, and its board holds the tickets put on it. The button sets `improvements.crew_project`. A save of the machine's settings that changes it or `updates.source_repo` is refused when the project it names is missing, archived, or hidden, or has another remote than that address, and when `updates.source_repo` is not `owner/name`; a save that changes neither is not held by them, so Crew's own saves, such as its choice of a model, go on. Crew's project is not archived while the setting names it. The gate's fixture project `crew-gate` is made first, once, and a press that made Crew's project but not the setting is finished by the next press, which takes the project only when it has the key `CREW` and that remote. `crew-gate` is a Go module on the machine with no remote, running, with In progress, Review, and Test, $2 a ticket, and 30 turns a run. A project now carries `hidden`, and a hidden one is in none of the operator's lists of projects: the projects list, the menu, the front page, and the Models screen; only `crew-gate` is hidden, and the project `fixture` is still listed (a known item). The real ticket of gate run N adds `Reverse` and its test in a package `gN` of its own, so its check fails on `main` before the work. That Analytics, the improver's signals, the inbox, the operator's MCP tokens, and the measurements of 10.5 leave out `crew-gate` comes with the gate (M16's fourth phase), since nothing runs there before it; it came (10.7).
- **What building Sources settled** (13.3, 13.6, 14.1). The build of a release is a sandbox kind of its own, `build`, a check sandbox with no agent: its grant is empty, so the broker gives it no login, no git identity, and no GitHub token, and it is Sources whatever is asked. Sources is no mode a project or a ticket can choose; the network settings still offer three. A Sources sandbox is started as a Model only one, with no resolver of its own, the server's door as its proxy, and the start-up test of the rules before it is used, and it has its own Docker daemon. The firewall sends `src-` to the Model only chain, `CREW-MDL`, and drops its IPv6 as for the other prefixes. The proxy gives a Sources sandbox the hosts of `sandbox.sources` and a Model only one those of `network.model_only_hosts`, never the other's. `sandbox.sources` starts from the hosts the Dockerfiles read, and the first build on the machine measures it, as M10 measured the model's list. A sandbox the server takes back after a restart gets its mode from its bridge's prefix. Schema step 55 lets a sandbox's row record `sources`.
- **What building `deploy/release.sh` settled** (16.1, 16.2 steps 4 and 5, 16.10, 16.12). The script has two verbs. `build` builds the four images from a tree given as a folder, with the proxy of a Sources sandbox passed to each build, saves them and PostgreSQL's image as files, and records what the runtime image got of `agy`; it is all a check sandbox runs. `write` writes `release.json` and the deploy archive with the release's own server program, `crew release write --tree`, from the tree's archive; only `crew-ctl build` runs it, on a checkout an operator trusts. `crew-ctl build`, and the first install from a clean checkout, export the commit with `git archive` and run both verbs on that tree, never on the checkout's files as they lie. A release Crew builds gets its `release.json` from the running updater instead, from the facts the running server read of the commit itself: the candidate's own program, code agents wrote, writes no fact a running release decides by. The runtime image's inputs are each file's sha256, and one hash of them all, `runtimeSha256`: everything under `image/`, `versions.lock`, `go.mod` and `go.sum`, what `harness/` holds that is not text for agents (its hooks, scripts, settings, and assets: code that runs in the image), and every Go file and embedded file of the module's packages that `cmd/crew-run` and `cmd/crew-harnessbuild`, which the image also builds, are made from, for linux on amd64 without cgo. They are read with Go's own package rules from the tree, not by `go list`, so the running release needs no Go and runs nothing of the candidate's; the packages from outside the module are fixed by `go.sum`, and Go by `versions.lock`. A release that records no inputs, such as every one before this, never has the running release's. The deploy archive, `deploy.tar.gz`, holds `crew-ctl`, `release.sh`, the host setup, the proof, the Compose file, the database's roles, `lib/`, the firewall, and `versions.lock`, at their paths in the repository, root's, 0755 or 0644, with no time, so the same tree gives the same bytes; `release.json` names it by its hash, and `Verify` checks it with the images. A tree whose schema or machine setup is not the program's is another commit's, and is refused.
- **What building the build settled** (16.2 step 4, 16.11, 13.10, schema step 56). A build is a row of `builds`: one runs at a time, and a version is built once, while a failed build leaves its number free. The server reads the running release's deploy archive, checks it against the hash `release.json` names, and takes `deploy/release.sh` from it; a running release with no deploy archive, as every one before this, builds nothing, so the first release with one is built by hand. It exports the commit from Crew's project's canonical repository as a check sandbox's tree is written (13.9), reads the commit's files, agents, runtime inputs, schema, and deploy archive itself, waits for room as every check sandbox does, and runs `release.sh build` in a sandbox of the kind `build`, as the two commands `build-runtime` and `build-rest` (below). The sandbox's home is the sandbox's to change, so the five image files and `pins.json` are copied out of it as plain files that do not lead out of it, hashed as they are copied, into `builds/` of the data folder, which the server writes and the updater reads. The updater's new action, `POST /load`, copies each file into a folder of its own, checks that the copy has the hash the server read, loads each image only when its file names its own tag alone, writes `release.json` from the server's facts and its own hashes, with the notes, and renames the folder to the version; anything refused leaves no folder and untags what it loaded. A load and a request of the updater exclude each other, since both read and change Docker's tags, and a build waits up to two hours for a busy updater (round 2 of the review). `agy`'s version comes from the candidate's image as data: one printable line of at most 80 characters, or `unknown`. A build the server's stop cut off is failed when it starts again. The build's log is the end of its output, 64 KB.
- **What building the trigger settled** (16.2 step 3, the routes). The commit is chosen on `main`'s own line, its first parents, since the newest release whose commit the repository holds (a release built from the operator's checkout before Crew's project was made may hold none, and then the whole line counts). A merged ticket is a merge commit on that line that was its ticket's merge candidate and passed its check; an improvement's is one whose ticket an improvement made. The build stops at the commit before the second improvement's merge; with no merge of a ticket on the line, only the operator's pushes, `release_after_tickets` builds nothing and the button builds. The number is one more than the highest of the release folders, the builds that did not fail, and the running version, in its patch, its minor (the patch to 0), or its major; `POST /crew/versions/build` takes `{"bump": "patch" | "minor" | "major"}`. The clock builds a commit by itself once: a build of it that failed waits for the press. `GET /crew/versions/{v}` gives the version's build, with the end of its output, and its `release.json` once it is a folder.
- **From the reviews of the build** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-20-review-m16-p3.md`, `docs/reviews/2026-10-20-security-m16-p3-build.md`; 13.10, 16.2 step 4, 16.3, 16.4). An image file's names are read as Docker reads them: a name is cleaned, so `./index.json` is `index.json`, a second entry of `manifest.json`, `index.json`, `repositories`, or `oci-layout` is refused, and the legacy `repositories` names tags too; a tag named in two of them is one tag. The file is not taken at its word: the updater reads the tags of the release's own names, the four of Crew and PostgreSQL's, before and after each load, also when the load failed, and a load that moved one, PostgreSQL's shared tag or its own included, or gave another, is undone and refused, so a load creates its own tag and moves none of those; a tag that cannot be put back is said in the refusal. A registry push's names are not the load's. A file is copied out at most as long as it was when it was checked, since the sandbox still runs. `crew-ctl install --upgrade-runtime`, which the refusal of a release that needs a newer setup names, is now a flag: an install from the checkout, which runs the host setup again; it does not replace Docker or Kata of another major version. A release whose PostgreSQL image keeps the tag and changes the image is code. The runtime image's inputs also hold the root `.dockerignore`, which decides what its `COPY` lines take, and each runtime package's assembly headers and `.syso` files; a runtime package with C, C++, Objective-C, Fortran, or SWIG files is refused, since the image builds without cgo. A running build counts as a large check in the capacity check, and the build holds the check's lock until its sandbox's row exists; a build's sandbox is removed when the server starts, as a check's is. A build's files are each at most 12 GB and 20 GB in all, and the disk keeps 10 GB free beyond them, which the server checks before it copies them out and the updater before it copies them in; each file is opened without following a link and without waiting, and checked once it is open; the updater checks that the folder it opened is the one it checked, and keeps the new folder root's alone until it is whole. A commit is cut off at the size a sandbox takes while it is exported. A fault of the server's on Build a release is a 500, not a precondition. The machine setup is 3: `builds/` and the rules of `src-` are the host setup's, so an installed machine is brought to it by `crew-ctl install --build` from a checkout, and the start-up test's message names the prefix of its mode. Left for the gate (M16's fourth phase): the candidate's runtime image comes out of the same sandbox as the candidate's other images, built after them, so equal inputs alone do not make it the running release's image; the live steps must not give it a login on the strength of the inputs alone.
- **The runtime image is built in a step of its own** (16.2 step 4). The question the build's review left for the gate is answered: `release.sh` has the verbs `build-runtime`, which builds and saves only the runtime image from `image/Dockerfile` and records what it got of `agy`, and `build-rest`, which builds the other images; `build` is both, for `crew-ctl build`. The server runs them as two commands in the one build sandbox and copies the runtime image and `pins.json` out, hashed, before the second command starts, and never reads what the second leaves at those names. Before the first command nothing of the candidate's has run but the extraction of its tree, so equal inputs make the runtime image a build of the running release's code. The other answers were a build sandbox of its own for the runtime image, which costs a second microVM a build, and the running release's runtime image by its id, which would not test the new prompts and skills in the image. The operator chose the two commands (2026-10-20).
- **Debian's packages over HTTPS** (13.6, 16.2 step 4). A Sources sandbox's proxy takes only CONNECT, so a source a Dockerfile reads over plain HTTP would not leave the build's sandbox, and Debian's images read `http://deb.debian.org`. Each stage that runs `apt-get` first copies the CA bundle of the pinned Go image, moves the `URIs:` of `debian.sources` to `https://deb.debian.org`, checks that none is plain HTTP, and tells apt where the bundle is; apt's packages are signed either way. The proxy stays CONNECT only. The operator chose this over a proxy that also forwards plain HTTP for marked hosts (2026-10-20). A build on the machine fetched every Debian package over HTTPS, before any build in a Sources sandbox.
- **What building the gate's first part settled** (16.2 step 5, 16.4, 16.12, schema step 57). `gate.json` gains its live part: `live` is `pending` from the updater's steps until the server's result is in, then `passed`, `failed`, or `skipped`, with `liveSteps`, `skipped` (each step that did not run, with why), `hold`, and `costUsd`. Pending is never passed: the folder has no gate while it waits, Install and the clock are refused with "the gate is pending", and `crew-ctl update` passes it by; only root's build by hand installs it. A gate written before 0.52 has no live part and is what its `passed` says. The updater's `POST /gate` takes the caller's steps and install test, bound to `release.json`'s hash, runs steps 2 to 4 of 16.4, the selftest, and the browser tests, removes the check server, and writes `gate.json` pending, or failed with every step that ran; a failed step of the caller's writes a failed gate and starts nothing. A release whose gate passed or waits is not gated again. `POST /gate/live` writes the server's result into a pending gate bound to the same `release.json`, once. The check server's spec is the updater's own `docker run`: the server's image by its id, the user crew, the scratch network, the check's three secrets, and the install file's address, admin email, data folder, and time zone; no Docker socket, no group of Docker's, no sandboxes' folder, no key file.
