Compose no longer has `crew-check`, and the check server that `crew-ctl gate` held for its own selftest and browser tests (the request `check`) is gone. With no socket the check server starts no microVM, so the updater starts the smoke run's microVM from the release's runtime image by its id, with no network, and the check server stores the event it printed (`crew admin proof store`); an install's check does the same, and the watch after the switch still has the new server start its own. The browser tests come from the release's `browser.tar` (below, from the reviews). `crew-ctl gate` runs `make check` and the install test on the checkout and sends them to `POST /gate`. Schema step 57 lets `update_requests` record `gate`.
- **What building the server's side of the gate settled** (16.2 step 5, 16.3, 16.11, schema step 58, the routes). The server drives a gate from the `gates` table, a round at a time on the releases' timer, so a restart takes it up where it was: a build that is done gets a gate, one gate's `make check` at a time, in a sandbox of the kind `build` on the commit exported again, waiting for room as a large check; then the updater's `POST /gate`, asked again while the updater is busy; then the live part, decided once its `gate.json` waits for it: at once when `release.json`'s runtime inputs are the running release's, else `waiting` for `POST /crew/versions/{v}/gate`, whose answer and `GET /crew/versions/{v}` give the gate with the runtime inputs that differ. The live steps are stepped every round until their result is in, which goes to the updater as one `POST /gate/live`. A pending gate `crew-ctl gate` asked for on the machine is taken up at its live part. A make check a stop of the server cut off runs again. A gate Crew drives does not run the install test, which needs a virtual machine started on the machine and the operator's yes: with `updates.install_test` `local` it is skipped with that reason, and with `off` as before; neither holds the release (from the review of this phase: the first version held every release for it, which would have ended 0.29's rule that text installs by itself). A gate that skipped a step never gives a release a clock, and a gate that holds puts the release on hold with its reason; the updater also refuses the clock's install of such a release. The eval suites run for a release that changes a file under `baseline/`, `harness/`, `image/`, `internal/cli/`, or `cmd/crew-run/`, by the files of the two `release.json`s.
- **From the reviews of the gate's first part** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-20-review-m16-p4a.md`, `docs/reviews/2026-10-20-security-m16-p4a.md`; 13.6, 16.2 steps 4 and 5, 16.4, 16.9). The browser tests no longer install their packages on the machine: Docker's default bridge, which the updater's first try used, is in none of Crew's firewall chains, and the image came from the candidate's `versions.lock` with any registry. `release.sh build-rest` installs them from the lock file with no install script, in a container of the build's own daemon through its proxy, so in Sources mode, and packs the tests with their packages as `browser.tar`, which the release folder holds and `release.json` names by its hash (`browser`), and `Verify` checks. The updater unpacks plain files and folders only, by cleaned names inside its folder, at most 512 MB and 50 000 entries, and runs the tests by the package's own file, in one microVM on the scratch network alone, in the image `versions.lock` names only when it is Microsoft's Playwright by its hash. Each microVM the updater starts has a name and is removed when its run fails, by a timeout too. The check server runs with no capability, no way to gain one, 4096 processes, and 8 GB. A gate's texts are written with no control character, and an error the updater writes leaves out the values of `-e`. A passed live part names its steps. The release page's gate report names the live part, what was not run, and a hold, escapes Markdown, and leaves out a step's detail, which stays in `gate.json`. The Crew level's minutes per ticket leaves out `crew-gate` too. Open, for the operator: whether the check server, which holds a copy of the real database and runs code agents wrote, runs in a microVM too.
- **The live steps' image** (16.2 step 5, 13.4, schema step 59). While a gate's live steps run, `crew-gate` names the candidate's runtime image by its id (`projects.runtime_image`), and every run of that project starts from it, its reviewer's too; its checks, which decide whether the real ticket passed, run in the running release's image, so the candidate grades nothing of its own (from the security review of the real ticket). The sandbox's row records the image it started from. Only a hidden project can name an image, and only an id. Install's answer, the updater's request, names in its first step each step of the gate that did not run.
- **What building the real ticket settled** (16.2 step 5, 12.7, 13.11). The live steps keep where they are in the gate's row, so a restart takes them up. The real ticket is added to `crew-gate` as the operator's (`GATE-n`, package `gN`), after `crew-gate` names the candidate's runtime image; the board and the pipeline take it as any ticket, a lead's analysis first. It passes when the board moves it to Done. It fails when it is closed, spends more than its budget ($2; the board itself only marks a ticket over its budget, so the gate checks the sum of its runs), when a run of it is stopped, or when it did not reach Done in eight hours outside a limit; a ticket that is stuck, as one that waits for a lead slot or for room, goes on, since the board clears that once a run starts again; it is skipped when no agent CLI is signed in, at its start or on its way, or after six hours at a limit, by a clock the gate keeps itself, since a subtask's own time moves when it continues. A ticket that did not reach Done is cancelled, so nothing of it runs on, and every way the step ends takes the candidate's image from `crew-gate`; each round, and a restart, take it too when no gate's live part runs. A run of `crew-gate` records the image it ran in. A hidden project has no project terminal, whose sandbox would get the agent CLI's login in that image. The eval suites are not built yet: a release that changes what agents see says "the eval suites: they do not run yet in a gate", and so does not install by itself; one that changes nothing agents see says "no eval".
- **From the reviews of the server's side of the gate** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-20-review-m16-p4b.md`, `docs/reviews/2026-10-20-security-m16-p4b.md`). The row follows the folder when an answer of the updater's was lost: a gate asked for whose `gate.json` waits already is decided, and a live one whose gate passed is done; an asked gate whose request left the updater's status, with no gate that waits, fails after two hours; a build whose folder was gated another way gets a row that is done, so it holds no newer build. A pending gate's row is made in one statement. A gate that runs at once because its inputs were the running release's is decided again against the release that runs, and its ticket, if one started, is ended (from the reviews of the real ticket); one the operator pressed is not. Run the live gate names the hash of the runtime inputs whose diff the screen showed, and is refused when it is not the release's. A skipped live part may name the step that did not run, which the release page shows as not run. A gate's texts lose every control, format, and direction-changing character and every separator but the space; the release page escapes `:` and breaks `@`, so it makes no link and no mention. The browser tests' folder is read only in their microVM, with a scratch folder for their results, and the first removal of a microVM left by an earlier run has a bound of its own.
- **What building the gate's eval suites settled** (9.6, 13.4, schema step 60). The operator answered how the suites run in a gate (`.agents/scratch/m16-p4d-questions.md`, 2026-10-20), after the code showed that `baseline/` is built into the server, not into the runtime image, so a suite that only starts from the candidate's image measures the running release's prompts. A suite of purpose `release` runs each case once on side `release`, from the candidate's runtime image with the candidate's six eval files laid over the baseline, and is compared with an earlier suite's baseline side on the cases both measured (`measure.AgainstEarlier`): a case may be retired or added between the two, and the earlier suite ran each case more often, so the cost is a run's. The image travels on the suite, then on the subtask of each agent run (`subtasks.image`, which only an eval's subtask may carry), then on the run's spec to its sandbox; `runs.image` and the sandbox's row record it. A release suite sends no `eval_result` signal, and waits behind the operator's queued suites. The pipeline stops a release suite its gate no longer runs, so a gate that ends any way leaves none running. A suite keeps the kind of what its next run waits for (`wait_for`), which the gate's clock reads. Schema step 60 adds `release` to the purposes and sides, the suite's gate, image, earlier suite, layer, and wait, one release suite a gate and project, the subtask's image, and the gate's suites with their clock and result; an older version reads all of it, and after a roll back would run a release suite left open as a suite with no image and no layer, within its budget, whose result no gate reads (a rare and bounded cost, from the cold read of the plan, `docs/reviews/2026-10-20-review-m16-p4d-plan.md`).
- **From the reviews of the eval suites** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-20-review-m16-p4d-p4f.md`, `docs/reviews/2026-10-20-security-m16-p4d-p4f.md`; 9.6, 13.4, 16.2 step 5, 16.4 step 3). The candidate's image reports what its runs cost, by which the real ticket's budget and the suites' cost are judged, so a release whose live steps ran in an image of other code than the running release's is held when they pass, and Install decides; the goals the suites count are decided by the held-out checks in the running image, as before. A run's sandbox takes a named image only while a release suite of its project from that image is open and its gate is live, so a run that continues an eval's subtask after either ended does not start from the candidate. The pipeline leaves a release suite whose project its gate names with no suite yet: the suite is started before the gate's row records it, and was stopped in that moment as a suite no gate named. `list_eval_results` leaves release suites out, as Analytics does. The check server refuses every runtime that is no microVM's, also when the server is allowed one, and a check server that does not start says it did not start in a microVM. A run's sandbox takes a named image only as the pipeline runs its suite: its gate live, its suites step not ended, and its list naming the suite's project (round 2). `list_eval_results` leaves release suites out in its query, before its limit of 20 (round 2). 16.3 names the gate's hold beside its skipped steps. Not changed: the eval suites' builders, reviewers, and testers in the candidate's image have each project's network mode, setup, and read-only token, as any run of the project; that is what the press consents to, and the press says so.
- **The check server in a microVM** (16.4 step 3). The candidate's server in check mode is code agents wrote, beside a copy of the real database, and it ran on runc as the user crew (an open finding of the security review of the gate's first part). It now runs with the microVM runtime, as the smoke run's and the browser tests' microVMs do, with no fallback to runc: a check that cannot start in a microVM fails, and says so. The operator chose this (2026-10-20). An install by hand starts no check server, so the first gate or update on the machine is what shows that it starts there; 0.2.8, installed by hand on dave, did not show it.
- **What building the gate's suites step settled** (16.2 step 5, 16.3). The operator chose two answers to the cold read of the plan (2026-10-20): when no project could be compared, the step did not run and the release waits for Install, as it did while the suites were not built; and the published hold and notes name no project. Before this, a project that could not be compared would have held nothing, so a prompt release on a machine with few cases would have installed by itself with no eval behind it. The board decides each project (`ReleaseBaseline`), and the live runner starts its release suite, records it in the gate's row, and reads its result when it ended; the gate's row keeps the projects, the clock, and the step, so a restart takes them up, and a suite a stop left between its start and its record is found by its gate and project. The clock is the gate's own, as the real ticket's: six hours on end with the suite waiting for a model, and 24 hours less the time at a limit. A live gate that goes back to waiting forgets its suites with its real ticket, and the pipeline stops any of them that ran; `Abandon` wakes it.
- **From the reviews of the real ticket** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-20-review-m16-p4c.md`, `docs/reviews/2026-10-20-security-m16-p4c.md`; 13.6, 16.2 step 5). One real ticket runs at a time, since `crew-gate` names one image for its runs: a gate whose ticket has not started waits while another's runs, and a gate takes from `crew-gate` only the image it set. The gate records the real ticket's key before it adds the ticket, so a stop between the two adds it once, on the next round. Every way a live gate leaves its live part ends its ticket: its gate passed another way, its folder no longer waits, or the release that runs no longer has its runtime inputs and the operator did not press, after which it waits for the press with no ticket; a ticket that cannot be ended yet, as before the server wired its board, keeps the gate as it is until the next round. The end of a real ticket cancels every other open ticket of `crew-gate` too, one an agent created or a lead's replacement, and the gate's recorded key counts only for a ticket the gate added. A gate whose step fails before it added a ticket holds no slot, and a running release that cannot be read decides nothing. `crew-gate` is made with no setup, `go test ./...` as its test, Model only, and no credentials beyond what every run gets. The live steps with no board say so in the log, round after round.
- **The repository's AGENTS.md in Crew's project** (16.2). Once Crew's project exists, Crew's agents read this repository's `AGENTS.md`, whose rules are written for the operator's coding agent on dave: it runs on the machine, uses `sudo` and Docker, and pushes `main`. The file now opens with a section for an agent in a Crew sandbox, which comes first: it is in a sandbox, pushes only its own branch through the git endpoint, has no root, no socket, and no secret, and is bound by the rules that keep the operator safe. The operator chose it after the security review of M16's second phase, before Crew's project is made on dave.
- **The fit check's judge and the held-out files' author** (9.6). The judge avoids the family of the project's builder, as every judge, and not also the family of the builder whose ticket wrote the held-out files. With two families, avoiding both can leave no judge, and this judge lists what the case's text leaves out; it does not grade that builder's work. The operator chose it after the security review of M16's first phase.
- **What building the gate on Versions settled** (16.2 step 6, the routes). The operator narrowed this part of the screen to the gate (2026-10-21): its steps, the live part, the diff and Run the live gate, what did not run, and the build's log. A gate that failed must show, and the folder's reader leaves it out, so the detail reads the folder's `gate.json` whatever its state, bound to the same `release.json` as a passed one. The candidate is the newest build or gate by when it started, not by its number (a live gate first, from the reviews below); the plan's first rule, by the builds alone, missed a gate `crew-ctl gate` made, and kept a release rolled back from, which is newer than the running release, so a release installed once is none. The panel is the candidate's whatever its state, so a failed build or gate stays on screen until a newer one starts; the Next release card keeps its one line of the gate. The live part's lines are the server's view only while the gate's row is live: once it is not, `gate.json`'s live steps say it. The logs are output of code agents wrote, so the panel removes control characters, colours, and characters that reorder text before it shows them, and the press's words name no project: no endpoint gives each project's network mode, and the suites' projects are fixed only after the real ticket.
- **From the reviews of the gate on Versions** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-21-review-m16-p5.md`, `docs/reviews/2026-10-21-security-m16-p5.md`; 16.2 steps 5 and 6). A gate whose live part runs is the candidate before a newer build, whatever its version, since the clock can start one by itself and would hide the gate that holds the login, and a pressed gate runs on when a newer release is installed. The confirmation keeps the version it was opened on and presses that version, and the panel starts again when the candidate changes. Every text of the panel and of the Updater card is screened, not only the logs, and a path of the diff shows each character outside printable ASCII, and the brackets of those marks, as its code point, so a file name cannot pass for another in the list the press is asked on. A runtime input whose path holds a control character is never the same runtime: `RuntimeSHA256` writes one line for each path, and a path with a newline could make other files hash as the running release's; the hash itself is kept, so the running release's hash still compares. The operator still consents on the paths, not on their content: the later part of the screen shows the candidate's tickets and commits (Q9).
- **The first gate on dave** (16.4 step 3). `crew-ctl gate 0.2.9` failed at Check: the check server, in its microVM since the check server's change above, said its database was down. A microVM has a network stack of its own, and Docker's name server, which answers at 127.0.0.11 in a container's namespace on the machine, does not answer inside it, so `crew-db-check` had no address there; a probe on dave showed the name unresolved from Kata and the database answering by its address. The updater now gives the check server the database's address and the browser microVM the check server's, as the sandboxes reach the server by its address. The smoke microVM has no network and was not touched. The gate of 0.2.11, the first on an updater with that fix (0.2.10, installed by hand), passed Check, the smoke run, and the selftest, and then could not start the browser tests' microVM: their results go to a tmpfs mounted inside their folder, which the microVM sees read only, and no runtime makes a mount point inside a read-only mount ("ENOENT" from Kata, `mkdirat` from runc). The updater then made the folder for the results before the microVM started; the gate after it showed that was not enough, and the results now go outside the tests' folder ("The browser tests' results", below). Twice the cause was missing from the gate's report: a failed command's error quoted only the end of what it printed, and Kata prints the cause first and a long stack trace after it. The error now quotes the start and the end, 600 bytes at most, cut at whole characters.
- **The check's `crew admin` in a microVM** (16.4 step 3). The security review of the first gate's fix (`docs/reviews/2026-10-21-security-gate-dns.md`) found that "Try the state" still ran the release's own `crew admin restore` and `migrate` on runc, through a service of the installed Compose file, with default capabilities, three of the check's secrets, and the whole folder of snapshots, every kept release's full dump of the database. They are code agents wrote, so the updater now writes their spec itself, as it writes the check server's, and runs each command in a microVM of its own, `crew-gate-restore`, with no fallback: the release's server image by its id, the user crew, no capability and no way to gain one, bounds of its own and 30 minutes a command, the scratch network alone with the database's address as a hosts entry, the migrate role's secret alone (both commands connect as that role), and only the snapshot it reads, read only. The service `crew-admin-check` is gone from the Compose file; only `crew-ctl install` writes that file, with the updater, so an updater never meets a Compose file of another version. The install's own `migrate`, `restore`, and `backup` on the real database are unchanged: they run the release the operator installs, or the running one. The operator chose this (2026-10-21). From the reviews of the change (`docs/reviews/2026-10-21-review-gate-restore.md`, `docs/reviews/2026-10-21-security-gate-restore.md`): the snapshots' folder is crew's, and Docker, which is root, follows a link in a mount's source, so the updater opens the snapshot without following a link, refuses what is no plain file, and mounts a copy of the file it opened, made in the check's own folder, root's, as crew's 0600; the microVM's root is read only, with a tmpfs of 64 MB, and a release whose server image declares a volume is refused, since Docker would make one on the machine's disk, so the microVM writes nothing of its own there; the snapshot must be crew's, with one name, since root copies it; the updater keeps at most 1 MiB of a command's output from its start and as much from its end; and `crew-ctl` shows what a step said and a request's error as one line without control or format characters, since they can hold output of code agents wrote, and a line of them could otherwise pass for one of its own. Two bounds are open: what the release's `crew admin` writes into the scratch database reaches the machine's disk, in the check's database folder, with no bound; and the check server's spec has no bound on the disk either.
- **The browser tests' results** (16.4 step 4). The gate of 0.2.14 on dave, the first on an updater with the restore in microVMs (0.2.13, installed by hand), passed "Try the state", Check, the smoke run, and the selftest, and failed at the browser tests: "EROFS: read-only file system, rmdir '/work/test-results'". Playwright removes its results' folder as it starts, and that folder was the tmpfs mounted inside the tests' read-only folder, which can be neither removed nor made again. The updater now names the results' folder itself, with `--output`, in a tmpfs of 256 MB of its own outside the tests' folder (`/tmp/gate`); the release's gate configuration also writes outside it (`outputDir`), so the gate of a release with it passes on an updater from before. The error that said so came whole, from the start of what the command printed.
- **The first gate that passed on dave** (16.2 step 5). The gate of 0.2.15, on updater 0.2.13, passed every updater step; its runtime inputs equal the running release's, so its live part ran at once once Crew's project was made (L2): the real ticket GATE-1 went through analysis, In progress, Review (on the other family), and Test to Done on its checks, for $1.13, and the eval suites did not run, as nothing the agents see changed. The release waits for Install. Then `crew-gate`, Done with its one ticket, ran the lead's retrospective and final report, outside the gate's budget and report (about $0.60): that was open, and is closed below.
- **The pin of `gh`** (12.8). The install by hand of 0.2.12 on dave (2026-10-21) failed at the runtime image: GitHub CLI's package source keeps only its newest version, and 2.102.0, which `versions.lock` named, was gone for 2.102.1. Docker's and Google's sources keep their older versions. So the `gh` pin breaks every build, by hand or by Crew, from the day `gh` releases until the pin moves. The daily look of 12.8 does not read the package sources of the runtime image's tools yet, so until it does the pin is moved by hand; that is open, with the daily look (M16's sixth phase). The pin is 2.102.1. The same install showed that `crew-ctl install --build` writes the deploy files before the build, so a build that fails leaves the machine with the new release's `compose.yaml`, `versions.lock`, and `crew-ctl` beside the running release's updater, whose "Try the state" then names a service that is gone; the next install that succeeds puts them right. That order was open, and is changed below.
- **The browser tests' microVM bounded** (16.4 step 4). The security review of the gate's browser fix (`docs/reviews/2026-10-21-security-gate-browser2.md`) found the browser tests' microVM with a writable root, no bound, the image's root user, and its default capabilities, while the restore's and the check server's had theirs. It now runs as the user crew, with no capability, no way to gain one, 4096 processes, 4 GB, and a read-only root with one tmpfs of 512 MB, `/tmp`, for the tests' home, Playwright's cache, and the results; the tests' folder is readable to crew, and still read only. A probe on Kata ran Playwright's own command so, with 0.2.15's packages: Chromium started and loaded a page, and a write outside `/tmp` failed. The operator chose this (2026-10-21). It runs in a gate only once an updater with it is installed.
- **`crew-gate` without a retrospective or a final report** (7.2, 9.8). After the gate of 0.2.15 on dave, `crew-gate`, Done with its one ticket, ran the lead's retrospective ($0.38) and final report ($0.20), outside the gate's budget and report, and the retrospective proposed an eval case there, whose fit check ran too. A hidden project now gets neither, as it is in no number of Analytics and no signal of the improver. The operator chose this (2026-10-21). It holds on a server that runs a release with it, so a gate's real ticket on a server before it still starts both.
- **Install puts the deploy files in place after the images** (16.11 step 8). The install by hand of 0.2.12 on dave failed at the runtime image and left the checkout's deploy files, its Compose file among them, beside the running 0.2.10's updater, which read a service that was gone until the next install. `crew-ctl install` now copies `crew-ctl` and its files after the images are built or loaded. The operator chose this (2026-10-21).
- **From the reviews of these three** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-21-review-s8-fixes.md`, `docs/reviews/2026-10-21-security-s8-fixes.md`; 9.8, 16.4 step 4, 16.11 step 8). The smoke run and the browser tests refuse a runtime that is no microVM's themselves, as the check server's spec and the restore do: the check server's refusal held only because the gate starts it first. A hidden project's housekeeping stays, and 9.8 says why. Step 8 says what a failed build leaves: the deploy files, not the install file.

### From 0.50 to 0.51

M16's questions, after L1 on dave measured a result (suite 4, 2026-10-20). The operator chose the recommendation of every question; a review of the first draft (`docs/reviews/2026-10-20-review-design-051.md`) found that two of them broke the rules that keep the operator safe, and the operator chose again.

- **Crew's project** (16.2): one button on the Crew level imports `updates.source_repo` and sets `improvements.crew_project`; one such project, whose remote must be the source repository. The operator's coding agent keeps building on `main` until M20, and Crew's project takes that as work from outside.
- **Build once, in a check sandbox** (16.2 step 4, 13.1, 13.4, 13.6, 16.8): the server exports the commit and builds in a check sandbox it starts as every other, in the new network mode Sources (`sandbox.sources`), which has its own Docker daemon and nothing of the machine's, driven by the running release's `deploy/release.sh` from its deploy archive, never the candidate's; the updater only checks and loads the images. The round-2 review found the first fix had the updater start a sandbox, against 13.3 and 13.10. This says exactly where "a sandbox with no agent in it" is, and it keeps the candidate's code, which agents wrote, away from the Docker socket until Install. The first draft had the updater build with the socket.
- **What starts a release** (16.2 step 3, 10.5): Build a release on Versions, or `updates.release_after_tickets` merged tickets; one build at a time, none when `main` has not moved; the build stops before a second improvement's merge; the next patch number unless the button's field names another.
- **The gate** (16.2 step 5, 16.4, 16.8): the server drives it, asks the updater for the check mode and the smoke microVM, runs the two live steps, and sends their result to the updater, which writes the one `gate.json`; a gate without that result is pending, never passed. The updater writes the candidate's check-mode spec itself, with no Docker socket, never from the candidate's Compose file.
- **The gate's real ticket**: one fixed ticket on the hidden fixture project `crew-gate`, a small Go function and its test, through In progress, Review, and Test, on the running server in the candidate's runtime image, with a budget of $2 and 30 turns a run; it passes when the board moves it to Done on its checks. Signed out, or six hours at a limit, the step and the suites are skipped with the reason, the gate passes with the note, and such a release never installs by itself, whatever 16.3 says of its kind.
- **Eval suites in the gate**: only when the release changes what agents see; every project's active cases once, in the candidate's image, compared with its last suite by the override's rule; worse holds the release for Install; queued in the `evals` class and stopped at a limit as any run. L1 cost $6.34 for one case at one repeat, because every failed check round went back to the builder, so the cost goes on the gate's report and `knowledge.evals.budget_usd` caps each project.
- **When the login enters a candidate image**: at once when `release.json`'s inputs of the runtime image (`image/`'s context, `versions.lock`, the Go files `cmd/crew-run` depends on) equal the running release's, since only text differs; otherwise after Run the live gate, which shows the diff first, and Install is a second press on a passed gate. The first draft gave the login to the candidate's image before the operator had seen its code.
- **The daily look** (12.8): the server's timer reads the sources, which needs no secret; `agy` is tried in a check sandbox from the running runtime image; one keyed ticket per new version. `image/bump-pins.sh` goes.
- **Every release measured** (16.8): on Versions, as news; only an improvement is taken out by Crew.
- **The candidate on Versions** (16.2 step 6, 16.9, section 20, the routes): the tickets in it, the operator's commits as "from outside", the gate's report, the kind, the protected paths, the build's log, and `release.json`; the notes come from the tickets and each line is screened as a title is.
- **The machine's `crew-ctl`** (16.11): an update replaces the containers, not `crew-ctl` and its files. `crew-ctl` and Versions compare them with the running release's deploy archive and give the one command to run as root, `install --deploy-files` of the release's own `crew-ctl`; the update is not held.
- **A case that measures guessing** (9.6): L1's case 0001 met no goal in four check rounds because its held-out test from WC-8 needs a type `Counts`, a constant `bufferSize`, and the counts returned with a read error, none of which its text states; the builder spent its rounds guessing names. A proposed case now gets a fit check by a judge on the other family than the project's builder, and is `waiting` with its list until the operator accepts it or changes its text. The output of a failed held-out check is kept for the operator, redacted and bounded. Case 0001 is made again with the three facts in its text.
- **An ended eval stops its runs** (9.6): an eval run that ends, or a suite that stops, stops its runs still going. This lifts the known limit of 0.50 that left such a run to its time limit. A ticket's check at a limit keeps 0.50's rule and waits; the first draft's clause about its dropped read error is left out, as the security review of M15 accepted.

### From 0.49 to 0.50

M15 on dave, after 0.2.1. The operator chose the recommendations of the report's items 1, 3 and 4.

- **An eval's reviewer reads its branch with tools** (9.6). It works as a ticket's reviewer does: read-only on `eval/<run>`, with tools, with no Crew MCP, and with the instructions of the case's base commit, as the tester. It is given the diff, has a ticket's run time (`limits.run_timeout_minutes`, no longer a judge's ten minutes), and answers `VERDICT: approve` or `VERDICT: changes`. A reviewer whose run ends, at its time, its turns, or its budget, before it gives a verdict measured nothing, like a run Crew ended; one that finishes its turn with no verdict still asks for no changes (security review of 0.50). This replaces 0.49's "the reviewer is one call with no tools". On dave, a reviewer started as a judge followed the reviewer's own instructions, which tell it to inspect the branch, and agy refused the tool, so the case measured nothing.
- **The generic check and the paths every project has** (10.5, step 2). A path a cited ticket changed refuses a patch that names it, unless the baseline's own text names the same path whole, and the patch names it only whole: today `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `GEMINI.md`, `.mcp.json`, `README.md`, `docs/architecture.md`, and `docs/setup.md`. A longer path that holds one, such as `services/payments/AGENTS.md`, still counts, and so does a file below `.agents/` or `.github/workflows/`, folders the baseline names, since its name below the folder is the project's (review of 0.50). Runs of ten words shared with the file still refuse. In M15 the check refused a patch that named `AGENTS.md`, because a cited ticket had changed it.

### From 0.48 to 0.49

M15, analytics, evals, and the Crew level. The operator approved its plan and its seventeen questions with the recommendations ("Go all approved").

- **An improvement before Crew's own project** (10.5, 16.2): an improvement that passed its evals becomes a ticket on the project that `improvements.crew_project` names. Until M16 makes that project, the setting is empty and a passed improvement waits as `passed`, with one inbox item. Applying (step 5's end), measuring (step 6), and taking out (step 7) are built and tested in M15 against the releases of M14: a passed improvement is applied once a release that holds its ticket's merge is installed, which from M16 is a release Crew builds from its own project; a worse result rolls back through the updater when the release that carried the improvement is the running one and nothing was installed since; otherwise it becomes a revert ticket on Crew's project. News goes out either way.
- **The improver's scope and token** (10.4, 11.5): its runs have the subtask scope `improver`, with no project and no draft. Its MCP token has a scope of its own, `crew`, made only when the improver starts. A project's token cannot call a Crew-level tool, and the improver's token cannot call a project's tool.
- **The improver is always Model only** (10.4, 13.6): `improvements.improver_network` is taken out. There is no choice.
- **What a patch may touch** (10.3, 10.4): a patch gives whole new texts of files, never a diff. It may touch the `.md` files of `baseline/agents/`, `baseline/stages/`, `baseline/guides/`, and `baseline/templates/`; a change of the harness waits until the evals can measure one. It may not touch the files of the roles that check, `baseline/agents/{reviewer,tester,e2e,verifier,judge}.md` and `baseline/stages/{review,test,e2e,verification,judge}.md`, nor the model or effort lines of any agent file. The prompt writer's files, `baseline/agents/prompt-writer.md` and `baseline/stages/{rewrite,review-text}.md`, wait for the operator's OK (7.2). The pipeline defaults are in `internal/settings/`, which is protected (16.8), so a patch of them is refused until they move into the baseline. 10.3 shows the baseline as it is.
- **The generic check** (10.5, step 2): it refuses a patch that holds any project's name, ticket prefix or ticket number, repository name or address, or a path changed by a ticket its evidence cites, or that shares a run of 10 words with the summaries, records, and memory entries of any project or with the files the cited tickets changed.
- **Overrides** (9.6, 10.3): one row per project and path, holding the whole file, with versions. The lead proposes one with `propose_change` (in housekeeping and the retrospective), and the operator can write one. One of a checking role's files or of the prompt writer's waits for the operator. The rest go to the project's eval suite: not worse applies it and tells the operator, with the result beside the one before. Too few cases leave it in `testing`, and the operator can apply it by hand. It is measured over the project's next 20 tickets, and a worse result takes it back, with news.
- **An eval case** (9.6): `base_commit` is the first parent of the ticket's merge commit. The held-out files are test files the ticket added or changed, named in the case and checked against the tree of the merge. Its checks are the ticket's command checks; a `judge:` check is left out, to keep the runs few. Cases come from the lead (in the retrospective or housekeeping, `propose_eval_case`), from the operator on a Done ticket, and from a fixed bug, through its fix ticket.
- **An eval run** (9.6): its runs have the scope `eval` and no ticket. Its branch `eval/<run>` is in the quarantine repository only and is never pushed to a remote. The builder, then the reviewer on the other model family, then the tester, each in its own sandbox. At most one suite per project and one on the machine at a time. None starts while the project is paused, and a suite stops at `knowledge.evals.budget_usd`. Its cost is in the project's spend and in no ticket's budget.
- **Not worse** (10.5): one function. The margin is the spread between the repeats of the baseline: the highest share of passed cases in one repeat less the lowest. With one repeat it is zero.
- **A ticket's version** (10.5, step 6): a ticket counts on a version when every run of it used that version. A ticket whose runs used two versions counts on neither and is shown as mixed. The metric an improvement names is one of a fixed list, each with the direction that is better. The first 20 tickets after it are compared with the last 20 before. With fewer, or with another change in the same release that could explain the result, it is inconclusive.
- **The retrospective** (9.8, 6.6): it starts when a part of the plan is complete, that is every ticket of the part is Done or cancelled and at least one is Done, and when the project is Done, before the final report. It is a lead run in the class of housekeeping and shares its weekly budget. The tag of 6.6 is made at the same moments: `part-<name>` on the part's last merge, and `done`.
- **Operator intervention** (10.2): Crew records a breakdown sent back, a ticket edited before its OK, a new feature discarded, a decision changed, a project limit raised, and a merge reverted, beside work merged outside Crew. Asking about a ticket is recorded with the chat, in M20.
- **Analytics** (9.7, 10.7): computed in SQL when read, and kept in `metrics` for five minutes. The groups of 10.7 that the pictures do not show, cost, the projects side by side, and prompts, are sections of the Signals tab. Models is the Models screen (12.3), which already says who chose.
- **A change to the prompt writer's rules** (7.2): built with the three examples, old rules and new, made by six calls of the prompt writer.
- **What building M15 settled.** An override names no metric of its own: it is measured by the share of tickets whose checks passed on the first request, over the project's first 20 tickets every run of which started after it was applied, against the last 20 every run of which started before. The margin is the spread its evals measured; an override with no evals, such as the operator's own, has one ticket's share, 1/20. A run's prompt names the override it read in the part's source (`override o_N vM of baseline/<path>@<version>`); when the override cannot be read the run gets the baseline's own and its source says so. The lead's `propose_change` and the operator's own override are the two ways one is written; a newer proposal for the same file withdraws one not yet decided.
- **From the reviews of M15** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-19-review-m15.md`, `docs/reviews/2026-10-19-security-m15.md`). Only a change the evals can measure goes to them without the operator: the builder's stage and agent files, `stages/in_progress.md` and `agents/builder.md`, since an eval runs only the builder, the reviewer, and the tester, and the other two check. A change of any other file, in an override or an improvement, waits for the operator, and the E2E tester's files count as a role that checks (AGENTS.md rule 15). An eval run that Crew ended for its own reason, a restart, its sandbox, a sign-in, a limit, the operator, the setup, a model, or a CLI that refused, measured nothing. A failed check records its round before its builder starts, in the state `fixing`, so the checks are not run again while the builder waits. A suite of a paused or archived project stops, and so does one whose override is no longer in testing; an eval run a stopped suite ended keeps that end. The generic check refuses a patch when a cited ticket's files cannot be read, and the improver's proposal and note are screened for secrets. A worse improvement counts as measured only once it is taken out, so a take-out that fails is tried again; taking out a passed one closes its ticket on Crew's project. The ticket is the improver's, analysed like an agent's, and its note is the improver's. An eval run has its project's network mode: the held-out tests and the merged work are kept from it on Crew's side only, so a project whose repository is public can be read from the internet. Housekeeping's weekly budget counts the retrospectives too. The inbox opens an override that waits, rather than applying it in one click. A known limit: the cases the lead proposes are active at once and count toward the cases that let an override of the builder's files apply by itself. Another: a suite that stops leaves a check sandbox that runs already to its time limit, though what it finds changes nothing.
- **What the live checks of M15 on dave found.** A run of an eval reads its branch only in its workspace too: crew-run fetches no `origin/main` for it; its builder keeps its branch's instructions, which start at the case's base commit, and its tester takes them from that commit, as a judge takes main's (13.8, R170) (the first eval run on dave ended at once on the refused fetch of main). A run whose workspace Crew could not make measured nothing, like one Crew ended. The server's image copies the module's root package, `improver.go` and `improver.md`. From the review after the live checks (`docs/reviews/2026-10-19-review-review-m15-postverify.md`): a run of an eval that a restarted server follows again keeps its branch only and its commit of instructions; a run of an eval whose case Crew cannot find does not start; and a reviewer's or a tester's run that Crew ended for its own reason, or whose workspace it could not make, measured nothing, like a builder's. The second round (`docs/reviews/2026-10-19-review-review-m15-postverify-round2.md`): the endpoint lets a run rewrite its own branch, so a builder's head whose history no longer holds the base commit ends its eval run with the goal not met, before the reviewer and the tester start.
- **What L1 on 0.2.2 found** (9.6, 12.7). An eval's reviewer that stopped at the Claude quota moved by itself to its next choice, Gemini 4 Argon, which was the builder's model, twice, after its eval run had already ended: the rule of 12.7 that keeps a check off the builder's family was kept only for a ticket's subtasks, and nothing ended an eval's subtask that waited. Both now hold for an eval: its reviewer at a limit moves only to another family than its builder's, and an eval run that ends, or whose suite stops, ends its subtasks that wait or stopped. A run that has no ticket keeps every rule a ticket's run keeps.
- **A suite that measured nothing, and a release's title** (9.6, 9.7, 16.10; the operator's answers to the report's items 3 and 4). A suite that ended having measured no case, because each of its runs ended for Crew's own reason, shows "nothing measured" on the Evals tab with the why of its last such run, or why it stopped; Analytics leaves it out of the share of goals met and counts it beside: on dave, two such suites read as "Goals met 0%". `crew-ctl build --title TEXT` gives a release one line of at most 120 characters, with no control character, as the heading of its notes, `# Crew X.Y.Z: TEXT`; Versions and the Baseline's card read it, and a release without one keeps the first line of what changed. An improvement the release carried still names its version on the Baseline's card first. M16 writes the notes from the release's tickets.
- **From the reviews of these fixes** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-19-review-evals-atlimit-title.md`, `docs/reviews/2026-10-19-security-m15-title-evals.md`). An eval's check at a limit whose builder's model Crew cannot read waits, under the rule for checks, rather than choose with no family to avoid. The subtasks of an eval run that has ended are ended on every round of the evals too, so one an error left, or one from before, ends as well. The Evals tab says why a suite measured nothing before the decision of an override's or an improvement's suite, which on no case says only that there are too few. A title is checked in characters, and refuses control, invisible, and direction-changing characters; the server takes the same out of any title it reads from the notes, and cuts it at 120 characters. A known limit: an eval run that ends while one of its runs still goes leaves that run to its time limit.
- **What 0.2.3 on dave showed** (9.6, 10.2, 12.7; the operator's answers to the fourth report's items 2 to 5). The Eval result signal of a suite that measured no case says so and why, "The eval suite of wordcount measured nothing: …", with the same why as the Evals tab, not "0 cases, 0% of the runs met their goal", which the improver had read as a result. The two signals written before stay as they were, and so does the 0% of suite 1 from 0.2.0: stored results are a record of what that version measured. A share of no ticket reads "—", not 0%: the Crew level's "Checks passed on the first request" says "no ticket has run on Crew 0.2.4 yet" until one has, and a project's Analytics shows "Passed first time" and the first-request share the same way; on dave it read "0%, on Crew 0.2.3, 0 tickets". A ticket's reviewer, verifier, or judge at a limit whose builder's model Crew cannot read, because no build ran or none recorded its model, waits, as an eval's check has since 0.2.3, rather than choose with no family to avoid; a role that checks nothing of the builder's, such as the tester or the analyst, still moves before a build as after, on a ticket and in an eval alike, and with `agents.checks_use_another_family` off no check waits for a family it need not avoid (review of these fixes, `docs/reviews/2026-10-19-review-024-fixes.md`; 0.2.3's text said an eval's tester kept off the builder's family too, which it never did). A suite needs at least one case to decide, whatever `knowledge.evals.min_cases` says, so one that measured nothing is always too few.
- **What 0.2.4 on dave showed** (10.7; the operator's answers to the fifth report's items 2 and 3). Latest signals on the Crew level leaves out `sandbox_startup`: one is recorded with every run, and 21 of its 30 lines were "Sandbox start-up", which Analytics already shows as an average. The table of signals by kind and project gets a column "No project", shown only when a signal has none, because the three choices of a rewrite belong to plans and counted 3 under All and 0 in every column. The same rows no longer count the fixture or an archived project, which have no column either, so every row adds up to All. The headline count of interventions and its bars leave out the fixture as well, as the finished tickets it is divided by always did (review of these fixes, `docs/reviews/2026-10-19-review-025-fixes.md`); an archived project's interventions stay in it, with its finished tickets. The numbers kept in `metrics` are kept under the version that computed them: the gate's copy of the database held numbers the running version had computed, younger than five minutes, which the release in the gate would have shown as its own (the same review).
- **What 0.2.5 on dave showed** (10.7; the operator's answer to the sixth report's item 2). With the start-ups gone, 23 of the 30 lines of Latest signals had no text of their own, so What repeated the signal's name. Latest signals leaves out `cost_and_time` as well, a measurement the Cost and time card shows. The other kinds say what their detail holds (`analytics.What`), and an open choice an agent decided is told by its decision's question and choice, each cut to one plain line of at most 200 characters, because they are an agent's words. A test fails when a kind on the list has no recorded detail in it or nothing to say; the review of the fix found `feedback` had none (`docs/reviews/2026-10-19-review-026-fixes.md`).
- **What 0.2.6 on dave showed** (10.7; the operator's answers to the seventh report's items 2 and 3). A fix round's line in Latest signals was its stage's one word, such as "merge". Its signal records a text, what sent the work back and the first line of what was found, with a record's headings and lines of markers only skipped (the reviews of the fix), such as "The merge with main failed: The ticket's branch conflicts with main in: a.txt", the finding cut to one plain line of at most 200 characters (`analytics.FixRound`). A fix round recorded before that says what sent the work back alone. The improver's note filled ten lines of the table and an open choice about two: a line of Latest signals shows at most 200 characters of What, with the whole text on hover, and the API keeps it whole for the improver.
- **How an eval runs, as built in M15** (9.6). One suite runs on the machine at a time, and in it one eval run at a time, so evals never take more than one run of the subscription at once. A run of an eval gets no Crew MCP, not main's skills and context, and reads only its own branch and its work in progress through the git endpoint: main and the summaries hold the work it replays. The builder works and pushes; the reviewer is one call with no tools, given the diff, and answers `VERDICT: approve` or `VERDICT: changes`; the tester reads the branch and answers `RESULT: pass` or `RESULT: fail`. Then Crew's checks run with the held-out files copied in. A review that asks for changes is a fix round, a failed check a check round, both within `limits.max_fix_rounds`, and each fixed head is reviewed and tested again. The builder is told which commands failed, not their output, which would show the held-out tests. A run that fails for Crew's own reasons measured nothing and counts in no result. A suite stopped at its budget says too few to tell. An override in testing gets its suite by itself; a project with no case decides at once that there are too few.
- **The retrospective, as built in M15** (9.8). A ticket closed as cancelled or as discarded both count as ended for a part. A part completed by the project's last ticket is read by the Done project's retrospective, which reads every ticket: it gets its tag and no run of its own. A Done project's retrospective runs before the part retrospectives that wait, and the final report waits for it; when housekeeping's weekly budget has no room left it is skipped, so the report does not wait a week, while a part's waits for room. Its stage prompt is `baseline/stages/retrospective.md`. The report's facts list the lessons the project's retrospectives wrote, and its `learned` section starts from them. A tag goes to the remote with the next push.
- **The improver and its improvements, as built in M15** (10.4, 10.5). `improver.md` is at the root of the repository with a Go file that embeds it, and both, with `internal/improve/`, are on the list of protected parts (16.8). The improver's token is the Crew level's when its run's subtask has the scope `improver`, its role is the improver, and it names no project and no draft; such a caller reaches the tools of the scope `crew` and the three a project's run shares with it under the same name, `get_metrics`, `search_summaries`, and `get_summary`, and nothing else. A patch changes at most five files of the baseline; one of the harness is refused, since the evals give the agents their prompts and cannot carry a change of the harness, and the harness holds what the roles that check read as well (security review of M15). The change side of an improvement's suite reads the patch by the file's path in the baseline, where the project has no override of its own of that file. The generic check reads only the lines a patch adds; a name shorter than three letters is no name for it. The evals of an improvement need ten cases across all projects, the default of `knowledge.evals.min_cases`, and use a cost margin of 20%. The ticket on Crew's project holds the change as Crew's note and checks each file by its sha256; it holds none of the evidence. A release that changed code or a protected part beside the improvement makes its result inconclusive. A change of the prompt writer's rules is measured by the share of first messages whose rewrite the operator used as it was, over ten plans on each side; its examples are made on the drafts of the operator's last three first messages. The operator's "Run the improver now" is kept in `crew_state.improver_asked`. Crew's own revert of an improvement is no operator intervention.

### From 0.47 to 0.48

M14, versions in the app. The operator approved its plan and its fifteen questions with the recommendations ("Go all approved").

- **Build and gate in M14** (16.2, 16.10, 16.11): `crew-ctl build --version X.Y.Z` makes the release folder on the machine from a clean worktree at a commit, and `crew-ctl gate X.Y.Z` runs the gate on it and writes `gate.json`, bound to the hash of `release.json`. The updater refuses a folder without a passed gate, except a build by hand. From M16 the server builds and gates by itself.
- **The gate of M14** (16.2): `make check` at the release's commit, then the built images in check mode on a scratch database: health, `crew admin selftest` with the fake harness on the fixture project, the proof event, and the browser tests of the screens that only read. No CLI and no subscription. The install test runs when `updates.install_test` is `local`, and the release says when it was skipped.
- **The updater** (16.4): a Go program in its own image that runs Docker Compose with the installed deploy files and reads `/etc/crew`. It runs as root in its container, since the Docker socket gives that anyway. It speaks HTTP with JSON on `/run/crew/updater.sock` (0660, root and the group `crew`): install a version, its status, and stop. `crew-ctl` asks it with `curl --unix-socket`.
- **What an older version reads** (16.6): each schema step says in its header whether the version before it reads the result (`-- older versions read: yes|no`). `release.json` names, as `readBy`, the lowest schema whose server still reads the database the release leaves: the last step that says `no`. A roll back to a release below it restores that release's snapshot, after the operator types the version's name. At the switch every new step applies in one transaction (`crew admin migrate --at-once`).
- **Which kind a release is** (16.3): `release.json` carries the files of the commit, each path with its git hash, and the model line of each agent file. The running version compares them with its own by a list of protected paths in its source.
- **Healthy in the watch** (16.4, 16.8): the health answer, which reads and writes the database, every 30 seconds, and one proof event at the start of the watch.
- **The daily backup** (16.9): `crew-<UTC time>.tar.gz` holds `crew.sql` without the event partitions of ended months, `repos/` of the projects without a remote, and `hosted/` (since 0.73, each hosted project's `data/` as `hosted/<project id>/data/`, never its copies or its logs; a file that changes while it is read is left out with a line in the log, and the backup goes on; the restore puts a project's data back only where its `data/` is absent or empty). Each ended month is `events-YYYY-MM.sql.gz`, written once and again without a deleted project. The plain dumps of M1 can still be restored.
- **Publishing** (16.9): the server pushes the tag with its SSH key, makes the release page and pushes the images to `ghcr.io` with the `gh` login, and tries again until it has. The notes are the commit subjects since the release before and the gate's summary until M16, when they come from tickets.
- **The Versions screen** (16.5): a release's results are the tickets Done on it and the share of them that were Done at their first request to move.
- **Numbers**: `crew-ctl build` needs `--version`. The first release is 0.1.0.
- **What building M14 settled.** The updater keeps its log as files on the machine, and the server copies each request into `update_requests`: the server stays the only writer of the database (13.1). A restore of the real database reads the snapshot into a new database, which takes the real one's name only when it is complete. The snapshot is a file of the daily kind, without the months whose files are current; `months.txt` in it names the folder of the month files. Healthy needs a database that takes writes: an update that changes no row. The check server gets no key file, but a random key of its own. The server replaces the updater's container with the new image and the old container's settings, once per version, and puts the old one back when the new one does not start. The kind of a release is decided at its notice, against the version that runs then. The first release folder an install writes has no gate: root rolls back to it with `crew-ctl rollback` as a build by hand, and the app cannot. `crew admin selftest` makes a project of its own and deletes it again, and lends agy's recorded list of models where agy has listed none. `crew-ctl selftest` runs it in a database of its own, `crew_selftest`, made for it and removed after it, so the running server's pipeline neither sees its project nor works it; the gate runs it on the check's copy. After a restore every personal token is ended; the sessions are the ingress's, and end with M17. PostgreSQL's image is saved in a release folder by its tag, since Docker keeps no digest of a saved image: a second machine (M17) takes it by its digest.
- **From the reviews of M14** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-18-review-m14.md`, `docs/reviews/2026-10-18-security-m14.md`): a restore reads a file only in psql's restricted mode, from pg_dump's `\restrict KEY` to its `\unrestrict KEY`, which is refused anywhere else, so no command runs from a backup or a month file, also after SQL on a line; a backup does not choose the folder its month files are read from. The check's database is a container of its own (16.4, step 3), and the check server refuses every request that would change something; the gate's browser tests install their packages without the secret and without install scripts, then run on the check's network alone with the check's own secret. The release folders are root's, and on the machine the updater takes only a folder that root owns and nobody else may write: a gate's verdict is believed only there. An image is named for its part and its version and by its id, and a saved image that names another tag is not loaded. `crew-ctl update` goes forward only; going back is `rollback`. The key of Docker's packages is checked by its fingerprint in `versions.lock`. Step 1 of a request is not seen half made; a request cut off after its switch starts the version before again; the server acts on an ended request once, in the transaction that copies it, and not on one that ended before it started.
- **Root at the socket** (16.11): the updater takes a caller as root when the kernel says its user id is 0. Without user namespaces that is root in any container that mounts `/run/crew` too, and the server, which holds the Docker socket, could start one. The rule that only root gives a build by hand holds against a mistake of the server, not against a server an attacker controls, which holds the machine anyway (16.3, "Why code waits").
- **`updates.auto_install: unprotected` and AGENTS.md**: AGENTS.md says without exception that a release that changes code waits for the operator; 16.3 lets the operator choose `unprotected`, under which a release of code that touches no protected part installs by itself. The default, `text`, keeps both. The operator decided (2026-10-18, "go all approved"): AGENTS.md rule 12 names the setting.
- **The copy on GitHub** (16.9): the tag is made through GitHub's API with `gh`'s login, at the commit the release was built from, and is never moved. Without `gh` nothing is copied, the Versions screen says so, and Crew tries again. The images are `ghcr.io/<owner>/<image>:<version>`.

### From 0.46 to 0.47

M13, a whole project and many projects. The operator approved its plan and its twelve questions with the recommendations ("Go all approved").

- **The project's setup in run sandboxes** (11.2, 12.4, 13.5, 21). The design said the setup runs first in every run, and no run sandbox ran it: only check sandboxes did. It now runs in the sandboxes of the builder, the tester, and the E2E tester, before the CLI starts. Two failures in a row, of runs or of check sandboxes, are checked on `main` before the fix ticket is made (schema step 48). What waits for the fix, how the fix ticket gets past a full stage, and how the waiting branches are brought up to date are in 13.5.
- **The capacity check** (7.9, 13.10): what is counted at which size, the machine read from Docker, the order for freed room, and the room a waiting large sandbox keeps. A run idle between its turns is not counted, so the checks it waits for never wait for its room.
- **Shares of a subscription** (7.9, 12.7): one new run per pass on a group that is close to its limit, to the project served least recently on it.
- **An end-to-end run that cannot start the application** (21, 14.1): Crew's own E2E check decides, by exit code 3; a setup that fails takes the way of row 14.
- **What a test file is** (7.1, 14.1), the reviewer's reason for each, and `named_tests_kept` with Review off. Since 0.90 a head is reviewed before it merges (`review_covers_head`, 7.4), and package files are listed to the reviewer (13.8).
- **`approvals.created_task: operator`** (7.3): the analysis is a proposal that waits in the inbox, for every ticket the operator did not make; calling it a new feature is a proposal too, with no clock. A personal token does not answer a proposal (11.7).
- **From the reviews of M13** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-18-review-m13.md`, `docs/reviews/2026-10-18-security-m13.md`): a check of the setup that a restart cut off holds nothing; a continue asks for room before it ends the run it replaces; deletions and test files are read from git's full list, never from cut patches; a check names a path by a word, not by a word that only ends with it; a head from GitHub that changes files that instruct agents is kept out (schema step 50); `setup` is taken from a run's home only when its sandbox ran a setup, and a stop ends a setup at once; Crew's fix tickets are made once.
- **A pull request changed or merged on GitHub** (21, schema step 49): read after every fetch with the server's token; merged, closed, moved, or gone another way. A ticket merged there is Done without Crew's own merge, and its checks run on `main` once the merge is in it.

### From 0.45 to 0.46

M12, documentation, memory, bugs, feedback, and skills. The operator approved its plan and its questions with the recommendations ("Go all approved").

- **The agents that check join the automatic choice in M12** (12.3, 23), now that bugs are recorded: the defects that escaped a reviewer, a tester, an E2E tester, or a verifier are counted, and the rules of 12.3 apply to them. A bug names the Done ticket its defect is in, `in_ticket` (9.4, schema step 46), when its reporter or the operator knows it; a defect escaped every run of an agent that checks that passed that ticket, once Analysis approved the bug's fix. Shares are over the agent's runs. A try or a comparison for an agent that checks goes to another family than the build's only.
- **Every run reads main's layer of skills and context** (12.9, 13.8): before every turn, also a builder's, unless its ticket's `may_change` names those files. Requests for the same skill are one ticket. The Settings screen lists both layers, read only.
- **A skill ticket's checks** (12.9, 21, schema step 45): `skills_vendored`, a new exit check of In progress, holds a branch that changes `.agents/skills/` until every row of its `VENDORED.md` names a source, the pin of a commit, and a licence; the reviewer of a skill ticket is told by Crew to read the skill as instructions, for a step that sends code or data out, reaches outside the project, or loosens a rule; and a change of skills is code, so it merges on the operator's Allow (13.8). A run asks for at most three skills. What main has of these files and a builder's branch does not track is kept out of its git, so its commit does not take it. The server reads the base layer's names and descriptions from `harness/` at its build.
- **The retrospective moves to M15** (9.8, 23), with the eval cases and the changes it proposes. M12 builds housekeeping.
- **Housekeeping's cadence** (9.8): one going per project, one start in 24 hours, none while paused, and its weekly budget over the last seven days. Its inputs are new feedback, an index over its cap, and a page marked since the last housekeeping started, so a page that waits for its `docs` ticket starts none. The lead's run reads main, pushes nothing, and ends when its turn ends; what it did is in memory, feedback, and the tickets it made. A page the lead reads and finds right stays marked until it changes.
- **Search and near-duplicates** (9.3): PostgreSQL full-text search with the `simple` configuration; a near-duplicate memory entry overlaps by 0.8 or more.
- **The changelog entry and decision pages are in the candidate merge commit** (9.9), before the checks on top of `main`. An operator's edit of a page is refused when the page changed since it was opened.
- **A fix's check that fails before the fix** (9.4) is the base check of Analysis (7.6): the first check that is not marked keeps and failed on `main` before any work is the bug's `regression_check`.
- **From the reviews of M12** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-18-review-m12.md`, `docs/reviews/2026-10-18-security-m12.md`): every tool that writes an agent's text into knowledge, a ticket, or a decision record refuses a text that holds a value the run's sandbox was handed or the shape of a secret, in every field; Crew's own writes into a merge never land on a file that instructs agents, whatever `docs.changelog` and `docs.root` say; who last changed a page or a skill is read from main's first parents, Crew's merges and the operator's commits, never from a message an agent wrote; `skills_vendored` fails a branch that removes `VENDORED.md`; a path pattern is matched in time linear in its length; a page indexed already is not read again, and the project's layer is read once for each main; a bug the operator closed is no escaped defect.
- **The pages Crew indexes** (9.9) are `README.md`, the changelog, and the markdown under `docs.root`, at most 1000 of 512 KB each, read at each change of `main` by the board's pass. A page's kind comes from its place; the ticket that changed it from the `Ticket:` trailer of the commit. The operator edits any page but the changelog, which is Crew's.
- **The operator's feedback** (9.5) comes from the Knowledge screen and a tick on a ticket's comment; other boxes in M17.
- **A result that keeps changing** (21) is the second fail-then-pass of the same end-to-end command in 30 days.
- **A bug follows its fix ticket** (9.4, schema step 44): in work past Analysis, fixed at Done, closed as `discarded` when Analysis discards the ticket, and moved to the first ticket that replaces a cancelled one. The operator's close is kept. Memory, bugs, feedback, the index of the pages, and the requests for a skill are the tables of the Knowledge group (15.3).

### From 0.44 to 0.45

The end of M11. Of the 88 rows of section 21, M11 found 11 with no test (`docs/testing/recovery-matrix.md`). The operator decided them ("go approved all"): the rows whose part is built get their tests in M11, and the rows whose part is not built yet move to the milestone that builds that part, which tests them.

- **M11's done-when** (23) now says that the recovery paths of section 21 are tested where their parts are built, and that each later milestone tests the rows of the parts it builds.
- **Rows moved to M12:** end-to-end tests that pass or fail at random, which are run once more in a new sandbox, and a result that keeps changing becomes a bug; bugs come with M12.
- **Rows moved to M13:** a project's setup that fails (its `fix` ticket after two failures in a row, and runs that need the setup waiting for it); an end-to-end run that cannot start the application twice in a row, which goes to the lead; a pull request merged or changed on GitHub by someone else; a builder who weakens their own test (the reviewer is shown every changed test file, and with Review off a deleted test file that a check names stops the move); and `approvals.created_task: operator`, which M4's settings accept and no part reads yet.
- **Rows moved to M17:** two microVMs in a row that do not start, which hold new runs and put the Kata and KVM check in the inbox; and the inbox item once the database can be written again. A project's budgets in its settings, now whole dollars, take cents when the settings get their screen.
- **The key that encrypts secrets is lost** (21, 12.6): a credential sealed with another key file reads as not set up everywhere, so Setup opens, shows it as one to set up again, and says why; the CLIs read as signed out; and a sandbox starts with what can be read.
- **GitHub gives no token for a sandbox** (21): the signal is `no_github_token`, with the sandbox, its project, and its run, once for each sandbox, recorded when the sandbox asks for its credentials. A sandbox's token sealed with a lost key is forgotten when the sandbox goes.
- **The install stops half way** (21, 16.11): `doctor` says what is wrong with an installation it finds, an install that stopped before it finished or services that do not run, and compares the installation's data folder with the answers. The install test has `PHASE=halfway`.

### From 0.43 to 0.44

M11, limits, and tickets that cannot get through. The operator approved its plan and its questions with the recommendations ("Go all approved"); the cold read of the plan (`docs/reviews/2026-10-16-m11-plan-cold-read.md`) settled the rest.

- **Prices for the estimate** (12.7, 14.1, schema step 36). The machine setting `prices` lists patterns of model names with dollars a million tokens in, out, and from the cache, at the vendors' standard list prices (not their launch prices): Claude Opus 4/20/0.20, Claude Sonnet 2/10/0.20, Gemini 4 4/20/0.20, Gemini Flash 1.50/7.50/0.15, GPT-OSS 0.15/0.60/0.15. A model no pattern names counts at the highest. A run records how its cost was found, and runs from before 0.44 are estimated once from their tokens in and out.
- **A run's budget is watched during its turn** (7.7, 8.2), from the usage of each step `agy` reports. The watchdog reads a project's own numbers; the usual time of a stage is the median of the project's past stays there, from three on; a ticket past its budget is recorded once, with its row in changes, as news.
- **A project's limits** (7.7): what counts as work added by agents, the reason in words, the backend's resume when no limit is passed, and Resume refused while one is.
- **Looking again** is a subtask of its own (schema step 37), started by any of the five ways a ticket cannot get through; `new_approach` supersedes the stopped subtasks, and one builder carries the approach out in the ticket's stage, as the ticket never goes back; replacements have origin `agent` with `replaces`, and a ticket that depended on the cancelled one depends on them (7.7). Only the board's move writes a ticket's stage, which a test holds.
- **Pause all and Stop all** (7.9, schema step 38): one row, `crew_state`, asked by every start of a run or a turn and by each pass of the pipeline, which then acts only on the turns of runs that are going; a row that cannot be read holds. A running run's own goal loop may finish it under Pause all, not under Stop all. Stop all's subtasks wait for Resume, which continues them without counting them as Crew's own continues, those of a paused project or a ticket on hold only once that ends (since 0.63); notice clocks and Crew's changes of a model wait, and Resume moves the clocks on by the pause. `crew-ctl pause | resume` write the same row through `crew admin`. The Projects screen has the switches, and the top bar says while Crew is held.
- **Revert, amendment, and delete** (7.13, 15.4, schema step 41): a revert is a ticket of the kind `revert` that names its merge and links `reverts`; its checks are the Done ticket's command checks turned round, `! sh -c '…'`, and those marked keeps, and the backend makes its commit with `git merge-tree` over the merge on main as it is. An amendment's tickets carry it and get the OK through the same inbox item and `POST /projects/{p}/ok` as the plan's; the OK is a row of the plan of its own (`amendment_ok`), and a project that was Done runs again. Delete archives first, stops the runs and waits a minute for them, refuses while a sandbox of the project runs, and removes the rows in one transaction through `crew_delete_project`, which the server's role may call; the repositories go after. `DELETE /projects/{p}` takes `{name}` and only deletes; archive is `POST /projects/{p}/archive`. The task view has Revert, the Plan screen Amend the plan, and Settings Archive, Export, and Delete.
- **The disk guard** (16.10, schema step 39): what it measures and removes at 85%, its stop at 95% in the row of Pause all with its inbox item, and the readings behind the Database screen's months of room.
- **The final report** (7.2, schema step 40): due from state, written by the lead with `write_report` beside Crew's facts, kept when the lead cannot write it, and shown on the Plan screen.
- **The Tickets screen** (20.1, tickets.png): every ticket of a project with its filters by stage, kind, and origin, from `GET /projects/{p}/tickets`.
- **From the reviews of M11** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-m11.md`, `docs/reviews/2026-10-16-security-m11.md`, `docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-m11-round2.md`; schema steps 42 and 43). Only the breakdown run of an open amendment writes its breakdown and proposes tickets, and the amendment's OK moves only its own tickets; one amendment is open at a time, checked under the project's lock. The database's delete function refuses by itself the fixture, a project that is not archived, and one whose runs still go, and `deleted_projects` can only be added to. A delete refuses before it archives anything, says a repository it could not remove, and the server removes such a repository when it starts. A revert's check is the Done ticket's turned round as one quoted word, `! sh -c '…'`. A replacement waits for what the cancelled ticket waited for, and one whose ticket spent its whole budget takes the project's default. Stop all looks for runs twice; token counts below 0 count as 0; text agents wrote is labelled so where Crew passes it on.
- **Housekeeping moves to M12** (9.8, 23), which brings the feedback, memory, and documentation it works on. M11 builds the final report.

### From 0.42 to 0.43

M10, network modes, optional stages, `judge:` checks, and pull requests. The operator approved its plan and its questions with the recommendations ("go approved 1-16").

- **The cost of a run of `agy` is estimated, in M11** (the operator's answer to Q1 of M10). `agy` gives tokens and no cost, so a budget in dollars never trips. M11, which records passed budgets, estimates a run's cost from its tokens in and out and a price per pattern of model names that the machine's settings keep (such as `gemini-*-flash*` and `claude-opus*`, in dollars a million tokens), filled from the baseline; a model no pattern names counts at the highest price, and the Models screen says so. The app shows such a cost as estimated. Budgets stay in dollars.

- **Model only works** (13.6, schema step 29). A sandbox's mode is fixed when it starts and recorded in `sandboxes.mode`: the ticket's mode, else the project's `sandbox.network`, else the machine's `network.mode`. A judge, a summary writer, a prompt writer, a login check, and the improver are Model only whatever is asked, and Setup has the internet. A Model only sandbox's bridge is `mdl-…`; its one resolver is the server's address, where nothing answers, and `HTTPS_PROXY` is the server's door. Before a Model only sandbox is used, a start-up test from inside it tries the internet and the machine; one that connects removes the sandbox, so no sandbox runs while the rules are missing. `network.model_only_hosts` holds agy's six hosts from the spike. The proxy is the server's door: port 443 of a listed host by name, from a known sandbox, to an address on the internet only; a refused host is logged. The firewall's `CREW-MDL` chain lets an `mdl-` bridge reach its own bridge and nothing routed, and `CREW-IN` takes the machine from it, as for `sdb-`. The run view shows the mode its sandbox had.
- **Spike item 4 answered: Model only works with `agy`** (13.6). Live, on dave: runs in Model only reached the model, and the agent's own request to another host was refused by the proxy. Taken off the list one group at a time, `agy` failed without `lh3.googleusercontent.com` and without `www.googleapis.com` (its check before a turn reads the account's picture and userinfo), and worked without `antigravity-unleash.goog` and `play.googleapis.com`. `network.model_only_hosts` now holds five hosts, without the flags and the logs.
- **The server never goes out through a sandbox's bridge** (13.6, found on dave when Model only was first installed). The server joins each sandbox's network as its door, with the lowest priority for the default route: Docker had given it the route of the network first by name, a sandbox's, so its own traffic left through the newest sandbox's bridge, which an `mdl-` bridge drops. The login check then could not renew agy's login, and said it was signed out. `network.model_only_hosts` adds `oauth2.googleapis.com`, where the login is renewed. The proxy tries IPv4 first and gives each address 5 seconds.
