- **`judge:` checks run** (7.1, 13.9, schema step 30). The lead's tools and the breakdown may write a check of the kind `judge`, one line, at most ten a ticket, never `keeps`. After the command checks pass on a head, one judge (the role `judge`, another family than the builder, which now also counts a builder's parts and joins) answers every criterion in its fixed form; the answer is a check run of the purpose `judge`, read by the new In progress exit check `task_judge_checks_pass`. Step 30 adds that check after `task_cmd_checks_pass` to every project that stores its stages block. A criterion not met is a round for the builder, or for a builder that joins parts; an answer not in its form is an error, asked again on the same head three times. The ticket view names the judge's answers. A run's model role is now always its own: the judge is no longer the summary writer's role.
- **E2E and Verification run** (7.1, 7.4, 13.9, schema step 31). Switched on in a project's Settings, each looks at a ticket once its stages before passed. The E2E tester (role `e2e`, in a fresh microVM with Docker, pushing nothing) drives the flows the ticket touches and records `add_e2e_result`: pass or fail, the flows, the log lines, and at most ten screenshots, PNG or JPEG of at most 2 MB, which Crew reads from its run's home without following a link and keeps with the entry (`record_files`; the task view shows them, and the app serves each only as an image). Crew then runs the E2E stage's `run` command in a check sandbox, a check run of the purpose `e2e`; `e2e_result_pass` needs both, on the head. A stage with no `run` waits and says so. The verifier (role `verifier`, another family than the builder, reading its branch) compares the change with the goal and the part of the plan, and records `add_verification`; `verify_pass` reads the newest one about the head. A failure in either stage is a fix round in place. The entries are records of the kinds `e2e` and `verify`, one each a run.
- **A project's own stage runs** (7.4, R101, schema step 32). Its entry names its agent's definition on `main`, its prompt, and an exit list Crew evaluates: `stage_verdict_pass` always, and commands and criteria as `cmd:` and `judge:`. The agent runs with the reviewer's model on the other family and records `add_stage_verdict` (an entry of the kind `stage`, with the stage); then Crew runs the commands and a judge answers the criteria. Validation refuses an entry with no agent on `.agents/agents/`, no prompt, no verdict in its exit, a check nobody answers, or a `run`. `PUT` and `DELETE /api/projects/{p}/stages/{id}`; the board and Settings show it by its title. A run's model role can now differ from its role (`modelRole`).
- **A pull request per ticket** (7.12, 6.7, schema step 33; repo.png). With a GitHub remote and `gh` signed in (`git.merge: auto`), a ticket that leaves In progress gets a pull request: its branch is pushed from the quarantine repository (`pushes.source`), at each new head while it is open, and held while a head changes files that are code without the operator's Allow; the pull request is opened through GitHub's API once the branch is there (`pull_requests`), and every entry and check run is posted to it as a comment or a status (`github_posts`, tried again like a push). At the merge the branch goes out at the merged head before `main`, so the push of `main` marks it merged; a closed ticket closes it. The Git screen has the table of repo.png, and the ticket view its link.
- **Work that came in from outside** (6.6, 7.12, 13.8, schema step 34). Crew fetches the remote's `main` every `git.fetch_minutes` and before a merge. A `main` that follows Crew's becomes the base and is checked in a check sandbox with the project's test command; a failure makes a fix ticket. One that changes files that are code waits in the inbox (Take it as main, Leave it, with its row in changes). One that went another way is merged with Crew's, checked, and then made `main` and pushed; a conflict or a failure makes a ticket that joins them, with the remote's `main` readable as `remote/main`, and Crew's other merges wait for it. A check in flight when Crew restarts ends as an error and runs again.
- **The two paths M9 left without a test** have one: the merge step clears a `code_wait` when the head that waited is gone, and `joined()` takes the project's and the ticket's locks before a subtask's row (a deterministic test: while another writer holds the ticket, `joined()` waits and holds no subtask row).
- **From the reviews of M10** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-m10.md`, `docs/reviews/2026-10-16-security-m10.md`; schema step 35). A ticket's branch is pushed at the commit that passed the gate for files that are code, never at what the branch holds when the push runs, and a held head cancels its branch's waiting pushes. The operator's answer about a remote main is kept by its commit, and a remote main that went another way is held as one that only follows is when it changes files that are code. The fetch runs beside the pass. A check run of a project's own stage names its stage, and a judge's check run keeps its criteria; a judge that cannot start yet counts no try. An agent's entry loses its secrets before it is stored and posted, and nothing posted to GitHub mentions anyone. The proxy checks that the TLS handshake in a tunnel names the host the tunnel was opened for, holds at most 64 tunnels a sandbox, ends one idle for 15 minutes, and dials no IPv6. The start-up test of Model only first proves it can reach the sandbox's own door, and only a timeout passes for the machine's address.
- **A judging run that wrote its entry and then met a limit is done** (12.7, found live in M10). WC-14's verifier wrote its verdict and met its CLI's limit seconds later; its subtask waited for a retry, and the merge waited behind it. A review, test, E2E, verification, or stage subtask whose entry is written is done, however its run ended.
- **The network in the app** (13.6, 20.1; settings.png). A project's Settings has the card "Network for this project's sandboxes" with Internet, Model only, and LAN, and "Use the machine's", which writes `sandbox.network` with its row in changes (`GET`/`PUT /api/projects/{p}/network`). A ticket's view has its own Network, written with the ticket's edit. Analysis can make a ticket `model_only` with `analyse_ticket`'s `network`, and nothing else: the internet and the LAN are the operator's to set.

### From 0.41 to 0.42

M9, the Crew MCP for tickets and subtasks. The operator approved its plan and its eight questions ("go all approved with your recommendations").

- **`agy` is the only agent CLI; `claude` is taken out** (the operator, during M9: "remove ALL agent clis and only build crew around the antigravity"). Google released Gemini 4 in Antigravity, and through `agy` the operator has Gemini 4 (`gemini-4-argon`), Gemini 3.8 Flash, Claude Opus 5.5, Claude Sonnet 5.5, and GPT-OSS 120B. Removed: the `claude` adapter, its login (`claude setup-token` and `crew-capture-token`), its Setup step, its install in the runtime image and its pins in `versions.lock`, its home in the harness build, its recordings under `testdata/claude/`, and its parts of the UI. No other CLI is added (R203, R204). What only `claude` produced went with it: Crew's id for a new conversation, the limits a run reads during its turn, the event that says Crew's MCP failed, a login that is a value, and a login that runs out. The adapter stays, so nothing outside it names the CLI. The rule for checks holds inside `agy`: a Gemini build is checked on a Claude model and the other way round. The defaults (12.3, 14.1): lead, planner, assistant, and improver on Opus 5.5 high, then Gemini 4; builder on Gemini 4 high, then Sonnet 5.5; reviewer and verifier on Opus 5.5, Gemini 4, then Flash; tester and E2E on Flash, then Sonnet 5.5; summary on Flash low; prompt writer and judge on Sonnet 5.5, then Flash; `lead_slots: {agy: 1}`. Schema step 23 takes `claude` off a machine that ran it: the stored login (deleted, as the operator asked), its login check, limits, and place in the lead queue; every claude entry of the machine's lists, a project's agents, and a ticket's pins. A role the operator set keeps its list without them; every other role's list is filled from the baseline when the server starts. The runs of `claude` and its models, marked gone, stay as the record. A file under `.claude/` or `.mcp.json` is no longer code for a CLI Crew runs.
- **The lead queue compares only the operator's priorities across projects** (7.9, R53, schema step 22; open item 1 of M8, from its security review). A ticket keeps the priority the operator set in `operator_priority`: on a ticket they add, and on each edit of its priority. The queue goes by class, the operator at the keyboard, the operator's priority (normal when they set none), the project served least recently, then the ticket's own priority, which an agent may have set in the breakdown or in Analysis, then the longest wait. A lead that marks its own tickets urgent goes first within its project only.
- **`may_change` is tested on every push** (13.8, 6.1). The git endpoint gives its hook the path rule of each ref a run may push: the paths the push changes, measured from where the branch leaves `main` (a subtask's branch from its ticket's branch, 7.11), as one diff of the whole branch, so a file changed and changed back is no change and a branch that merged `main` in is not charged with `main`'s files. Every file that does not instruct agents is allowed (`**`); a file that instructs agents for any CLI (what each reads as instructions, what is code for it, a GitHub workflow, and the folders an index file names on either side of the change) only when the ticket's `may_change` names it. A parallel subtask's push stays inside its `touches`. The hook's shell part tests the refs as before, and then hands the lines to the server's own program (`crew git-pre-receive`), which reads the pushed objects in git's quarantine; a request with rules and no program is refused. A ticket an agent created gets its `may_change` paths only once the operator agreed. Work in progress has its branch's rule, without a part's `touches`: the next run of the subtask starts from it, and a stray file there must not lose a stopped run's work (from the reviews).
- **Files that are code wait in the inbox** (13.8, 7.8, schema step 24). When the merge queue finds that a ticket's branch changes files that are code for the agent CLI, or GitHub workflows, it records the head, `main`, and the files in `code_wait`, and the ticket waits; the inbox shows them with See the diff, Refuse, and Allow the merge. The answer is about that head (`code_say`): Allow lets the merge check and the move go on for it; Refuse starts one fix round in place that tells the builder which files to take out. A head pushed after an answer asks again, and an answer about a head that no longer waits is refused. The two M6 paths got tests of their own: a fixed head's checks in a last In progress are tried three times and then the ticket waits, and a decision's base check is tried three times for its subtask (the subtask's retry gets the one try left, and a second analysis three again).
- **From the reviews of M9** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-m9-mcp.md`, `docs/reviews/2026-10-16-security-m9-mcp.md`). A join's merge commit takes the later parent's date, so the same join is the same commit and its check run is found again (a join by a merge re-ran its check on every pass). A personal token's comment is stored with the token as its author and reaches the agent fenced as data (it reached prompts as the operator's instruction). A token's ticket keeps origin `token`, also with may answer, which now marks it approved by being added (`approvedByAdding`): it cannot skip Review, and its `may_change` waits for your yes. A token has at most ten tickets waiting for analysis in a project and fifty a day. A blocked address's wrong tokens add no row, old rows of wrong tokens are pruned, and a right token works from a blocked address; every request of a token counts toward its minute, under a lock; making, switching, renewing, and revoking a token is in the history. The work-in-progress ref has its branch's path rule. A part must name the files it touches. `link_tickets` links only from the run's own ticket or one it created, never from one that is done or closed, at most twenty a run. A helper is no work of its own, and ends whenever its parent's run is not working. `code_wait` is cleared when the head changes no file that is code. Writers of a ticket's subtasks take the project and ticket locks first, also when they record a join. Schema step 28 dismisses open recommendations of a claude entry, stops comparisons with one, and takes claude out of `lead_slots` and `extra_models` wherever it is. The address of a request to `/mcp` is taken from the ingress's `X-Forwarded-For`, which is safe while only the ingress reaches the operator's door (as on dave): rebutted, with the reason in the code.
- **From the second review of M9** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-m9-mcp-round2.md`). The work in progress has its branch's rule without a part's files, so a stray file does not lose a stopped run's work. A token's ticket cannot skip Review in an import either (one list, `board.MustReview`). At most twenty comments of a token wait for an answer on a ticket or the plan. The caps of a token's tickets and of a run's links count and act as one step. A token is made or changed together with its row in the history.
- **A check sandbox left from before a restart is removed when the server starts** (17). The check run is an error then and runs again in a new sandbox, so nothing used the old one: on dave one stayed up after an install during the live check L1.
- **The Crew MCP screen, and parallel work on the screens** (20.1, 20.2; mcp.png, task-parallel.png, board-parallel.png). The app's page is `/crew-mcp`, as `/mcp` is the server's own: the server, ticket or subtask, what agents created with its counts, your own tools with the personal tokens (create, shown once, may answer, renew, revoke, last use with its address), what agents created newest first, the calls newest first with the run's subtask or the token's name, and the tools with who may call each and their calls. The task view says who created each subtask and where it works (created by the lead in SF-9.1, with `create_subtask`, in parallel on `sub/SF-9.2`, its files, joined), names a helper's parent, and shows parts that run at once as one Running now with the others named; a board card says "2 builders work in parallel".
- **The Crew MCP for the operator's own tools** (11.7, schema step 27). A personal token is made on the Crew MCP screen, for the projects named, and shown once: `crew_pat_` and 32 random bytes; Crew keeps its SHA-256. It runs out after `mcp_tokens.days` unless renewed, can be revoked, and has "may answer" off unless switched on. A server of its own answers it at `/mcp` of the operator's door, which the ingress serves without the sign-in, with every identity header and the portal's cookies taken out; the server checks the token itself. Five wrong tokens from one address in an hour block that address for the rest of the hour, and a token makes at most `mcp_tokens.calls_per_minute` calls (429 past either). Its tools: the read tools of the runs with a project named, `list_projects`, `get_inbox` (its projects' items), `create_ticket` (origin `token`, analysed like an agent's, or the operator's own with may answer), and `comment`; with may answer, `answer_new_feature` and `change_decision`, which are not even listed without it. What it does is recorded with "token <name>", and every call in `mcp_calls` with the token. A run's token never works there, and a personal token never at a sandbox's door. The git switch is deferred (above).
- **Subtasks agents create, in parallel, joined by the backend** (7.5, 7.11, 11.4, 11.5, schema step 26). `create_subtask` (the lead while it analyses its ticket, the builder while it builds it) queues a part with its title, what, what done looks like, an optional check command, its role (builder or tester; the E2E tester comes with M10), the files or folders it touches, and the parts it comes after, by number; it records who created it and from which run, and counts toward `max_added_subtasks`. `add_helper` records a helper under the run's own subtask, which ends with the run's turn; `subtask_done` ends a part's run. In In progress a ticket with parts starts every queued part whose `after` is done and joined and whose files no running part names (a part that names none runs alone), up to `max_parallel_subtasks`, each on `sub/<ticket>.<n>`, cut from the ticket's branch, or from `main` while that is not pushed; the git endpoint measures its pushes from there and keeps them inside its files. A lead's plan in parts replaces the one builder; a builder that splits off a part works beside it, and its `request_move` ends its run, as the ticket's checks run after the last join. After `subtask_done` the part's check runs on its branch. One at a time, while no run works on the ticket's branch and no check of the ticket runs, the backend merges a done part's branch into the ticket's branch in the canonical repository (a ticket's branch behind the part takes its head), runs the part's check on the merge, moves the ticket's branch forward only, and records the join as a subtask of the backend; a part that pushed nothing, or that the branch has already, counts as joined. A conflict, or the ticket's checks failing after the last join, starts one builder subtask (`join`, not counted as added) on the ticket's branch with what failed and the part's branch to read; after two such, the ticket waits for the lead's look again (M11). The board does not move a ticket out of In progress while a part or a join is not done and joined. The stage prompts `part` and `join` are new; the lead's Analysis and the builder's In progress say how to split work.
- **A `main` from the terminal with files that are code is held** (13.11, schema step 25), not refused: `terminal_mains.code` names those files, and the inbox item names them before the rest. Only a push whose changes cannot be read is still not taken.
- **Your yes for an agent's ticket that may change instructions** (13.8, 7.8). A ticket of origin agent, token, conversation, or chat whose analysis names paths in `may_change` waits in Analysis, ready, with "waits for you to agree", and the inbox asks with Open, Refuse, and Agree. Agree lets it change them (`mayChangeAgreed` in its analysis); Refuse takes them out of its `may_change`, and it goes on without them. A new list from a later analysis needs a new yes. A ticket of the plan or of the operator needs none.
- **A new feature in the app** (7.6, 7.8): the inbox lists each ticket in Analysis whose status is `new_feature_notice`, with who created it and from which ticket, its goal, and when it goes ahead by itself, or that it is held, or, with `approvals.new_feature: operator`, that it waits for the operator; its alternatives are Open, Approve now, Discard, and Hold, or Lift the hold. Lift the hold starts the clock again with what was left. The task view has the same three. The project's activity has the news of a notice given and of a new feature that went ahead when nobody objected.
- **`create_ticket` and `link_tickets`** (7.5, 11.5). A ticket an agent creates is in the Backlog with what it saw (`seen`), why, and optionally a first what, goal, and check; it is marked created by an agent with the role and the run, and linked `created_from` to the ticket the run works on. It cannot be created before the project's OK, when the lead proposes tickets instead, and one run creates at most ten. An agent sets no priority on it (R53). The prompt of every later run on it says what its creator saw and which ticket it was created from. The stage prompts of the builder, the reviewer, and the tester say to create a ticket for work outside their own, after a search, and not to do it. An agent links `depends_on`, `duplicates`, and `created_from`; `replaces` is the lead's `cancel_and_replace` (M11), and a bug's link comes with `report_bug`, which waits for the bug records of M12 (the operator's answer to question 4 of the plan of M9).

### From 0.40 to 0.41

M8, models, lead slots, and the quota guard. The operator approved its plan and its six questions ("go approved all"): a `main` pushed from the project terminal waits for a confirmation, R140 says what the terminal holds, Gemini Flash is the checks' last entry, the project's Settings screen gets its agent parts only, news is read from the tables that cause it, and three live checks.

- **The models are read as each CLI lists them** (12.3, schema step 15). The login check reads the list where the login is, with no model request: `agy models`, and `claude`'s answer to the `initialize` request, read by `crew-run` through standard input that it closes once the answer came. A row keeps the name as it is passed, the model without its effort, the efforts it offers and whether the effort is part of the name, the name to show, the full name an alias points at, the family, the limit group, when it was first seen and last read, and when it went. A name a CLI refused in a run is passed over for a while (below, from the reviews). A failed read keeps the last list and is counted. A run records the name it passed beside the model, and the full name the CLI said it ran from its first event.
- **One choice for every run** (12.3, 12.7): a pin on the ticket, the project's setting, then the machine's entry, model and effort one by one; the nearest effort when the model lacks the one asked for, below first. The rule for checks also moves a check off the operator's own choice for a project or a ticket, and the run records `check_rule`; only `checks_use_another_family: false` keeps it. A continued run keeps where its model and effort came from.
- **Install fills `agents.roles` from the baseline** (12.3, schema step 16). The lists are `baseline/roles.yaml`; the listing of 14.1 shows the same, and a test holds the two together. When the server starts, an agent the machine's settings do not have gets the baseline's list and effort, and an agent it has gets the baseline's entries that are new to this machine after its own: `baseline_entries` holds every entry a machine was given, so an entry the operator took out does not come back. The machine's `agents.roles` is always stored whole, so a later listing changes no agent.
- **At a limit, at a lost sign-in, and at a refused name** (12.4, 12.7). A run that ends at its limit marks its group, or Opus or Sonnet alone when `claude`'s message names it, until the reset the CLI said, else for 15 minutes; its subtask waits until then, and the login check reads the limits again; how long a run's own report holds a group is below, from the reviews. A new reason, `model`, is a name the CLI refused: it is passed over for a while (below, from the reviews). The run driver's monitor asks the pipeline for the next choice of every subtask that waits for one of these, with the ticket's pin and the rule for checks as a new run would have them, and continues it there in a new sandbox, recorded as `limit`; with `at_limit: wait`, or with nothing else that can run, the subtask waits. `claude`'s message "You've hit your session limit · resets 3:45pm", or the same for the weekly limit, Opus, or Sonnet, is read as a limit; it is the vendor's documented text, and the first real one is recorded when it is met (spike item 68).
- **The quota guard holds runs back** (12.7): above `quota.slow_down_at` of a window, one run at a time works on its group, and a new one waits while one does, so the window is shared out instead of spent at once; no try goes there. The pipeline serves the project whose last run started longest ago first. Other groups are not held back.
- **An agent with no model** (12.3, 7.8): when no CLI in use offers any of an agent's entries, its runs wait, and the inbox has an item of the kind "what Crew could not do by itself", which goes when the agent has a model again. A pin or a project's setting that names a model no CLI lists is passed over, and the signal `model_gone` is the news of it.
- **One lead queue** (7.9, schema step 17): every loop that starts a lead run, the planner's drafts and breakdowns and the pipeline's analyses and the lead's turns, writes what waits into `lead_waits` on each pass and takes away what no longer waits; a run that starts takes its row away. A free slot of a CLI goes to the first row of that CLI in the queue's order: the class in the order of `lead_queue.order`, the operator at the keyboard first in a class (a plan draft), then the ticket's priority across projects, work with no ticket counting as normal, then the project served least recently, then the longest wait. While a group of a CLI's models is above `quota.slow_down_at`, the guard halves that CLI's lead slots, never below one (12.7).
- **Results, and Crew's choice for the builder** (12.3, schema step 18). A builder's unit is a ticket: the pick (entry and effort) of its first build, whether Crew's first check run for a move passed, its fix rounds, and the estimated cost and time of its builds and fixes; a checking agent's unit is a run with its verdict, and whether it asked for a fix. Numbers are kept under the model a run really ran, so an alias that moved starts again, and are shown for every kind of work and for each. The pipeline looks at them once a minute: it ends a comparison that has its units, goes back from a change whose watched tickets are worse (and blocks that pick for `retry_days`), and changes the builder's first entry and effort when one is better, or as well for less, by the plain rules, at most once in `min_days_between` and only to what can run, and to the family the checks run on only when another family can run them. Each is a row of `model_choices`, which is also the news. For an agent the operator set, Crew writes a recommendation instead, with Use it and Not now. Every build of one ticket runs on the pick of its first; a new ticket is a try with `explore_share`, chosen by a hash of the ticket so it is the same on every pass, in turn another entry and the own entry one effort lower; a running comparison sends every second ticket to its other pick. No try or comparison on a pinned ticket, in a project that set the builder, while a change is watched, or to a pick that cannot run or is close to its limit. Until bugs are recorded (M12) Crew changes only the builder; the other four agents follow their list.
- **Parked question 100 answered:** the reviewer and the verifier get `agy` with `gemini-3.8-flash` as their last entry.
- **A `main` from the project terminal waits for your confirmation** (13.11, schema step 19), as the operator decided on M7's open item 1: anything that runs in the terminal while edit mode is on could push one. The pushed commit is kept under `refs/crew/held/<id>` of the canonical repository, the git endpoint serves Crew's `main` again, and the inbox has the item with its diff, Take it as main, and Refuse. Taken, it becomes `main` only while Crew's `main` is still the one it went on from, and goes on to the remote; the history has every step.
- **The screens of M8** (20.1, 20.2). The Models screen has what Crew chose, with its cards and a comparison; every model by CLI and family, with the entries no CLI offers now marked as skipped, a name you add, and one model opened with the agents that use it, what it has done for each agent and effort, and its latest runs; the agents, each with its model, effort, who chooses, its next choices, and for a checking agent what the rule for checks does now; which model each agent gets in each project; and the results. Agent CLIs shows each group's limit as the guard keeps it, and the rule for checks. The front page has the lead slots of each CLI with the runs on them, and the queue. A project's Settings gives each stage's agent a model and an effort of the project's own, and the rule for checks its switch; the rest of the project's settings is edited from the UI with M17. The task view's "Agent for this ticket" pins a model, an effort, or both for the builder, the reviewer, or the tester, and says what each would run on now.
- **From the reviews of M8** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-m8-models.md`, `docs/reviews/2026-10-16-security-m8-models.md`). What a run says about the limits, in its events or its last result, is its own account (12.4): it holds a group, or a model, for 15 minutes at most and makes no group close to its limit for longer; a run that ended at a limit has Crew's own login check run with its request, and only what that check reads holds a group to its reset; no reset is believed further away than a week. A name a CLI refused in a run is passed over for 15 minutes, then tried again, and Refresh offers it again at once; it is never taken away for every project. A name a CLI lists is kept only in the shape a model's name has (letters, digits, `. _ : - [ ]`, never a leading dash). `gpt-` is the GPT family. The builder's family, for the rule for checks, is that of the model Crew passed. A name the operator adds leaves a CLI's short list in place. Every change of the machine's settings, the operator's and Crew's, reads them under one lock; Crew's own change of an agent the operator set meanwhile is not made, and a change Crew watches ends, as `superseded`, when the operator sets the agent; a run's report changes no window that Crew's own check read in the last 30 minutes, and never frees one the check holds; a decision of a held main that began and never ended, as at a restart, leaves it to be decided again after 10 minutes. A held main is claimed by the one decision that takes or refuses it. A checking agent's approving run is counted as a run that asked for no fix.
- **R140 and the glossary** of the requirements say what the project terminal holds since 0.40: a clone of the project's repository, which takes no push in diagnostics mode, in place of a repository mounted read-only. The operator approved the wording.
### From 0.39 to 0.40

M7, the web UI. The operator approved its plan and its four questions ("go all approved").

- **The inbox is read, not stored** (7.8): each item is read from the state that causes it when the inbox is shown, and goes when its cause does. The kinds that exist by M7 are a project that waits for its OK, a project Crew paused at a limit, and a CLI that is signed out; the others come with the milestones that make their causes. `make check` also runs the UI's types and unit tests.
- **From the reviews of M7** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-15-review-m7-web-ui.md`, `docs/reviews/2026-10-15-security-m7-web-ui.md`). The project terminal's log is sealed with the key of the credentials and capped at 16 MB an attachment (schema step 14); edit mode goes back to diagnostics when no page is on the terminal; a `main` it pushes that changes a file that is code, or a workflow, is not taken, and one not taken is in the history with the reason; a terminal connection is taken only from `https://` and Crew's own address, as every change. The history of the Git screen reads git's output with separators that change on every reading, so a commit's message or author cannot hide or shift a commit; the operator's branches under `you/` are read like Crew's; a diff is one git process, at most 1 MB of patches and 1,000 files, with the number of the rest. A first message is sent once, whichever round is chosen; a request the prompt writer refuses makes no draft; at most three rewrites are written at once; a line of a rewrite that only begins like a heading is its own. A changed decision's ticket holds only the operator's words and choice: the agent's question, choice, and reason are a note of Crew's on that ticket, which names the role and which no check reads as that role's work. A ticket's view reads the newest 500 pushes of its branch. A `main` the terminal pushes is also not taken when what it changed cannot be read. Known gap, approved for M7: the fresh sign-in that 20.3 asks for before a terminal opens comes with the sessions (M17).
- **Which run pushed which commits** (6.7, 15.3, schema step 10): the git endpoint records every ref a push changed, with its commit before and after and the run that pushed, in `ref_pushes`, only added to; a new branch's before is where it left `main` at that moment. A ticket's commits are grouped by the subtask whose run pushed them, and a subtask's own diff is its first push's before against its last push's after. Commits from before this record are listed as not recorded by a run. Work in progress is not recorded.
- **Changing a decision** (7.5, schema step 11): Change in the Decisions list creates the operator's ticket that carries the change out, of the kind `chore`, created from the ticket the decision was made on, and the decision keeps what it was changed to and by which ticket. A decision is changed once; replacing its memory entry comes with memory (M12).
- **The Git screen** (6.7) reads `main` and the tags from the canonical repository and the branches runs push from the quarantine one; a history of all branches names the branch each commit was reached by. The clone address of a local project, served over HTTPS with a personal token, comes with the tokens (M9); pull requests with M10.
- **The project terminal** (13.11, schema step 12) is a clone from the git endpoint, which takes no push from it in diagnostics mode, and in edit mode `main` (only forward), branches under `you/`, and a ticket's branch while no agent runs on it and it is not done. A push to a ticket's branch becomes a comment of yours on the ticket, which reaches its next run. `project_terminals` holds the open terminal and its mode, `terminal_log` what was typed and printed, removed after 180 days; opening, switching, and closing are rows in `changes`, and a jump into a run's microVM is one in its ticket's history. The idle reaper closes a terminal after 30 minutes without input. A fresh sign-in before a terminal opens comes with the sessions (M17).
- **The prompt writer** (7.2, schema step 13): a call of the `prompt` role in a judge sandbox, with `baseline/agents/prompt-writer.md` and the stages `rewrite.md` and `review-text.md`, the operator's text fenced as data, and in a project its goal, the titles of its plan's parts, and at most five rewrites the operator edited there. Its answer is read only in its fixed form. A new plan draft starts with no message while its first message's proposal is written; the planning agent starts with the text chosen. When there is no proposal to choose, because it was off, late, failed, or good as it is, the operator's own text is sent at once and the draft says why. `prompt_rewrites` keeps every rewrite with its round, the operator's choice, and the text sent or used; a draft's message names the rewrite it came from; the choice is the signal `rewrite_choice`. Improve text and Review text work in every box where the operator writes by M7 (a draft's message, a ticket the operator adds, a comment on a ticket or on the plan, sending a breakdown back), and on the plan of a draft they are a message to the planning agent with the part quoted, fenced as data. The fence of 13.8 is one package, `internal/fence`, for the pipeline and the planner.
- **The task view** (20.2) reads a run's prompt cut into its saved parts, each with its source, which add up to the prompt; and the last lines of a run's log. A continue the operator asks for clears the ticket's `stuck`; Crew's own retry does not.

### From 0.38 to 0.39

M6, one autonomous ticket. The operator approved its plan and its fourteen items ("go all approved").

- **The pipeline** (15.2, 11.3) is the orchestrator's part for tickets, beside the planner: it analyses a ticket in Analysis, starts the builder of a ticket in In progress and the agent of each optional stage for the branch as it is, once per commit, acts on what each run reported after its turn ended, runs the goal loop, the fix rounds, and the merge queue, and has the summary written. It keeps no plan: what runs is derived from the board, the subtasks, and the check runs. A turn that ended without its reporting call gets one reminder; then the run ends as a stop (`rounds`) and is retried once. At `max_fix_rounds`, after two stopped runs, or after two failed analyses, the ticket waits with the reason, for the lead's look again, which is M11's.
- **Runs on a ticket** (6.1, 7.11, 12.10): a subtask is numbered under its ticket. The builder clones and pushes the ticket's branch. The lead, a reviewer, and a tester read and push nothing, not even work in progress; the breakdown's run of M5 lost its branch the same way. A fix round is a new subtask that continues the builder's conversation. A reviewer runs on another family than the builder, by the family of the model's name (12.3); when no entry can, it runs on the builder's and a signal says the rule could not be met. The summary writer is a judge: one call with no tools, no repository, no Crew MCP, and no harness in its home (spike item 79).
- **The prompts of the builder, the reviewer, the tester, the analysis, the fix round, and the summary writer** are in the baseline (`agents/`, `stages/`). Everything an agent wrote reaches another as a fenced block that names its author, between lines of a random mark the text cannot know, so a text cannot end its fence (13.8, security review of M5). The operator's comments come unfenced, with their ids.
- **The core of the Crew MCP** (11.5): `get_summary`, `search_summaries`, `set_steps`, `tick_step`, `add_note`, `reply_comment`, `write_review`, `add_test_result`, `request_move`, `fix_done`, `analyse_ticket`, and `decide`. A run's token names its ticket and its subtask; a write goes to its own ticket only. A review and a test entry are about the commit the run cloned, once per run. `analyse_ticket` tries the decision on the board at once, and Crew applies it after the turn.
- **The checks of M4 that waited for M6** read the records and Crew's check runs: `note_exists`, `task_cmd_checks_pass` and `task_checks_pass` (a check run on the branch's head that ran every command check), `review_verdict_approve` (the newest review, of the head), `test_result_pass` (the tester finished on the head, and Crew's run of the checks and the project's tests passed there), and `merges_and_passes_on_main`.
- **The tables of M6** (15.3, schema step 8): `records` (notes, reviews, test entries, and comments, with the commit they are about), `decisions`, `subtask_steps`, `check_runs`, `summaries` with their facts and a search index, and `signals`, only added to. A subtask records what its run reported, its goal rounds, and the commit its stage agent judged. A check has `keeps`; a run says whether it may push.
- **The routes of M6** (15.4): `POST /api/projects/{p}/comments`, `GET /api/projects/{p}/tasks/{t}/subtasks`, `GET /api/projects/{p}/tasks/{t}/summary`, `GET /api/projects/{p}/summaries`, and `GET /api/projects/{p}/decisions`; a task's view adds its records and its check runs.
- **The signals of M6** (10.2): check rounds, rework with its reason, analysis decisions, decided alone, flaky checks, cost and time when a ticket ends, a check rule that could not be met, the watchdog's warnings and stops (not the operator's), and each run's sandbox start-up, from its start to its first event.
- **From the reviews of M6** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-15-review-m6-one-ticket.md`, `docs/reviews/2026-10-15-security-m6-one-ticket.md`). A ticket whose branch changes a file that is code for a CLI (each adapter names them: `agy`'s hooks, MCP configuration, plugins, skills, and agents; `claude`'s settings files, `.mcp.json`, skills, and agents) or a GitHub workflow never merges by itself: Crew makes no merge candidate for it, `merges_and_passes_on_main` does not pass, and it waits for the operator, whose say comes with `may_change` and the inbox (M9). A run that does not build reads `main`'s copies of the files its CLI reads as instructions, never the branch's: `crew-run` takes the branch's out before every turn, tracked or not, without following a link, and writes `main`'s through the workspace's root (for `agy`: `AGENTS.md` and `GEMINI.md` at every level, and `.agents/rules`, `skills`, `agents`, with the files that list more of them). The tree a check sandbox gets is written by Crew from the commit's objects, so the commit's `.gitattributes` cannot leave a file out or change one. A model its CLI no longer lists is gone, and the role's next entry runs (12.3); a check whose other family is offered but whose CLI is signed out waits, and runs on the builder's family only when no CLI offers another. A decision whose checks could not be run on `main` three times is not applied: its run ends as a stop of the sandbox. A run answers only the operator's comments it was given; every comment its prompt holds is given to it. A run writes one review or test entry, held by a unique index (schema step 9), and at most 20 notes; a prompt keeps the newest review and test entry before the newest notes; a ticket's budget is at most the project's. When In progress is the last stage, the merge queue runs the ticket's checks on a head a fix round pushed; a ticket at its limit of fix rounds gives up its turn to merge; merge errors are counted by the head they merge; a builder is not told that Crew's own merge step failed its build; a goal round is a signal once, with its turn; a run that continues a subtask in a new sandbox begins its goal loop and its reminder again. A ticket's `stuck` (schema step 9) says why the pipeline starts nothing more for it, beside the board's `waiting`. The merge subtask is recorded once `main` moved. A failure of a conversation's summary is logged and stops no ticket, and a summary is tried three times.
- **From the live run on dave** (wordcount WC-7). Every clone holds `main` as `origin/main`, which a reviewer and a tester compare the branch with and a fix round merges; the stage prompts say so. A judge whose call stopped, as one whose model asked for a tool it does not have, is retried as a judge. A ticket on hold in Analysis gets no analysis run until it is let go, as 15.2 says of every run. After M6 the operator decided that a ticket withdrawn in Planning gets the facts of a summary and no summary writer (8.4); on dave, WC-1 to WC-6 had each had one. A model that a CLI no longer lists (`agy`/`gemini-3.1-pro` of the defaults) is passed over for the role's next entry, as said above.
- **Crew's summary of a conversation** (12.10): once a conversation of a project, in planning or running, has 26 turns after its last summary, a summary writer's call with no tools summarises them but the newest six, with the summary before it, in at most 400 words (`baseline/stages/conversation.md`). A conversation rebuilt from its turns begins with that summary, then the turns after it in full, at most 24; with no summary, the last six as before. This is what M3's row left to M6.

- **`agy` loads plugins from a workspace** (13.8), tried before a run that judges used `agy`: a plugin under `.agents/plugins/` and one that `.agents/plugins.json` names outside `.agents/` both ran their hook and contacted their server; front matter of a skill or an agent, a nested `.agents/`, and files written during a turn loaded nothing in that turn, but a plugin written then loaded in the next. The adapter of `agy` names `.agents/plugins/` and `.agents/plugins.json` beside the two files, so `crew-run` takes them out before every turn of a run that does not build.

### From 0.37 to 0.38

M5, plan to OK. The operator approved its plan and its decisions ("go all approved").

- **The baseline is a Go package** at the root of the source, `baseline/`, built into the server (10.3). M5 brings its first files: the lead's agent file, the guides for planning and for the breakdown, the templates of a plan and of a ticket, and `tools.yaml`, the words of the Crew MCP's tools and who may call each. The planning agent is the lead's agent file with the planning guide; its model comes from the `planner` entry of `agents.roles`, the breakdown's from `lead`.
- **The Crew MCP** (11.5) is Crew's own server, with the standard library only: MCP over HTTP at `/mcp` on each sandbox's door, answered as plain JSON, as the spike's stand-in was for both CLIs (`initialize`, `ping`, `tools/list`, `tools/call`; a notification gets 202; `server/discover` and other methods are "not found"; GET is not offered). A request is a run's when it comes to that run's door, from its address, with its token; the role and the one project or plan draft come from the token's row while the run lives. A tool's arguments never say who calls, and an argument a tool does not have is refused. `tools/list` shows a token only its own tools. A refusal is told to the agent; a failure of Crew is told without its text, which stays in the server's log. Every call is a row of `mcp_calls`, with every long text in its arguments cut to its start and its length.
- **A run of a role** (the planning agent, the lead) has a subtask with no ticket, of the scope `crew` with its plan draft, or `project` with its project. Its token names the subtask's role and project or draft. A draft's run has no repository and works in an empty folder; when the draft names a GitHub repository, its read-only token names that one. A project's run works on its project's repository, and the git endpoint refuses it every other. Its sandbox has no Docker. The runs of the roles with Crew MCP tools, the planning agent and the lead in M5, get the server's address; `crew-run` asks it to initialize with the run's token before the CLI starts, and ends the turn with `error` when it does not answer (spike item 10). `claude` gets it with `--mcp-config` beside `--strict-mcp-config`, from a file in the run's home; `agy` in `~/.gemini/config/mcp_config.json`. Both files are written with mode 0600 and removed for a run without it. The saved prompt lists its parts with their sources (8.6), and a run records where its model came from.
- **A plan draft** (7.2) is started with the operator's first message, and may name a GitHub repository, `owner/name`, for the planning agent to read. Every message waits in `draft_messages` until a run takes it. The planner (15.2) queues the planning agent for a draft with a waiting message: a first run, whose prompt is the lead's agent file, the planning guide, the plan template, what the draft is, and the message; or the next turn of the same conversation, in its microVM while the run waits for its next turn, and otherwise in a new one (12.10). A message that comes while a turn runs waits for it to end, and the messages that waited go together. The planning agent writes each version of the plan with `write_plan_draft`; a version is a row that never changes.
- **The prompt writer comes with M7**, with the Plans screen and the menu on a selection, as M7's row says. In M5 the operator's first message is sent as written; `planning.rewrite` is read from M7.
- **A project from a draft** (7.2, steps 2 and 3): `POST /api/projects` with `draft` takes the draft's newest version as the original plan, and its Goal section the goal where the request gives none. The plan is the planning agent's text, which is data: its front matter may switch optional stages on beside the defaults, never off, and its budget is not taken; the request's `stages` and `budgetUsd` decide (security review of M5). A budget is a finite number, 0 or more. The draft is the project's from then on, with its runs and their conversations, so their cost is the project's. A draft whose newest version changed meanwhile makes no project, and a draft makes one project only.
- **A new repository on GitHub** (6.6): `"repository": {"kind": "github"}` creates a private repository under the account `gh` is signed in as, named after the project or `name`, through GitHub's API with `gh`'s token; the server runs no `gh` program. Without `gh`, `url` gives the SSH address of an empty repository the operator made. GitHub refusing makes nothing (21); a repository that GitHub made and the machine could not is named in the error, empty, to be given as `url`. Git goes over SSH with the key from Setup, written for one push into a folder of its own and removed after, against GitHub's host keys pinned in the source, never learned on first use. The server's image gets `openssh-client` and `libnss-wrapper`: OpenSSH refuses a uid with no entry in `/etc/passwd`, and the server runs as the machine's `crew`, whose uid differs between machines, so the one `ssh` of a push gets an entry for its uid from a file beside the key. `main` is pushed at once, and a push that fails is a row of `pushes` that the board's loop tries again, waiting longer each time; a push is never forced, so a remote whose `main` went another way refuses it and keeps it.
- **The lead's breakdown** (7.2, step 4; 15.2): a project in planning with no breakdown queues the lead, whose prompt is its agent file, the breakdown guide, the ticket's form, and the project's name and key; it works on the project's `main`. `propose_tickets` adds every ticket of one call in one transaction, or none: each has a why, a what, a goal, and at least one check; `@n` names the n-th ticket of the same call in `dependsOn`; `withdraw` closes the lead's own tickets still in Planning as discarded, with the reason. A turn that ended with no breakdown or no ticket gets one reminder. A turn that ended with both ends the run, so its microVM is not kept; a comment the breakdown is sent back with continues its conversation in a new microVM, once for each comment. `write_breakdown` and `propose_tickets` are refused after the OK.
- **Lead slots** (7.9): a run of the planning agent or the lead takes a slot of its CLI while its turn runs; a planning agent waiting for the operator holds none. A run waits for a slot of the CLI of its first entry that can run, and does not move to another CLI for one. Drafts go before breakdowns, then by age. The model is the role's first entry whose CLI is in use and signed in, or the project's own setting for the role, which wins (12.3, rule 15 of `AGENTS.md`); Crew's automatic choice and the quota guard are M8's.
- **The routes of M5** (15.4): `GET, POST /api/drafts`; `GET /api/drafts/{d}`, with every version, the waiting messages, the turns with the CLI, the model, and the effort of each, and the planning agent's newest run; `POST /api/drafts/{d}/messages`; `POST /api/projects` with `draft` and the repository kind `github`; `GET /api/projects/{p}/git`, with the remote, `main`, and the pushes; `GET /api/projects/{p}/mcp/calls`, with the calls of the draft it was made from; and `GET /api/crew/mcp/tools`.
- **From the reviews of M5** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-15-review-m5-plan.md`, `docs/reviews/2026-10-15-security-m5-plan.md`). The Crew MCP answers a run only while a turn of it runs, so what a turn left running cannot write between turns. A run makes at most 400 calls; a call past them is refused and not recorded, and the stored arguments of a call are cut at 8 KB as a whole. A draft keeps at most 100 versions of its plan, a project at most 100 versions of its breakdown and 100 comments it was sent back with, and a breakdown at most 300 tickets. A ticket's part and the heading of its source are one line of at most 200 characters, its quote and the reason of its docs at most 4,000, and it depends on at most 50 tickets. A breakdown and a comment on it are refused after the OK under the project's lock. A draft's subtask keeps the draft's scope after the draft became a project: its token never names the project, and its runs never get the project's repository; the planner ends the runs of such a draft. The operator's messages of a draft, and the job of a comment or a reminder, are marked taken in the transaction that records the run or the turn that takes them, and a turn that did not start takes the mark back. A breakdown run that ended well with nothing written, by its idle time, gets its one reminder too; one the operator stopped, or the driver gave up on or retries, does not. A turn that did not start because its run ended meanwhile takes back its mark and its turn. The planner starts runs only once the server has taken over the runs of the server before it and the sandboxes' door answers. A push ends after two minutes, its whole group of processes with it, and `ssh` sends keepalives; the board's loop pushes beside its reconcile, so a remote that stalls holds up no board; a failed push is recorded in Crew's own words, and what git printed stays in the server's log. The key of a push is written in a folder of the server's own, which the server empties when it starts; the preload that gives `ssh` its passwd entry is in the ssh's own command. A goal is cut at a character. The image's check of `libnss-wrapper` fails when the library is missing.
- **The lead revises a ticket of its breakdown in place** (11.5), approved by the operator after M5, where a revision had withdrawn six tickets and proposed five again with new numbers to change one: `propose_tickets` takes `revise`, the number of one of the lead's tickets still in Planning with the whole ticket as it should be. The number, the history, and the tickets that depend on it stay; what it depends on is replaced, and `@n` may name a ticket proposed in the same call. A priority or a budget the revision leaves out stays as it was. A ticket the operator changed since the lead last wrote it is refused: the operator's change wins, and the lead says in the breakdown what should change. One row of history holds the ticket before and after. Withdrawals come first, then new tickets, then revisions, all in the one transaction. The breakdown guide tells the lead to change only what the comment asks for.
- **A repository's own CLI configuration stays out of a run that does not build** (13.8), approved by the operator after M5's reviews: each adapter names the files its CLI loads from a workspace by itself, and `crew-run` takes them out of the workspace before every turn of a run whose role does not build. On dave, with Crew's image, `agy` ran a workspace's hook and gave its MCP server five requests both with the workspace in `trustedWorkspaces` and without it, so leaving it untrusted is no way out.
- **The tables of M5** (15.3, schema step 7): `plan_drafts`, with the GitHub repository a draft may name for the planning agent to read; `plan_draft_versions`, only added to; `draft_messages`, where a message no run took yet is what the planning agent is queued for; the draft on `subtasks`, `conversations`, and `run_tokens`, so a run with no project belongs to its draft; `projects.draft` and `projects.remote`; `mcp_calls`, only added to; and `pushes`, whose failed push is pending again.

### From 0.36 to 0.37

M4, the board core.

- **A project is a row and two repositories** (6.1, 6.6). `POST /api/projects` takes a name, a key, the plan, the optional stages the plan asks for, and a repository that is new on the machine or an existing one cloned over `https`; a repository Crew creates on GitHub comes with M5, where its done-when needs it, and so does the first push to GitHub over SSH that spike item 32 left for later. The repositories are made first, and removed again when the rows cannot be written. A new local repository gets one commit with `README.md`, `AGENTS.md`, and `CHANGELOG.md`, made with the operator's git identity from Setup. The canonical repository is `<data>/canonical/<project>.git`, which the host setup makes and the server mounts. The fixture of M3 is a project row with the key `FIX`, and gets its canonical repository when the server starts.
- **A project's settings** are `board.yaml` of 14.1, validated like the machine's. The stages are checked as a list: the four fixed stages first and Done last, Crew's four optional stages once each in their order, a stage of a project's own between them, and every check a stage names one that 7.1 knows or a `cmd:` or a `judge:`.
- **Tickets** (7.1, 7.12) get the project's key and a counter. Before the OK they are in Planning; after it, one the operator adds goes to the top of the backlog. A ticket of the plan or of the operator waits for analysis as `waiting`; one of an agent as `proposed`. The operator edits the title, the kind, the why, the what, and the goal only before Analysis, and the priority, the hold, and the budget until the ticket is done.
- **The OK** moves every ticket in Planning to Backlog in one transaction, and the project's history keeps the plan rows and the tickets it approved. It is refused with no ticket in Planning. Sending the breakdown back is a row of `plans`.
- **Analysis decisions** (7.6) are the board's to check, and the lead's to make through the Crew MCP (M6): approve, with a why, a what, a goal, and a check; skip, with a reason and one of the four conditions; discard, with a reason; and the notice of a new feature. A ticket of the plan or of the operator is only approved or skipped. Review cannot be skipped by a ticket of an agent, a conversation, or a chat. With `approvals.new_feature: operator` a notice has no clock, with `agent` it is ready at once. Hold stops a notice's clock and keeps what was left of it; letting the ticket go starts the clock again with that. `approvals.created_task: operator` comes with M9, where agents create tickets.
- **Moves** are made by the board's reconcile step, on a timer of 30 seconds and on every change, for each running project: the clocks of notices and of skipped tickets, then every ticket from the rightmost stage, each in a transaction of its own under the project's lock, until nothing moves. A ticket moves to the next stage that is on and that it does not skip when the exit checks of its stage and the enter checks of the next pass, and the next stage has room; otherwise its `waiting` says why. A move to the left is refused whoever asks. A project is Done when every ticket is done, closed, or skipped, and a ticket added later makes it run again.
- **The checks this version evaluates** are those of the ticket's rows and of its branch: `has_why_what_goal`, `has_check`, `analysis_approved`, `dependencies_done`, `branch_pushed` (the branch is in the quarantine repository), `has_commits`, and `docs_done`. The others need Crew's records or its check sandbox: they do not pass, and the ticket's `waiting` names the milestone that brings them (M6, or M10 for E2E, Verification, and a stage of a project's own). No route and no admin command records a check result: the tests script them.
- **Limits** (7.7): the board holds the WIP limits, a hold, and a paused project itself. The budgets, the fix rounds, the share of added work, and the subtasks agents add are functions of numbers with their tests, called by the milestones that make what reaches them.
- **Export and import** (6.2): `plan/breakdown/` keeps every version, `plan/sent-back/` the comments, and `repo.bundle` every branch and tag; a ticket's moves are in its `task.md`.
- **The routes** are under `/api` (15.4): `GET, POST /api/projects`; `GET /api/projects/{p}/overview`, `/plan`, `/board`, `/analysis`; `POST /api/projects/{p}/ok`, with `{"sendBack": "..."}` to send the breakdown back; `POST .../pause`, `/resume`, `/archive`; `GET, PUT .../stages`, which takes `{"e2e": true}`; `GET, PUT .../settings`; `POST .../export`; `POST .../tasks`; `GET, PATCH .../tasks/{t}`; `POST .../tasks/{t}/decision` and `/discard`.
- **History:** `changes` names the project of a row, and the server's role may only add to `plans`, `stage_moves`, and `check_results`, as to `changes`.
- **From the reviews of M4** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-15-*-m4-board.md`). An import lands paused, keeps the board's rules, and marks every move it brings as imported (schema step 6); the export reads its rows in one snapshot, and the route writes it to a file before it answers. A ticket whose step fails does not stop the others in a pass: its `waiting` says what failed. The clocks of a project that has settled to Done are read too, and a ticket to analyse again makes it run again, so an `all_done` skip fires. A hold on a notice that had passed lets it go ahead when the hold is let go. The enter checks of Analysis are evaluated. A project's name cannot have the shape of an id. A new repository's canonical folder is claimed before it is made, so two that make one name at once never remove each other's; objects from outside are checked as git's fsck checks them, and the quarantine repository gets only `main` and Crew's branches of a bundle; the bundle of an export is made in a temporary repository, so writing it changes nothing. A token that clones an existing repository has a holder of its own. What git printed stays in the server's log. `crew-ctl project export` unpacks into a new folder beside the target, and takes a target that is a link or another user's folder for no.
- **From the second round of the reviews of M4** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-15-review-m4-board-round2.md`). The server's loop reconciles a Done project too, for its clocks. An imported ticket's every move goes to the stage the board would have chosen (`Pipeline.Next` with the ticket's skips and the stages on in the export's settings), or to closed; so a ticket that passed a stage which was switched on only later is refused, a known limit: switch that stage off in the export's `board.yaml` for the import, and on again after. Agents' tickets past Analysis keep their state: the project lands paused, and the operator's resume is the decision. A failed creation frees the folder it claimed. `crew-ctl project export` refuses a parent folder of another user, or one others may write to that is not sticky, and no longer changes the mode of a folder that exists.

### From 0.35 to 0.36

M3, one headless run.

- **The adapters start a turn and read it** (12.2, 12.5). Each says how one headless turn is started, how every line its CLI prints becomes exactly one run event, why a turn ended, what a result says about use, and which of its files hold a conversation. A line an adapter does not know is a `status` event, so nothing is dropped. `agy` gets `--mode accept-edits` and its settings file for the run; `claude` gets `--setting-sources user --strict-mcp-config` beside the flags of 12.2. The recordings of `testdata/` are replayed through each.
- **Secrets are removed by value and by shape** before anything from a sandbox is stored (8.3): every value the broker handed the sandbox, then each line inside a string that holds a needle of styrman-harness's list, and each key block whole. A JSON line keeps its form: only the strings that change are written again.
- **The git endpoint** serves the quarantine repositories under `<data>/repos/` on the sandbox networks, at `/git/<repo>.git`, through `git http-backend`; the server's image gets `git` (6.1). A run's token reads `main`, its branch, and the work in progress it continues from, and pushes only its branch and `refs/crew/wip/<run>`. The refs it may not read are hidden, a push is checked by the endpoint's own hook outside every repository, and no ref is removed. The endpoint serves protocol version 0 only: version 2 fetched a hidden commit by its id in the test, because its fetch is a request of its own and checks nothing against what was listed.
- **`crew-run turn turn-NNNN`** reads `run.json`, the token, and the turn's prompt, pulls the credentials, checks the login, writes the CLI's settings, clones the branch the first time, and runs the CLI in its own process group with the prompt on standard input, which is then closed (12.4). The CLI writes into the spool itself. A stop is asked for by the backend with the file `~/.crew/stop`, which holds the reason; crew-run then sends the interrupt, and after 20 seconds kills the group. After every turn crew-run keeps the workspace on `refs/crew/wip/<run>` when it holds anything, also after a turn that ended well, and writes `exit.json` last. `run.json` names the repository by its path on the sandbox's door, the broker's address, which is known only when the sandbox's network exists. The Crew MCP comes with M6: until then `run.json` names none, and crew-run checks none.
- **The harness** is the interface of 12.5 with three more calls: `Interrupt`, a stop that keeps the work in progress; `Resume`, which follows a run again after a restart from the last stored line; and `Alive`. `Kata` reads each turn's spool through the run's home every quarter of a second, ends the turn when `exit.json` is there and the spool is read to its end, and then takes the CLI's files of the conversation by their fixed names; a line over 16 MB is one error event. It writes into a run's home only inside it: a folder or a file the sandbox made a link is not followed out (`os.Root`). `FakeHarness` plays recorded output through the same adapters, with the offsets a spool would give, and holds a turn until it is interrupted when a test asks.
- **The run driver** (`internal/runs`) records a run with its subtask, its conversation, and its saved prompt, and writes every event with the place of its line in the spool (`run_spools`) in one transaction, so every line is one row exactly once, also across a restart. The end of a turn, its use, the agent's turn, and the CLI's own files (`cli_files`, kept seven days, sealed with the key file since 0.78) are one transaction too. Use is the difference to the CLI's last totals of the conversation, kept under the CLI's id of it. A run is `starting`, `working`, `idle`, or `ended`: a turn that ended well leaves the run `idle` in its microVM for ten minutes, for another turn in it (the first way of 12.10), then it ends with `ok`. Events that cannot be stored for two minutes stop the run with `error`; they are stored when the database takes them again, before the microVM goes.
- **A subtask with no ticket** has the scope `project` and the fixture project's fixed id, until M4 makes projects rows, and its branch is `subtask/<n>` (6.1).
- **What follows a stop** (12.4): a reason that counts is retried once at once in a new microVM, which continues the conversation; a second stop leaves the subtask `stopped` for the operator until the lead looks at it (M7). `login` checks the CLI's login at once, and the subtask continues when the CLI is signed in at a check after the stop. `quota` waits for its reset where one is known, else for the operator. `operator` waits; `restart` continues at once. Crew's own reason always wins over what the turn says about itself. Since 0.63 (S23-D2) none of these continues while the subtask's project is paused or its ticket on hold: the monitor's three ways to continue by itself, `wakeWaiting`, `moveWaiting` and `resumeHeld`, read one condition, and before 0.63 they read neither, so a subtask stopped before a pause was retried, with an `agy` turn, in a paused project.
- **The watchdog** (8.2) warns after five minutes without an event and stops after fifteen, warns at 80% of the tool steps and stops at the limit, and warns when the same tool with the same input failed three times in a row and stops at six, with `error`. A CLI that says Crew's MCP server failed is stopped with `error`. A turn that does not end 90 seconds after it was asked to stop loses its microVM.
- **Continuing** (12.10) takes the first way that is possible: the same microVM while the run is `idle`; else a new one with the CLI's files put back, from the run's work in progress; else, when the files are no longer kept, do not open with the key file (since 0.78), or the next turn runs on the other CLI, a new conversation rebuilt from the last six turns in full, with the older ones counted, until summaries exist (M6). A run that continues with no text from the operator gets Crew's message, which says why the last turn stopped.
- **After a restart** the driver follows every run whose microVM still runs from its last stored line, and ends one whose microVM is gone with `restart` (21).
- **The git endpoint's rule** (6.1): a request at a run's own door, from its address, with its token, reads `main`, its branch, its own work in progress, and the one it continues from, and pushes its branch and its own `refs/crew/wip/<run>`. The repositories are in `<data>/repos/`, which the host setup makes and the server mounts. The fixture's repository gets two small files on its first start, so any installation has one; `crew admin repo seed NAME URL` brings in another, from GitHub with the App's token that can only read, revoked after.
- **The cost of a run** is kept to six decimals (`numeric(14,6)`): a short call costs a fraction of a cent.
- **Admin commands:** `crew admin run start|ls|show|events|stop|finish|continue|wait` ask the server in its own container, through its API, with the secret the ingress and the server share (16.11). `crew admin proof event` is the part of install's proof that M3 adds: a microVM prints an event with `crew-run proof`, and it is stored and read back. `crew-ctl proof` runs it.
- **The Runs screen and the Run screen** (20.1, `agents.png`, `run.png`). Until projects exist (M4), Runs is a screen of Crew for the fixture project: what runs now with its step, its time, and its cost; what waits and why; the form that starts a run, with the models each CLI listed itself; and what finished. It becomes the project's Agents screen. The Run screen shows the subtask, the sandbox, what the run holds and may touch, how the CLI was started as its adapter starts it, the conversation and the way it was continued, where each part is kept, the saved prompt, the turns, the other runs of the subtask, and the trajectory, live, with the lines as the CLI printed them on request. Stop, Finish, and Continue are on it; Continue takes the first way that is possible, or the one chosen, and another CLI, which rebuilds the conversation.
- **From the reviews of M3** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-15-*`). What a sandbox writes into its home is its own word, so: a turn's reason is taken from `exit.json` only when it is one of crew-run's (`ok`, `timeout`, `error`, `refused`, `login`, `quota`), and anything else counts as an error; Crew continues a subtask by itself eight times at most (`subtasks.auto_continues`), then it waits for the operator; the server writes into a home only through a file it makes new under a random name, never opened through a link or a FIFO; a conversation's id at the CLI is kept only when it is letters, digits, dashes, and underscores and begins with a letter or a digit, so it never reads as a flag; and the detail of a stop is redacted, and Crew's message to the next run holds Crew's own words only. When the server starts, every run sandbox no live run follows is removed, and a run that cannot be followed again loses its microVM; its spec comes from the database. One caller ends a run, and its stop is counted once. A new run starts from the newest work in progress of its subtask, also after a run that ended before its workspace existed. A continue that fails leaves the idle run as it was, and a turn that does not start is taken back. A microVM that died while its run waited is noticed, and the run continues in a new one. The time limit and the silence are a turn's, with the warning at 80% (8.2; `runs.turn_started_at`, schema step 4). The git endpoint passes git only the headers smart HTTP needs and sets protocol version 0 last, so no spelling of the header asks for version 2; a push is bounded on the wire and as git counts its pack (`receive.maxInputSize`, 512 MiB), and a push larger than git's post buffer, which comes in chunks, is read into a file first, because a CGI program takes no chunked body. Each turn of a rebuilt conversation begins with a mark the turns' text cannot know. The run's token goes to Crew's endpoint only (`http.<url>.extraHeader`). A run gets the harness also in a microVM without Docker (`CREW_HARNESS`): found on dave, a minimal run's `agy` listed none of its skills.
- **From the second round of the reviews of M3** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-15-review-m3-runs-round2.md`): the harness marks the end it makes itself, so a microVM that died during a turn keeps the reason `sandbox`; the end of a run that cannot be written stays pending, with the run and its microVM, and the monitor writes it; an automatic continue counts only when a run started, and the operator's own continue or a turn that ends well counts them from nothing again; a line runs through the needles' patterns only when it holds a fragment one of them needs; a sandbox made while the server follows its runs again is not removed.
- **The routes of runs** are `GET /api/runs`, `POST /api/runs`, `GET /api/runs/options`, `GET /api/runs/{id}`, `GET /api/runs/{id}/events?after=N&raw=true`, `POST /api/runs/{id}/stop`, `POST /api/runs/{id}/finish`, and `POST /api/subtasks/{id}/continue` (15.4).

### From 0.34 to 0.35

M2, Setup and credentials.

- **The credentials are sealed with AES-256-GCM** from Go's standard library, with the key file, and the id of each credential is sealed in with it, so a value moved to another row does not open (15.3). Two tables are added: `sandbox_tokens`, for the token of a sandbox that has no run, and `cli_logins`, for the state of each CLI's login (12.6).
- **Sandboxes of every kind** are started by the server, each on a bridge of its own named `crew-<id>` with an `sdb-` interface, which the server joins at the subnet's last address before the microVM starts (13.3, 17). The server holds the Docker socket through the socket's group, given to its container by `crew-ctl`; no account joins the `docker` group (13.8). Each sandbox's home is a folder of `<data>/sandboxes/`, removed with it, and a sandbox the database does not know, or a Setup, a login check, a judge, or a check sandbox left from before a restart, is removed when the server starts (a check that ran then is an error, and runs again in a new one; found on dave in M9, where one was left running).
- **The broker** answers on the sandbox networks only, at `GET /v1/creds` and `GET /v1/git`, and a request must arrive at a sandbox's own door, from that sandbox's address, with the token in its `~/.crew/token`; the database keeps the token's hash in `sandbox_tokens` (12.4, 12.6, 20.3). The server's door for the ingress answers nothing that arrives at a sandbox's door. `crew-run creds` writes the files into the home and the values into `~/.crew/creds.env`, which a login shell reads; the git helper asks for the temporary GitHub token with the same token. styrman-crew's way, a login shell that asks by network alone, is gone.
- **The temporary GitHub token** is an installation token of the App, asked for with the permission `contents: read` and the names of the sandbox's repositories, which must all belong to the account the App is installed on. A sandbox with no repository gets none, because GitHub reads an empty list as every repository. The token is kept sealed until the sandbox ends, so a restarted server still revokes it. The App's JWT is signed with `github.com/golang-jwt/jwt/v5`, the one new dependency (12.6).
- **Setup** runs one step at a time, `crew-setup git|ssh|gh|gcloud|agy|claude`, in a setup microVM that is started for the step and removed when it ends, collected or not. Crew reads each login from that home by its fixed name, only as a regular file, never through a link, and under 512 KB. `gh` is signed in with `--insecure-storage`, because there is no keyring, so its login is the file `hosts.yml`. The GitHub App is not set up in the terminal but with a form, which takes the App's id and its key file, and is stored only when GitHub says the two belong together and the App may only read contents. `claude setup-token` runs through `crew-capture-token`, the spike's wrapper, which writes the token to a file the terminal never shows (section 24, item 76). The Google account of an `agy` login is read with `tokeninfo` (12.6). `crew-run creds` writes `agy`'s `useG1Credits` as off whenever it writes `agy`'s login (12.2).
- **The adapters exist** in `internal/cli/`, one file each, `agy.go` and `claude.go`. Each says where its login goes in a sandbox, a file or a variable, how long it lasts, how often the scheduled check makes the real request, what it writes in a home before the CLI first runs, how its login is checked, and whose a login is. Nothing else in Crew branches on a CLI's name: the broker, Setup, the login check, and `crew-run` ask the adapter. The stored login of a CLI is the credential of Setup with the CLI's name (12.2, AGENTS.md).
- **The login check** is `crew-run login-check CLI [--real]` in a login-check sandbox of its own, which the broker gives that CLI's login and nothing else. `agy`: `agy models`, and once a day `agy -p /credits` as the real request, which also shows the paid credits that are left; `claude`: always the one call on `haiku` with no tools, whose `rate_limit_event` is kept with the result. A refused check is tried again at once, and two refusals in a row mark the CLI signed out; a check that could not tell changes no state. A CLI with no login in Setup is signed out without a sandbox. After a check, a login file the CLI renewed is kept only when Google's `tokeninfo` names the account of Setup; another account is refused. Only the renewed access token and its expiry are taken, into the stored login: the stored refresh token stays, because a sandbox may write anything in its home, also another account's refresh token beside the operator's access token. A login is checked as soon as Setup collects it. The models `agy models` lists are kept in `models` (12.3); `claude`'s list is read with M8.
- **Setup and Agent CLIs are the first two screens** (20.1). Until a CLI is signed in, the first screen is Setup. The terminal of Setup is xterm.js, which makes two style elements of its own, so the shell's policy lets in styles with the nonce of its scripts (`style-src 'self' 'nonce-…'`), and the page puts that nonce on the style elements its own code makes; `'unsafe-inline'` stays out (20.3). The mail account of Setup comes with notifications (M18), and the limits on Agent CLIs with the quota guard (M8).
- **The user `agent` has the uid of the system user `crew`** in a sandbox the server starts: Kata shares a sandbox's home with the same owner on both sides, and the server, which runs as `crew`, makes the home and reads the logins back from it. The image makes `agent` at uid 1000, and its start script moves it to the uid it is given. Found on dave: the first setup microVM ended at once (13.3, 16.11).
- **The broker learns a sandbox again** when a token does not match the address it comes from: Docker gives a removed sandbox's subnet to the next one, and a login check started right after another was refused. It reads the live sandboxes from Docker, at once when one started since it last did (12.4).
- **`gh` is signed in with `write:packages`.** A `gh` login with the default scopes gets a token for GitHub's registry, but the registry refuses an upload with it ("does not match expected scopes"). With the scope it accepts one. The login stays with the server, so a server that is broken into can also publish packages; that is accepted. `crew admin probe ghcr OWNER/NAME` starts an upload and leaves it unfinished, so nothing is published (16.12; section 24, item 32).
- **Admin commands for a machine:** `crew admin sandbox start|ls|rm|creds` and `crew admin login check|set|offer`, run in the server's container, show what each kind of sandbox gets and drive the login check by hand (16.11).
