- **From the reviews of M2** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-14-*`): a terminal of Setup removes only the setup microVM it opened, and only while it is the latest page attached to it, so a terminal that ends late no longer removes the next step's microVM (the blank terminal found on dave); a reloaded page takes its step over; a page that goes away while the microVM starts gives the start up; a removal that fails is kept and tried again. A sandbox's token row goes first when it is removed, then the container, then its GitHub token, so nothing new is made for it in between; a GitHub token made before a restart is revoked before a new one; and a removed sandbox gets none. The broker refuses a request that is not at a sandbox's door before any work in the database. The server refuses a runtime that is not a microVM before any sandbox starts. A file from a sandbox's home is opened once, never through a link and never waiting on a FIFO. In the real check of `agy`, only a sign-in error of `/credits` is a refusal; a timeout changes no state. Check now starts no second check of a CLI. A link in the terminal shows its address under the pointer before it opens (12.4, 12.6, 13.5, 20.1).
- **The routes of Setup and Agent CLIs** are `GET /setup`, `WS /setup/terminal`, `POST /setup/close`, `POST /setup/github-app`, `GET /clis`, and `POST /clis/{cli}/check` (15.4).
- **Items 32, 42, and 76 are answered** on dave (`spike/answers.md`): `gh` with `write:packages` may push to GitHub's registry; the App's one-hour token reads the project's repositories over HTTPS and with `gh`, cannot push, and is revoked when its sandbox is removed; `crew-capture-token` keeps the token of `claude setup-token` off the screen. A removal of a sandbox runs to its end even when the request that asked for it goes away (12.4).
- **`agy` stays on your personal Google account,** your choice at the first sign-in on dave, in place of the account used for `agy` only of 0.33 (12.6).
- **The terminal of Setup** opens a link where it is printed, also one that runs over several rows, whether the terminal wrapped it or the CLI broke it itself, as `agy` and `claude` do; only https links open, in a tab of their own. Ctrl+V is the browser's paste, and a field under the terminal sends a line, for the code a sign-in asks for. A step that ends keeps its terminal on the page until it is closed, and a step that fails says its exit code. Found at the first sign-in on dave: a link could not be clicked, and gcloud's code could not be pasted (20.1).

### From 0.33 to 0.34

M1, the first build of Crew's machine, server, and database.

- **Go 1.27 for the server.** styrman-crew's server ran Go 1.25, which left Go's support when 1.27 came out in August 2026; the server is internet-facing, and the current PostgreSQL driver needs a newer Go. The ingress keeps the Go of its pinned Caddy builder image (16.12).
- **The health check is the one open path** of Crew's address, so install can prove the server answers behind the ingress; it carries no identity and says only the version and whether the database answers (19.4).
- **`crew admin proof`** writes a row and reads it back, for install's proof (16.11).
- **Kata 4** works with Cloud Hypervisor under Docker when its configuration is given as `ConfigPath`, not `KATA_CONF_FILE` (13.2; section 24, item 13). Items 29 and 59 are answered yes (`spike/answers.md`).
- **The host setup on Ubuntu** 26.04 works as on Debian 13, and the whole install cycle passes on both (section 24, items 34 and 64). `install --build` on an empty Docker takes about 7 minutes (13.4).
- **A sandbox has four capabilities,** `SYS_PTRACE` added: its own Docker could build an image but not run one whose user is not root (13.5). The guest's Docker also gets a tmpfs on `/run/docker` and `/run/containerd`.
- **The fixture project** exists (`testdata/fixture-project/`), and `tools/e2e/sandbox.sh` works on it in a sandbox of the runtime image; with `AGENTS=1` each CLI fixes a failing test there in one turn. Two quirks for 12.2: `--print-timeout` takes a duration, and `claude`'s stream through an attached exec is lost after `init`.
- **The redirect address of the sign-in client** is named by `crew-ctl`'s question, doctor, and install: on dave the client listed only styrman-crew's address, and only the first sign-in showed it. Google cannot be asked whether an address is listed (16.11).

### From 0.32 to 0.33

The spike (M0) answered most of its questions on dave. These changes follow from its answers, which are in `spike/answers.md`, and from your approvals of 2026-10-13.

- **dave's real size** is 12 cores with 16 threads and 62 GiB, not 24 cores (3, 23, 25; R65). About 10 sandboxes fit, and the sizes stay: parked question 101 is answered with my recommendation (13.10).
- **What the subscriptions carry:** ten `agy` runs used up the Gemini five-hour window in 33 minutes, so `agy` gets 1 lead slot, the way out of section 24 for items 1 and 35 (7.9, 14.1). `claude` carried five at once with room to spare. The limit of `agy` is made exact (12.2).
- **Copies of one `agy` login:** they keep one refresh token, but ten renewing at once made one fail. Sandboxes get a fresh access token, and a run that fails its login before any work is tried once more (12.6).
- **The models on your accounts.** `agy` offers no `gemini-3.1-pro`, so the builder runs on Sonnet through `claude` until Pro is offered, and a check of its work cannot run on another family (12.3, parked question 100). `claude`'s list has `fable` and `default`, and `haiku` has no effort levels.
- **The limits of `claude` can be read:** every turn prints a `rate_limit_event`, and the quota guard reads it (4, 12.2, 12.7).
- **The login checks:** `agy models` checks an `agy` login before a run; `claude auth status` only says a token is set; the scheduled check of `claude` is one call on `haiku` with no tools; the account of a renewed `agy` login is read from Google, not from the file (12.6).
- **An `agy` login opens Google Cloud:** its token has the scope `cloud-platform`. You decided that `agy` is signed in with a Google account used for `agy` only (12.6).
- **When the Crew MCP fails,** both CLIs carry on without it and an agent improvises. `crew-run` checks it first, and the driver stops a run whose CLI reports it failed (12.2, 12.4).
- **A turn's result is the last one:** `claude` can print a second `result` when a background subagent finishes (12.2, 12.4).
- **The rows of 12.2 for 1.3.4 and 2.1.287:** the prompt flag together with stream-json input is refused, `--print-timeout` defaults to 0, a missing login still waits 60 seconds, `useG1Credits` cannot be seen in the file, one file continues an `agy` conversation, MCP tools of `agy` go through `call_mcp_tool`, `--setting-sources user` keeps out all of the project's own files, and the new events of `claude`.
- **Conversations:** `agy` reports usage for the whole conversation, and continues on another model or family (12.10).
- **The machine:** KSM shares nothing with Cloud Hypervisor, and without an address pool Docker stops at 29 networks (13.2, 13.10). The hosts each CLI needs are measured (13.6). What each CLI loads from a workspace is confirmed (13.8).
- **Taking dave over:** styrman-crew was uninstalled at level 3 before M0, so steps 3 and 5 of 17.6 are done and its project data is gone. Items 16 and 29 of the spike moved to M1 (section 24).

### From 0.31 to 0.32

- **Docker is the only target.** Every part of Crew is a container. On the machine itself are only the parts that 13.1 lists: Docker with Kata, the firewall, `crew-ctl` with its files, the folders, and three small systemd files. Every other installation is the same images (13.1, 16.12).
- **The lead queue goes by priority.** A lead run waits for a slot of its own CLI, and within a class the ticket with the higher priority goes first (7.9).
- **Crew chooses the CLI and the model.** For the builder, the reviewer, the tester, the E2E tester, and the verifier the choice is automatic, from Crew's own numbers. Crew tries other entries with one run in ten, tells you what it changed, watches it, and goes back if it was worse. An agent you set yourself is left alone and gets a recommendation. 0.31 only recommended (12.3).
- **Improve text and Review text.** In planning the first message still gets a proposal by itself. Everywhere you write, you can select text and choose Improve text or Review text. A ticket you add gets no proposal by itself any more (7.2).
- **A chat on every ticket.** For a running ticket and for a done one, an assistant answers what happened and why. You can mark text anywhere in the task view and comment on it, and the comment goes to the chat. Tell the agent, Make a ticket of this, and Remember this take a message from the chat to the work (11.9).
- **Answered:** questions 94 to 99. Question 89 is changed.
- **What decides a change of a model** is defined for each agent, and tries, going back, and comparisons are spelled out (12.3).
- **A comment on a ticket with no run waits** for the next run on that ticket (11.6).
- **The spike changes nothing on dave.** Three questions moved to M1 (section 24).
- **Audited before the build.** Two independent reviews read 0.32: one the design and the requirements, one the build kit as the builder would use it. What they found is corrected, and section 28 lists it.

### From 0.30 to 0.31

- **Crew stays private.** You decided not to publish it: it is for you, on your own machines and on the clients you choose. The licence file, the check for your names, the plan for a public repository, and `install.sh` are dropped. Reproducible builds, no secret in the repository, and install on another machine stay (16.12).
- **Other installations follow the main one.** Dave builds Crew. Another installation gets finished releases and builds none (16.12).
- **Lead slots are counted for each CLI:** 2 each, and each can be changed (7.9).
- **Crew recommends, and you choose.** Crew measures each agent on each CLI, model, and effort, and recommends another one when the numbers are clear. Nothing changes until you press Use it. You can start a comparison. The improver no longer changes a model (12.3).
- **The models of `claude` are read from the CLI,** with the request its own SDK uses, and no longer from a short list in the code (12.3).
- **The longest sign-ins.** The one-year token for `claude`, and for `agy` the token file, used and stored every day (12.6).
- **Crew proposes a better prompt.** Before your first message in a plan draft, or a ticket you add, reaches an agent, a prompt writer proposes a better wording, with what it changed, its assumptions, and its questions. You choose which text is sent. Every rewrite is saved and measured (7.2).
- **Other CLIs later.** The adapters are the preparation. Nothing else is built for `codex` or `opencode` now (12.2).
- **The spike has three more questions,** 78 to 80 (section 24).
- **Answered:** questions 89 to 93. Question 87 is dropped, and 88 is changed.
- **New parked questions:** 94 to 99.
- **Read by an independent review.** One review read 0.31 for what still said the old things and for what the new parts left unsaid. What it found is corrected (section 28).


### From 0.29 to 0.30

- **`claude` is back, and there is room for more CLIs.** Crew runs `agy` and `claude`. Each CLI is behind one adapter, and another CLI, such as `codex` or `opencode`, is one more adapter (12.2). You decided this in 0.30, after 0.26 had made Crew `agy` only.
- **A model belongs to a CLI.** Each agent has a short list of entries, each a CLI with a model. Claude models run through `claude` and Gemini models through `agy` by default, and every agent has an entry on each (12.3).
- **One CLI can stop.** A CLI that is signed out or at a limit hands its runs to the agent's next choice on the other CLI, where the rule for checks allows it (12.6, 12.7).
- **`claude` signs in with a one-year token,** kept as a value. A sandbox holds the login of the one CLI it runs (12.6).
- **The harness has one source again,** in Claude Code's format, built for each CLI (12.1).
- **Files of `claude` in a repository** are instruction files, and its settings, `.mcp.json`, and hooks are code that waits for you (13.8).
- **The spike has ten more questions,** 65 to 74, about `claude` (section 24).
- **Answered:** the board has one level (2), a ticket merges on its own (3), the licence is Apache 2.0 (87), and the repository is `kesokaj/crew` and starts private (88). Crew is built directly on dave.
- **New parked questions:** 89 to 93.
- **Read again by an independent review.** One review read 0.30 for what still said "`agy` only". What it found is corrected (section 28).


### From 0.28 to 0.29

- **Install, update, and uninstall are features.** `crew-ctl` checks the machine, gathers what it needs from a `.env` file or by asking, installs Docker and Kata, and brings up Crew. It updates and rolls back through the same updater as the app, and uninstalls at three levels. All of it is tested on a fresh machine before every release (16.11).
- **Crew will be public.** Every version is fixed in one file, the build takes nothing private, and the repository holds nothing of yours. A list says what happens before it is made public (16.12).
- **A release that changes code waits for Install.** Only a release of prompts and harness text installs by itself. You chose my recommendation (16.3).
- Parked question 85 is answered. Questions 87 and 88 are new.
- **Reviewed before the build.** Two independent reviews read this version: one for statements that contradict each other, one as the builder of the first milestones. What they found is corrected in this version, and the larger points are in section 28. The choices I made from them are in section 4.

### From 0.27 to 0.28

- **Every model can be browsed.** A Models screen shows every model `agy` offers, by family, with the agents that use it and its results, and gives a model to an agent. Each role is an agent with its own model and effort (12.3, 20.1).
- **At a limit a run moves, if it can.** It takes its agent's second choice in the other group of models. A check never moves to the builder's family, and waits. `agents.at_limit: wait` brings back the rule of 0.27 (12.7).
- **dave is taken over from styrman-crew.** It is the same machine. styrman-crew is removed with its own uninstall after the spike, and only Crew runs there (17.6).
- **Crew acts as you.** A sandbox keeps your own gcloud login. This is now the ninth rule of section 2.
- **The sign-in is styrman-crew's.** The token file of your Google sign-in, with no keyring and no other way prepared (12.6).
- Parked questions 81, 83, 84, and 86 are answered, and 82 in part. Question 85 is still open. Spike items 57 and 58 are new.

### From 0.26 to 0.27

- **Crew reads the models from `agy`.** `agy models` is read after every sign-in, once a day, after a new version of `agy`, and on Refresh. The list is in the database and on the Agent CLI screen. You asked for this (12.3).
- **The effort is a setting,** beside the model: a default per role, a project's own, and a pin on a ticket. `agy` has Opus and Sonnet models as well as Gemini, and the defaults now use them (12.3).
- **A check uses another model than the build.** A reviewer, a verifier, and a `judge:` check run on another family than the builder. You decided this (12.3).
- **Limits are counted in two groups,** as the vendor counts them: Gemini models, and Claude and GPT models. A limit on one group holds only the roles on it (12.7).
- Parked question 80 is answered. Questions 81 and 82 are new. Spike items 47 to 51 are new.
- **Version 0.27 was audited before the build** by six independent reviews: contradictions, readiness to build, security, what is copied from styrman-crew and styrman-harness, the requirements against the design, and the facts about outside products. What they found, and what remains, is in section 28.
- **Rules that were missing are written:** how each kind of run ends, why a run stops, what a check's result can be, how a merge is made, the core tables, the first install, and the order of the build plan (11.3, 12.4, 13.9, 7.12, 15.3, 16.11, section 23).
- **Safer by default:** the GitHub token of a sandbox names its project's repositories, Crew's own run of the checks decides a stage, files that are code for the CLI or for GitHub wait for you, a login from a run is tested before it is kept, and requests to the app must come from its own pages (12.6, 13.9, 13.8, 20.3).
- **Facts corrected** against the vendor's documentation and the two repositories: how `agy` is called, where its sign-in is kept, that it has no numbered download, that its limits can be read with `/usage`, that paid credits must be off, what styrman-crew's firewall and ingress really do, and Kata's advisories (12.2, 12.6, 12.7, 12.8, 13.2, 17.2).
- Questions 83 to 86 are new. Spike items 52 to 56 are new, and items 7, 15, 28, 39, and 41 are answered in part by the vendor's documentation.

### From 0.25 to 0.26

- **`agy` only.** Crew runs the Antigravity CLI and nothing else. Everything about two CLIs is removed: choosing a CLI, taking over, checking with the other CLI, lead slots per CLI, and the `claude` login. You decided this (section 12).
- **The model for a role is a setting,** with a default from the baseline and a pin per project or per ticket. Results are still measured per model (12.3).
- **Crew now depends on one CLI and one account.** When `agy` is signed out, at its limit, or its account is stopped, every project waits (12.6, 12.7).
- **Trajectories are kept for good,** until feedback has been run on them and eval cases have been made. Each one shows whether that was done (8.3).
- **Backups stay on dave.** The events of each month that has ended are backed up once, so a day's file stays small (16.9).
- **A finished project runs again when a ticket is added to it,** by you or by the lead in housekeeping, and gets a new final report (7.2).
- Parked questions 78 and 79 are answered. Question 80 is new. Spike item 36 is dropped.

### From 0.24 to 0.25

- **The database is the brain.** Everything Crew saves is in a PostgreSQL database that runs in its own container, `crew-db`. Git holds the code, the documentation, and a project's skills. You decided this (section 6, 15.3).
- **The `board` branch and the instance repository are removed.** Tickets, records, knowledge, settings, plan drafts, and improvements are rows. Every change is kept in `changes`. A project can be written out as files and read back (6.2, 6.4).
- **Trajectories and the CLI's own conversation files are in the database.** A run's home is removed with its sandbox, and a conversation is continued from what the database holds (8.3, 12.10).
- **Credentials are in the database, encrypted** with a key that is a file on the machine (15.3).
- **Updates and backups work on the database:** a dump as the snapshot, schema changes tried on a scratch copy first, and one dump in each day's backup file (16.4, 16.6, 16.9).
- **Each sandbox gets a temporary GitHub token that can only read.** The SSH key and the `gh` login stay with the server. You chose my recommendation (12.6).
- Parked question 77 is answered. Questions 78 and 79 are new.

### From 0.23 to 0.24

- **The proposal for sandboxes is now a temporary token.** You asked whether Crew creates a key and removes it. Crew would ask GitHub for a read-only token each time a sandbox starts, and it stops working after one hour. It needs a private GitHub App, created once. Still parked question 77 (12.6).

### From 0.22 to 0.23

- **`gh` is signed in.** You decided this. Crew creates repositories, pull requests, and release pages (6.6).
- **A token, compared.** You asked whether `GH_TOKEN` is better. For Crew itself it is not. For sandboxes a fine-grained, read-only token would be, and it is proposed as parked question 77 (12.6).

### From 0.21 to 0.22

- **No GitHub token.** Git goes over SSH with one key from Setup. `gh` is an optional sign-in for what a key cannot do: creating a repository, pull requests, and release pages (6.6).
- **Credentials are copied into every sandbox** when it starts, from the one store in Crew. Nothing is chosen per project. The second, weaker GitHub token is dropped, and Crew notices a commit on `main` that it did not make (12.6).
- **Lead slots:** 2 for each agent CLI for now (7.9).
- **The Linux is Debian or Ubuntu.** The Docker part of the host setup is written out, step by step, as styrman-crew does it (13.2).
- **Decided:** an override is applied when the project's evals are not worse (9.5), a failing setup command gets a fix ticket, and the tool set of the runtime image stays (13.4).
- Parked questions 9, 69, 70, 73, 74, and 75 are answered. Question 76 is new.

### From 0.20 to 0.21

- **A ticket only moves to the right.** It never goes back a stage. A stage that fails is fixed where the ticket stands, with a fix subtask. A ticket that cannot get through is cancelled and replaced by other tickets, which are tagged to it. Skipped tickets and new features stay in Analysis. Nothing is parked any more (7.4, 7.7).
- **Crew chooses the CLI.** No project is locked to `claude` or `agy`. Crew picks the CLI and the model for each run from what worked best, and you can pin one. Lead slots are counted per CLI. A third CLI such as `codex` can be added (7.9, 12.3).
- **Setup is outside the projects, and sandboxes get the credentials.** The git identity, an SSH key, `gh`, gcloud, `claude`, and `agy` are set up once. The credential store gives them to each sandbox when it starts, as in styrman-crew. What that costs is written down, with four limits (12.6).
- **Skills and context per project.** styrman-harness 2.7.0 is the base. A project keeps its own layer in its repository, sideloaded into every run. An agent that misses a skill asks with `request_skill`, and a ticket adds it (12.9).
- **Conversations.** The operator can talk to an agent with no ticket. The agent works in an ephemeral sandbox, about one project or about none, and what should land becomes a ticket. Every turn is saved in Crew's database (11.8).
- **Conversation awareness.** Every run belongs to a conversation that is stored three times and continued in one of three ways, so a builder that comes back for a fix knows what it did, on `claude` and on `agy` (12.10).
- **Trajectory tracking** is spelled out: what one event holds, the index, where a trajectory is found, and that no run goes on without one (8.3).
- **The machine is dave:** 12 cores with 16 threads, 62 GiB, a 1 TB SSD, Linux, bare metal. How many sandboxes fit at once is set from what the spike measured (13.10).
- **Mail first.** The phone is skipped for now. What needs you is one mail each, and news is one mail a day (section 18).
- **Backups are local** tar.gz files to start with, and nothing checks Crew from outside. Both are your decisions (16.9, 16.10).
- **The owner** of new repositories is the person who installs Crew. Go and TypeScript are confirmed.
- New parked questions 73 to 75. Questions 69 and 70 are explained better.

### From 0.19 to 0.20

- **An audit of everything.** Four independent reviews read the design, the requirements, the simulation, and the walkthrough. About 340 stale or contradicting statements are corrected. Section 28 lists the 25 gaps they found, and what is still open.
- **One name for each thing.** Tools are named after tickets (`get_ticket`, `list_tickets`). The screen is Agent CLIs. What reaches you is "what needs you", in one list (7.8).
- **Crew has two repositories of its own**: the source, which agents work on, and an instance repository on the machine that no sandbox can clone (10.3).
- **Gaps closed with a default of mine**, all listed in section 4: who can change a check (7.1), shares of a subscription and Pause all (7.9), flaky checks (7.10), after Done (7.13), the meaning of "not worse" (10.5), rules for personal tokens (11.7), logins removed after a run (12.6), instruction files (13.8), looking after the machine (16.10), a data copy before a deploy (19.3), no shared cookie (19.4), and sessions (20.3).
- **Settings corrected.** Removed: `sandbox.logins`, `approvals.merge`, `on_task_budget`, `on_stuck`, `rollback_if_worse`, `snapshot_before_update`, and `baseline: follow`. Added: the hosting keys, `take_over`, `cpu_overcommit`, `timezone`, `disk`, `sessions`, `mcp_tokens`, and the eval keys.
- **The build plan is reordered** (section 23), and the spike has six more questions (section 24).
- New parked questions 66 to 72.
- **The requirements are rewritten by topic.** Every requirement carries its number and the design section that covers it. R163 to R178 are new. Seven numbers are withdrawn and listed there.

### From 0.18 to 0.19

- **Agents do not wait for the operator.** An agent that meets a choice the plan does not settle decides, records it with `decide` in Crew's database, and carries on. The operator can change any decision in the project's Decisions list (7.5, 11.5).
- **A ticket that passes its budget carries on.** The event is recorded on the ticket and as a signal (7.7).
- **A ticket that cannot get through goes to the lead**, not to the operator: one new approach, then parked. The project can still be Done (7.7).
- **What waits for the operator** is now only at the level of a project or of Crew: the project OK, a project limit, a sign-in, and a protected release (7.3).

### From 0.17 to 0.18

- **Less waits for you.** A new feature gives notice and goes ahead after 24 hours. An improvement whose evals are not worse ships in the next release without a separate approval, and Crew rolls it back by itself if it measures worse. A question from an agent comes with the agent's own recommendation, and the agent goes on with it after 4 hours (7.3, 10.5, 11.5).
- **What still waits** is listed with its reason: the project OK, limits, sign-ins, and protected releases (7.3).
- **Your own tools may answer**, as a switch per token: questions, new features, and a ticket's limit (11.7).
- **No Google Workspace today.** Google Chat stays off.
- A walkthrough of the whole system with diagrams and every screen is published beside the simulation.

### From 0.16 to 0.17

- **Autonomy** is now a stated decision: the system should be autonomous from the operator as much as possible.
- **Parallel subtasks.** The lead can split the build of a ticket into subtasks. Those that do not depend on each other run at the same time, each on its own branch `sub/<ticket>.<n>`, and the backend joins them (7.5, 7.11, 6.1).
- **The MCP for your own tools.** A personal token lets tools outside Crew read, create tickets, and comment. Decisions stay in the app (11.7).
- **Notifications in the web, iPhone first.** No mobile app. Crew's web page on the Home Screen notifies. Pressing a notification opens the item, and pressing an alternative decides (section 18).
- **Mail alternatives** are compared (18.4). Your Google account with an app password stays the choice.
- **Hosted projects** are protected as in styrman-crew: Google sign-in against a list of mail addresses, with only the admin email as the default (19.4).

### From 0.15 to 0.16

- **Crew MCP.** Agents create more tickets with `create_ticket` and more subtasks on their own ticket with `create_subtask`. A created subtask gets its own run. The helper tool is now `add_helper`. A Crew MCP screen shows the tools and every call (7.5, 7.11, 11.5).
- **Notifications without Google Workspace.** Google Chat is off. Crew notifies from its own app and by mail, and an answer is two presses (section 18).
- **Updates install by themselves.** Crew gives notice of a release, and it installs after 24 hours. Install now and Hold are the alternatives. A release that changes a protected part still waits (16.3).
- **Hosting on the internet.** A hosted project is reached from the internet through the ingress's sign-in: only you, listed accounts, any Google account, or public. It may reach the internet from inside (19.3, 19.4).
- **Releases are private to start with** (16.9). A preview per ticket is left for later.

### From 0.14 to 0.15

- **Notifications.** Google Chat and mail, with the alternatives of a decision in the message as links that are confirmed in Crew (section 18).
- **Hosting.** A built project can be hosted from the machine with one button, as a microVM of its own, at an address from the ingress copied from styrman-crew (section 19).
- **Top bar.** The version sits by the logo. "Sandboxes" instead of "MicroVMs". The CLI limits are off the top bar and the front page.
- Sections 18 to 25 of version 0.14 are now 20 to 27.

### From 0.13 to 0.14

- **Releases without keys.** No signing keys. Every approved release is a tag and a release on GitHub with notes, with its images in the repository's registry (16.9).
- **Copies off the machine.** Snapshots of Crew's stores go to a backup place. A dead disk loses nothing but the logins (16.9).
- **New image, by itself.** After an update every new microVM uses the new runtime image. Nothing is set per project (16.7).
- **Edit mode.** The project terminal stays read-only for diagnostics and gets an edit mode that can push. A push to a ticket's branch waits until no agent is running there (13.11).

### From 0.12 to 0.13

- **A new updater.** styrman-crew's updater is not copied. A release is built once on the machine, checked beside the running version, then switched, watched, and switched back by itself. No signing keys, no building while updating, and no root script on a timer (section 16).
- **Sign-in.** The ingress and its Google sign-in pattern are copied from styrman-crew.
- **Terminals are for diagnostics.** A project terminal with the repository mounted read-only, and a direct jump into a running task. Nothing can be pushed from a terminal. The task terminal with its on-hold rule is gone (13.11).
- **The machine.** Bare metal with Docker, dedicated to Crew. Limits are read from the machine at Setup.

### From 0.11 to 0.12

- **Not a full copy.** Crew has its own server and UI. It copies the sandbox, the runtime image, and the credential store from styrman-crew, plus the terminal, the updater, and the ingress (17.2). The file explorer, the Git view, the Browser view, and the microVM per project are not copied.
- **A terminal on a ticket.** A button opens a terminal inside the running run's microVM, or in a new task terminal on the ticket's branch (13.11).
- **Git in the app.** A Git screen per project, git on every ticket and subtask, local or GitHub with a change later, and cloning a local project from Crew (6.7).
- **Logins.** You sign in to `claude` about once a week today and to `agy` almost never. The one-year `claude` token is confirmed (12.6).
- **Repository and machine.** `kesokaj/crew` is created when the build starts, and Crew runs on a machine of its own.

### From 0.10 to 0.11

- **Crew is its own repository.** A new private repository, `kesokaj/crew`, that replaces styrman-crew. It starts as a copy of styrman-crew and styrman-harness, renamed (section 17). The rules of the old repositories no longer apply.
- **One runtime image.** Both CLIs, the harness, and `crew-run` are in one image that is kept current: a daily look for new versions becomes a ticket and then a release (12.8).
- **No `sh-agent`.** The harness is a folder in Crew, built into the image for both CLIs. Memory, search, and metrics are Crew's own (12.1).
- **Logins.** Setup at first start in a setup-only microVM, as in styrman-crew. New: a login check before every run and on a schedule, and signing in again from the inbox (12.6).
- **Fewer sign-ins.** The one-year `claude` token is offered first, and a login renewed during a run is kept. Both are to be confirmed in the spike.
- **Antigravity.** The risk with its terms is accepted. `agy` stays on.
- **Build plan.** A milestone for the new repository and one for setup and logins are added. The last milestone switches styrman-crew off (section 21).

### From 0.9 to 0.10

- **No model API.** Agents are the command-line agents `claude` and `agy`, started headless and logged in with a subscription. The OpenHands SDK and the model proxy are gone (section 12).
- **Built on styrman-crew and styrman-harness.** I read both repositories. Crew is now designed as the next step of styrman-crew's Go server, and styrman-harness is the baseline agents read (section 17).
- **Sandboxes are microVMs.** Docker containers with the Kata runtime and Cloud Hypervisor, one per run, with their own Docker daemon (section 13). Firecracker is explained and left out for now.
- **A network switch with three modes:** Internet, Model only, and LAN. Model only is new (13.6).
- **The CLI and the model can be chosen** per project, per role, and per ticket (12.3).
- **Logins** follow styrman-crew's broker, with four changes for many short runs (12.6). The login of the CLI is now inside the sandbox.
- **A quota guard** handles the subscription limits that all runs share (12.7).
- **Versions, self-update, and roll back** are a section of their own (section 16): one version for everything, Crew builds Crew, update and roll back from the app.
- The backend is Go, the UI is styrman-crew's, and the database is an index only.
- Sections 16 to 23 of version 0.9 are now 18 to 25.

### From 0.8 to 0.9

- **Four jobs named.** Crew shows what agents are doing, gives them structure, is their ticketing system, and holds each project's documentation (section 1).
- **The stages changed** to Planning, Backlog, Analysis, In progress, and Done, with optional stages between In progress and Done: Review, Test, E2E, and Verification (7.4). Ready and Build are gone. The backlog review became the Analysis stage, and every task passes through it (7.6).
- **Planning is a column.** The breakdown waits there for the project OK, and new features wait there later.
- **Subtasks added.** One per stage visit, plus helpers an agent starts, each with its agent, model, steps, and result (7.11).
- **Prompts are saved and shown** for every subtask, with the parts they were built from (8.6).
- **The task view is specified.** It shows the running agent, its model, its prompt, the source plan, and the subtasks (18.2). An activity feed was added.
- **Tickets and the development workflow.** Ticket numbers, kinds, links, a branch and a pull request per ticket, and a history (7.12).
- **Documentation added** as the sixth kind of project knowledge: pages in the repository, indexed and shown in the app, part of every task's exit checks, with decision records and a changelog (9.9).
- New roles for the optional stages: E2E tester and verifier.

### From 0.7 to 0.8

- **The flow was rebuilt around nine steps:** plan, project, plan added, breakdown, one OK, monitoring, why-what-goal, the Crew MCP, and the backlog review (section 2, 7.2).
- **One level of work.** Backlog items that were split into tasks one at a time are gone. The plan is split straight into tasks, and a task names its part of the plan.
- **Plan before project.** A plan draft is developed with a planning agent before any project or repository exists.
- **Crew creates the repository on GitHub** with the operator's token. The token stays with the backend (6.6).
- **The approval moved.** The operator gives one OK after the breakdown, instead of approving a project plan before items were analysed.
- **Why, what, and goal** are required on every task (7.1).
- **Any agent can create a task through the Crew MCP.** It goes to the backlog, where another agent reviews it and approves, skips, discards, or sends it to the operator (7.5, 7.6). This replaces discovered tasks, feature proposals, and the separate approval of fixes.
- **Monitoring, trajectories, and task summaries** are a section of their own (section 8). Summaries are stored in Crew's database.
- **Tasks merge into `main` one at a time**, after their checks pass on top of the current `main` (7.10).
- **A Crew level was added.** One developer has several projects that share nothing. Crew has all the data and records signals from every project. An improver proposes changes to a baseline of agents and pipeline that every project runs on (section 10).
- **Agent definitions and stage prompts moved** from each project to Crew's baseline. A project keeps only its overrides.
- The lead no longer analyses and verifies each item. Its work is the plan, the breakdown, backlog reviews, the final report, and housekeeping.

### From 0.6 to 0.7

- Every project must have a git repository. Heavy git integration spelled out.

### From 0.5 to 0.6

- A fix is approved by another agent. Sandboxes can reach the internet.

### From 0.4 to 0.5

- Approval at the entrance of the backlog. One sandbox per run with a runtime image. A settings section. Single machine.

### From 0.3 to 0.4

- Direct memory writes, feedback between agents, a global limit of 4 lead runs, automatic start.

### From 0.2 to 0.3

- Project knowledge: memory, bugs, feedback, evals, and analytics per project.

### From 0.1 to 0.2

- Named Crew. Goals with checks. Stage moves applied by the backend when checks pass.
